## input
Verified against Chromium source (main-thread event queue, Android MotionEvent conversion, pointer event factory, prediction, Windows pen processor, Ink API), AOSP input source, the Pointer Events spec, MDN/BCD, blink-dev intents and app code. Key facts: (1) Chromium clones every arriving mousemove/touchmove into a pointerrawupdate that is dispatched immediately AND queues the same sample for a rAF-aligned pointermove, so each sample reaches JS on both channels with identical coordinates and timeStamp; pointerrawupdate events themselves coalesce when the main thread is busy, so getCoalescedEvents() must be read on both event types. (2) Chrome-for-Android calls Android's requestUnbufferedDispatch on ACTION_DOWN, so it receives one MotionEvent per hardware sample; Android WebView (synchronous compositor) never does, so Android batches pen samples per vsync and Chromium converts ONLY the newest sample of each batch (historical samples are never turned into web events) - in a WebView on a 120 Hz Samsung tablet, most pen samples are lost before JS ever sees them, which is the real cause of the 'skipped points on fast curves' complaint, not the app's de-dup. The embedding Android app can fix this by calling View.requestUnbufferedDispatch itself. (3) getPredictedEvents() is real on Android and Windows (Kalman, up to 25 ms ahead, pointermove only, position-only). (4) navigator.ink exists on all Chromium platforms but the point-forwarding path is gated on a ui::Compositor that RenderWidgetHostViewAndroid never provides, so delegated ink trails are inert on Android/WebView; they work on Windows (DirectComposition). (5) Windows pen: WM_POINTER read with GetPointerPenInfo only (coalesced history discarded), pen-in-contact is routed as a touch event (touch-action applies), timeStamp is processing time. (6) timeStamp resolution is 100 us (5 us if cross-origin isolated). (7) S-Pen gives pressure, tiltX/tiltY (integer degrees), pointerType 'pen'/'eraser'; twist is always 0 on Android; Android's palm-rejection FLAG_CANCELED is not surfaced. Recommended collection method: a single-channel design - pointerrawupdate + getCoalescedEvents() as the only geometry source when available (secure context, Chromium/Firefox 148+), pointermove only for getPredictedEvents(), pointermove+getCoalescedEvents() as the fallback, no cross-channel timestamp de-dup at all, plus unbuffered dispatch in the WebView host, touch-action:none, and a desynchronized canvas as the pointerdown target.

Findings:
- [high] Spec: pointerrawupdate fires for property changes that do not fire pointerdown/up, must be dispatched 'as soon as possible', may be coalesced, may carry a coalesced list, and its predicted list is 'usually' empty. Coalesced lists are chronologically sorted with timeStamps <= the parent's; predicted lists have timeStamps >= the parent's. - Pointer Events Level 3 text: 'In contrast with pointermove, user agents SHOULD dispatch pointerrawupdate events as soon as possible and as frequently as the JavaScript can handle the events.' ... 'if there is already another pointerrawupdate with the same pointerId that hasn't been dispatched in the event loop, the user agent MAY coalesce the new pointerrawupdate with that event'. 'While pointerrawupdate events may have a non-empty coalesced events list, their predicted events list will, for performance reasons, usually be an empty list.' For getCoalescedEvents: 'the list is a sequence of all PointerEvents that were coalesced into this event ... The coalesced events list MUST be chronologically sorted by timeStamp' and the parent 'may have additional processing (for example to align with the display refresh rate) ... the coalesced events lists for these events always contain at least one event.' Issue #380 (closed by PR #383) is what settled that rawupdate predicted lists are usually empty. Requires a secure context. (https://w3c.github.io/pointerevents/ and https://github.com/w3c/pointerevents/issues/380) => Defines the contract 2.0 can rely on: geometry must be read from getCoalescedEvents() (never just the parent), and prediction is only available from pointermove.
- [high] Chromium implementation: every mousemove/touchmove arriving in the renderer is cloned into a pointerrawupdate dispatched immediately, while the original is queued for rAF-aligned pointermove. The same hardware sample therefore appears on both channels with the same coordinates and the same platform timestamp. - MainThreadEventQueue::HandleEvent (Chromium main): 'if (has_pointerrawupdate_handlers_) { if (type == kMouseMove) { auto raw_event = std::make_unique<WebCoalescedInputEvent>(std::make_unique<WebPointerEvent>(kPointerRawUpdate, mouse_event), latency); QueueEvent(CreateForRawEvent(...)); } else if (type == kTouchMove) { for each touch_point with state kStateMoved { raw_event = WebPointerEvent(touch_event, touch_point); SetType(kPointerRawUpdate); QueueEvent(...) } } }' followed by 'QueueEvent(std::move(queued_event))' for the original. IsRafAlignedEvent() returns true only for kMouseMove/kMouseWheel/kTouchMove (when allow_raf_aligned_input_ and no low-latency request) and false for kPointerRawUpdate, so raw updates go out via PostTask (DispatchEvents) while moves wait for DispatchRafAlignedInput(frame_time) (BeginMainFrame; 5 s fallback timer kMaxRafDelay). The single-event WebCoalescedInputEvent constructor pushes one clone into coalesced_events_, so the raw clone carries only the top-level sample of the arriving event. The blink-dev intent states pointermove is 'rAF-aligned and hence may add half a frame of latency on average'. PointerEventFactory::CreateEventSequence builds each coalesced PointerEvent with 'PointerEvent::Create(type, new_event_init, event.TimeStamp())' - the original per-sample timestamps - and the parent with web_pointer_event.TimeStamp(). (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc (see also .../third_party/blink/common/input/web_coalesced_input_event.cc, .../third_party/blink/renderer/core/events/pointer_event_factory.cc, https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/mUW58VMIrTM/m/gIotA4HwBAAJ)) => Listening to both channels for geometry guarantees duplicates; the duplicates are byte-identical in Chromium, so a correct design uses one channel for geometry and never merges. Latency win of pointerrawupdate over pointermove is ~half a frame (4 ms at 120 Hz) plus avoiding BeginMainFrame scheduling.
- [high] pointerrawupdate events coalesce with each other while the main thread is busy; the dispatched event equals the newest sample and getCoalescedEvents() holds every sample in order. Raw updates are kept in order relative to pointerdown/up, and Chromium does not fire a pointerrawupdate for an ordinary pen-down/up (only for chorded-button moves). - QueuedWebInputEvent::FilterNewEvent: 'event_->CoalesceWith(*other_event->event_)'; WebCoalescedInputEvent::CoalesceWith: 'New events get coalesced into older events, and the newer timestamp should always be preserved ... event_->Coalesce(*newer); event_->SetTimeStamp(time_stamp); AddCoalescedEvent(*newer)'. WebPointerEvent::CanCoalesce requires same type (kPointerMove or kPointerRawUpdate), modifiers, id and pointer_type; WebPointerEvent::Coalesce does '*this = pointer_event' (movement deltas accumulated). DispatchEvents comment: 'Do not pass a non-rAF-aligned event to avoid delivering raw move events and down/up events out of order to js.' PointerEventManager only calls CreatePointerRawUpdateEvent when a kPointerDown/kPointerUp was converted to pointermove ('This is a chorded button move event. We need to also send a pointerrawupdate for it.'). MDN adds: 'An application that cannot keep up will feel less responsive rather than more, so heavy optimization inside the event listener is needed.' (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/common/input/web_pointer_event.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/input/pointer_event_manager.cc ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/pointerrawupdate_event) => The 2.0 raw-update handler must iterate getCoalescedEvents() (not just the event) and must be cheap: append samples to a typed buffer, do no painting there; paint once per frame.
- [high] Android batches ACTION_MOVE samples per vsync into one MotionEvent with 'historical' samples and (for finger, mouse AND stylus) resamples the newest sample to frame time minus 5 ms; batching and resampling are skipped only when the View requests unbuffered dispatch. - AOSP InputEventReceiver.md: 'input events are batched to only deliver 1 event per frame to the app ... The app is notified that a batched event is available for consumption, and schedules a runnable via the Choreographer to consume it a short time before the next frame ... consume(.., consumeBatches=true, frameTime=<valid frame time>) ... will return all batched events up to the frameTime point.' MotionEvent.java: 'motion events with ACTION_MOVE may batch together multiple movement samples within a single object. The most current pointer coordinates are available using getX ... Earlier coordinates within the batch are accessed using getHistoricalX ... To process all coordinates in the batch in time order, first consume the historical coordinates then consume the current coordinates.' PointerCoords.isResampled doc: 'a motion event will always contain at least one sample that is an original event from the input device ... Resampling does not occur if unbuffered dispatch has been requested'. InputConsumer.cpp/Resampler.cpp: RESAMPLE_LATENCY = 5ms, RESAMPLE_MIN_DELTA = 2ms, RESAMPLE_MAX_DELTA = 20ms, RESAMPLE_MAX_PREDICTION = 8ms; canResampleTool() returns true for FINGER, MOUSE, STYLUS, UNKNOWN. View.requestUnbufferedDispatch(MotionEvent): 'Until this View receives a corresponding ACTION_UP, ask that the input system not batch MotionEvents but instead deliver them as soon as they're available ... Side effects include: increased latency, jittery scrolls and inability to take advantage of system resampling.' (https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/master/core/jni/android_view_InputEventReceiver.md ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/MotionEvent.java ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/native/+/refs/heads/main/libs/input/InputConsumer.cpp ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/native/+/refs/heads/main/libs/input/Resampler.cpp ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/View.java) => At 120 Hz a 240-360 Hz S-Pen yields 2-3 samples per MotionEvent under buffered dispatch; whether those samples survive depends entirely on whether the consumer (Chromium) reads the history or the host requests unbuffered dispatch.
- [high] Chrome for Android requests Android unbuffered dispatch on every ACTION_DOWN, so Chrome receives one MotionEvent per hardware pen sample; Android WebView does not (it uses the synchronous compositor), and the experimental buffered-input mode is off by default. - content/browser/web_contents/web_contents_view_android.cc: '// True if we want to disable Android native event batching and use compositor event queue. bool ShouldRequestUnbufferedDispatch() { static bool should_request_unbuffered_dispatch = !GetContentClient()->UsingSynchronousCompositing() && !base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(input::features::kUseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch); ...}' and 'bool WebContentsViewAndroid::OnTouchEvent(const ui::MotionEventAndroid& event) { if (event.GetAction() == DOWN && ShouldRequestUnbufferedDispatch()) view_.RequestUnbufferedDispatch(event); ...}'. android_webview/common/aw_content_client.cc: 'bool AwContentClient::UsingSynchronousCompositing() { return true; }' (content default returns false). components/input/features.cc: 'BASE_FEATURE(kUseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch, base::FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT)'. ViewAndroidDelegate.requestUnbufferedDispatch skips only LG devices on Android <= 11 for stylus (crbug.com/1064161 workaround), showing stylus streams are otherwise passed to container.requestUnbufferedDispatch(event). ui/android/event_forwarder.cc comment: 'In the case of unbuffered input that chrome uses usually history_size == 0 and oldest_time == latest_time'. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:content/browser/web_contents/web_contents_view_android.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:android_webview/common/aw_content_client.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:components/input/features.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/ViewAndroidDelegate.java) => Explains why the same web code feels different in Chrome vs the shipped Android (WebView) app: Chrome gets per-sample events; the WebView gets per-frame batches.
- [high] Chromium converts only the newest sample of each Android MotionEvent into a web event; historical (batched) samples are never converted into coalesced pointer events, and the web event's timestamp is taken from the OLDEST sample in the batch. - ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent builds 'result.touches[i] = CreateWebTouchPoint(event, i)' using event.GetX/GetY/GetPressure/GetTiltX/... (current sample only); the file contains no reference to GetHistorySize/GetHistoricalX. RenderWidgetHostViewAndroid::OnTouchEvent passes the single MotionEventAndroid straight to CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent; MotionEventAndroid exposes GetHistorySize/GetHistoricalX/Y/EventTime but no web-event code consumes them (the gesture detector uses them for velocity only). EventForwarder.java: 'Android may batch multiple events together for efficiency. We want to use the oldest event time as hardware time stamp ... Note that we still keep the historical events for tracking velocity. final long oldestEventTime = historySize == 0 || mUseBufferedInput ? latestEventTime : MotionEventUtils.getHistoricalEventTimeNanos(event, 0);' and MotionEventAndroid::GetEventTime() returns cached_oldest_event_time_, which CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent uses as the WebTouchEvent time. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/EventForwarder.java ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/android/motion_event_android.cc) => In the Android WebView build, 1-2 of every 2-3 pen samples are dropped inside Chromium before any DOM event exists; getCoalescedEvents() cannot recover them, and each surviving point carries a timestamp up to one frame (8.3 ms at 120 Hz) older than its coordinates. This is the mechanism behind 'points skipped, stroke connects straight to where the pen ended' - the app's timestamp de-dup is not what drops them.
- [medium] Inference (high confidence): the WebView host app can restore per-sample delivery by calling View.requestUnbufferedDispatch itself on the WebView for stylus ACTION_DOWN, exactly as Chrome does; a persistent variant exists since API 30. - View.requestUnbufferedDispatch(MotionEvent) is public API (API 21): 'action != ACTION_DOWN && action != ACTION_MOVE || !event.isTouchEvent() -> return; mAttachInfo.mUnbufferedDispatchRequested = true'. requestUnbufferedDispatch(@InputSourceClass int source) (API 30): 'similar to requestUnbufferedDispatch(MotionEvent), but does not automatically terminate, and allows the specification of arbitrary input source classes ... Set to InputDevice#SOURCE_CLASS_NONE in order to return to default behaviour.' Chromium's own use (ViewAndroidDelegate.requestUnbufferedDispatch -> container.requestUnbufferedDispatch(event)) confirms it works for the WebView container View and for stylus tool types (only LG <= Android 11 excluded). Because the request is on the View receiving the stream, a WebView subclass overriding dispatchTouchEvent/onTouchEvent can make the call before delegating to super. Android warns: 'Buffered dispatch provides many of benefits, and just requesting unbuffered dispatch on most MotionEvent streams will not improve your input latency' - for inking, per-sample delivery is the point, and Chrome accepts the trade-off for all touch input. (https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/View.java ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/ViewAndroidDelegate.java) => The cheapest, highest-impact fix for the Android app: one native override, no web code change, brings the WebView to parity with Chrome (all samples, hardware timestamps, pointerrawupdate per sample). Must be verified on the target Samsung tablet by logging inter-sample intervals.
- [high] Windows pen input in Chromium: each WM_POINTERUPDATE is read with GetPointerPenInfo only - the Windows coalesced history (GetPointerPenInfoHistory) is never read - so samples coalesced by Windows during UI-thread stalls are lost; pen-in-contact without barrel buttons is routed as a TOUCH event (touch pipeline, touch-action applies), hover or barrel-button contact as a MOUSE event; pressure = raw/1024, twist = rotation % 180, tilt as integers; event timeStamp is the processing time, not the hardware time. - hwnd_message_handler.cc HandlePointerEventTypePenClient: 'if (!GetPointerPenInfo(pointer_id, &pointer_pen_info)) {...}' then pen_processor_.GenerateEvent; the file has no GetPointerPenInfoHistory/GetPointerInfoHistory call; ctor 'pen_processor_(&id_generator_, true)' (direct manipulation on). pen_event_processor.cc: 'If the flag is disabled, we send mouse events for all pen inputs ... Send MouseEvents when the pen is hovering or any buttons (other than the tip) are depressed when the stylus makes contact', 'float pressure = pointer_pen_info.pressure / 1024; int rotation_angle = rotation % 180'; touch events use ui::EventTimeForNow(). events_win_utils.cc EventTimeFromMSG: 'we use TimeTicks::Now() for event timestamp instead of the native timestamp'. Microsoft: 'If the application does not process pointer input messages as fast as they are generated, some moves may be coalesced. When an application receives a coalescable pointer (of type PT_PEN) message, it can use the GetPointerPenInfoHistory function to retrieve information for all the individual inputs ... The most recent entry is the same as that returned by the GetPointerPenInfo function.' (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/views/win/hwnd_message_handler.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/views/win/pen_event_processor.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/win/events_win_utils.cc ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-getpointerpeninfohistory) => On Windows (Chrome, Edge, WebView2, Electron) the web page sees every pen report as long as the browser UI thread keeps up; timeStamp deltas are jittery (processing time), so velocity/pressure smoothing must not trust them blindly; touch-action:none is required on Windows too because pen contact is a touch event; twist is capped at 179 degrees.
- [high] getPredictedEvents(): Chromium (Chrome 77+, Android, WebView, Windows) generates predictions with a Kalman filter by default, only on pointermove, up to 25 ms ahead in steps equal to the measured inter-sample interval (min 2.5 ms, default 8 ms), only once the filter is 'stable'; predicted events are clones of the parent with only the position changed. Resampling of the parent pointermove to frame time (kResamplingInputEvents) is disabled by default, so the parent pointermove equals the last real sample. - MainThreadEventQueue ctor: 'event_predictor_ = std::make_unique<InputEventPrediction>(base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(blink::features::kResamplingInputEvents));' features.cc: 'BASE_FEATURE(kResamplingInputEvents, base::FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT)', 'BASE_FEATURE(kInputPredictorTypeChoice, ... DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT)' -> 'selected_predictor_type_ = kScrollPredictorTypeKalman'. InputEventPrediction::HandleEvents handles kMouseMove/kTouchMove/kPointerMove only: UpdatePrediction per coalesced event, ApplyResampling only if enable_resampling_, then AddPredictedEvents: 'max_prediction_timestamp = last_event_timestamp_ + MaxPredictionTime()' looping 'predict_time += TimeInterval()' and 'predicted_event = coalesced_event.Event().Clone(); ... SetPositionInWidget(pred)'. input_predictor.h: kTimeInterval 8 ms, kMinTimeInterval 2.5 ms, kMaxResampleTime 20 ms, kMaxPredictionTime 25 ms, kMaxTimeDelta 20 ms (gap resets). KalmanPredictor::HasPrediction: 'x_predictor_.Stable() && y_predictor_.Stable()'. PointerEventFactory::Create: 'if (type == kPointermove) predicted_pointer_events = CreateEventSequence(...)' (never for rawupdate). BCD: getPredictedEvents Chrome 77, Firefox 89, Safari 18.2. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/input_event_prediction.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/base/prediction/input_predictor.h ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/common/features.cc ; https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/PointerEvent.json) => Prediction is usable for the preview tail (≈3-6 points at 120-240 Hz, ≤25 ms) on both target platforms, but only from pointermove and only for position - pressure/tilt of predicted points are copies. Since resampling is off, the pointermove parent is a real sample identical to its last coalesced entry.
- [high] Event.timeStamp resolution in Chromium is 100 microseconds (5 us in cross-origin-isolated pages), clamped deterministically, so the same platform timestamp always maps to the same DOMHighResTimeStamp; Android supplies nanosecond-precision hardware times; Windows supplies processing times. - time_clamper.h: 'kCoarseResolutionMicroseconds = 100; kFineResolutionMicroseconds = 5'; time_clamper.cc: 'int resolution = cross_origin_isolated_capability ? kFineResolutionMicroseconds : kCoarseResolutionMicroseconds; clamped_time = time_lower_digits - time_lower_digits % resolution; ... tick_threshold = ThresholdFor(clamped_time, resolution)' (hash of clamped value with a per-process secret, i.e. deterministic per input). Event::timeStamp -> Performance::MonotonicTimeToDOMHighResTimeStamp(platform_time_stamp_). Android: MotionEventUtils.getEventTimeNanos uses MotionEvent.getEventTimeNanos() (API 34) or the hidden getEventTimeNano via reflection; MotionEvent.java: 'The value is in nanosecond precision but it may not have nanosecond accuracy.' Windows: EventTimeFromMSG returns EventTimeForNow(). (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/timing/time_clamper.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/util/MotionEventUtils.java ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/win/events_win_utils.cc) => Two distinct 240 Hz samples (4.17 ms apart) never collide at 100 us resolution, and a sample seen on both channels has an identical timeStamp - so equality de-dup is sound in Chromium but unnecessary if a single channel is used; on Windows, timestamps reflect UI-thread scheduling, so two back-to-back reports after a stall can land within the same 100 us bucket.
- [medium] Ink API / delegated ink trails: API surface (navigator.ink.requestPresenter, updateInkTrailStartPoint) ships in Chrome 94+/Edge 93+ on all Chromium platforms including Android and WebView per BCD, but the trail is only rendered when the browser can forward pointer points to viz; Windows uses the OS DirectComposition ink path, other platforms use a Skia fallback, and on Android the forwarding is gated on a ui::Compositor that RenderWidgetHostViewAndroid never provides - so delegated ink is inert on Android/WebView. - BCD Navigator.ink / Ink.requestPresenter / DelegatedInkTrailPresenter.updateInkTrailStartPoint: chrome 94, edge 93, chrome_android/webview_android 'mirror', firefox/safari false. skia_renderer.cc: 'delegated_ink_handler_ = std::make_unique<DelegatedInkHandler>(output_surface_->capabilities().supports_delegated_ink)' and 'if (UsingSkiaForDelegatedInk()) ... DrawDelegatedInkTrail'. render_widget_host_impl.cc ForwardDelegatedInkPoint: 'auto* delegated_ink_point_renderer = delegate_->GetDelegatedInkRenderer(view_->GetCompositor()); if (!delegated_ink_point_renderer) return;' web_contents_impl.cc GetDelegatedInkRenderer: 'if (!compositor) return nullptr;'. RenderWidgetHostViewBase::GetCompositor() returns nullptr and render_widget_host_view_android.h declares no GetCompositor() override (only GetCompositorImpl/GetCompositorViewportPixelSize); no other Android ink path exists in rwhva/web_contents_view_android/delegated_frame_host_android. delegated_ink_point_renderer_base.cc FilterPoints: 'If no point with any pointer id exactly matches the metadata ... return an empty vector so that nothing will be drawn.' WICG explainer: 'Operating system compositors typically introduce a frame of latency ... If the system knows the last rendered point, it can produce the segments of the ink trail for input that has been delivered, but not yet rendered' and it is 'a progressive enhancement for the last few pixels'. Edge reported 'up to a 240% improvement in latency' on Windows 11 (vendor claim, secondary reporting). (https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/Ink.json ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:content/browser/renderer_host/render_widget_host_impl.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:content/browser/web_contents/web_contents_impl.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:content/browser/renderer_host/render_widget_host_view_android.h ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:components/viz/service/display/skia_renderer.cc ; https://github.com/WICG/ink-enhancement ; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/ZtqwKR_HIAE/m/t61vWodBBQAJ) => Do not build the Android preview around navigator.ink - it will resolve a presenter and silently draw nothing. Use it opportunistically on Windows (Chromium-based hosts) where it removes roughly a compositor frame from the visible tail; keep the app's own preview as the primary path everywhere. (The 'inert on Android' conclusion is inferred from the source paths, not from a vendor statement.)
- [high] Secure-context requirements: pointerrawupdate is exposed only in secure contexts (Chrome 142+ enforces the spec; earlier Chrome exposed it everywhere) and getCoalescedEvents is [SecureContext] in Blink; getPredictedEvents is not. file: URLs, https, and localhost count as potentially trustworthy. - pointer_event.idl: '[Measure, SecureContext] sequence<PointerEvent> getCoalescedEvents(); [Measure] sequence<PointerEvent> getPredictedEvents();'. runtime_enabled_features.json5: 'name: "PointerRawUpdateOnlyInSecureContext", status: "stable"'. BCD Element.pointerrawupdate_event chrome: 'version_added: 77 ... Before version 142, pointerrawupdate events were exposed to non-secure contexts.' Secure Contexts spec 'Is origin potentially trustworthy?': 'If origin's scheme is either https or wss, return Potentially Trustworthy' ... 'If origin's scheme is file, return Potentially Trustworthy' with the note that UAs 'MAY choose to more strictly assign trust in a way which excludes file'; loopback 127.0.0.0/8, ::1 and 'localhost' names are trustworthy. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/events/pointer_event.idl ; https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/Element.json ; https://w3c.github.io/webappsec-secure-contexts/) => If the packaged apps load content over plain http:// (e.g. a local dev server on a LAN address) both APIs vanish and the app silently falls back to per-frame pointermove without coalesced samples - a plausible contributor to 'preview sometimes missing' and lost points. Serve via https (WebViewAssetLoader https://appassets.androidplatform.net, WebView2 virtual host mapping), file://, or localhost.
- [high] Cross-browser support (BCD): getCoalescedEvents - Chrome 58, Firefox 59 (Firefox for Android: 'always returns an empty array'), Safari 18.2; getPredictedEvents - Chrome 77, Firefox 89, Safari 18.2; pointerrawupdate - Chrome 77, Firefox 148 (140-147 partial, movementX/Y always 0), Safari: not supported; altitudeAngle/azimuthAngle - Chrome 86, Firefox 131, Safari 18.2; twist - Chrome 57, Safari 13. - Values read from mdn/browser-compat-data api/PointerEvent.json and api/Element.json (chrome_android, webview_android, edge, samsunginternet_android are 'mirror' of Chrome). tldraw's experience (PR #5554, reapplied as #5898): 'on iOS, in some circumstances ... the getCoalescedEvents is not present, so a null check was added'; they enable coalesced-event replay 'for drawing and highlighting tools' only and observed smoother segments 'with a 20x CPU slowdown'. (https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/PointerEvent.json ; https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/Element.json ; https://github.com/tldraw/tldraw/pull/5554) => The web build needs a three-tier capability ladder (pointerrawupdate -> pointermove+coalesced -> pointermove), with feature detection of both the event and the method (typeof e.getCoalescedEvents === 'function' && list.length > 0).
- [high] S-Pen attributes as exposed by Chromium on Android: pointerType 'pen' (TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS) or 'eraser' (TOOL_TYPE_ERASER); pressure passed through unclamped from MotionEvent (forced to 0 while no button is down, i.e. hovering, and to 0.5 if NaN); tiltX/tiltY computed from AXIS_TILT+AXIS_ORIENTATION and rounded to integer degrees; altitudeAngle/azimuthAngle derived from tilt; twist is ALWAYS 0 and tangentialPressure 0 on Android; legacy Samsung S-Pen action codes 211-214 are remapped to DOWN/UP/MOVE/CANCEL. - blink_event_util.cc ToWebPointerType: STYLUS->kPen, ERASER->kEraser, FINGER->kTouch. motion_event_android.cc: 'float MotionEventAndroid::GetTwist(...) { return 0.f; }', 'GetTangentialPressure ... return 0.f', ConvertTiltOrientationToTiltXY: 'tilt_x = RadToDeg(atan2f(sinf(-orientation_rad) * r, z))'. pointer_event_factory.cc: 'GetPointerEventPressure(force, buttons): if (!buttons) return 0; if (isnan(force)) return 0.5; return force;' and 'setTiltX(round(web_pointer_event.tilt_x)) ... setAltitudeAngle(PointerEventUtil::AltitudeFromTilt(...)) ... setTwist(web_pointer_event.twist)'. Android stylus docs: pressure is 'between 0 (no pressure) and 1 ... though higher values can be returned depending on screen calibration. Make sure you verify the value is between 0 and 1'; tilt 'Positive angle in radians where 0 is perpendicular to screen and pi/2 is flat'. SPenSupport.java: SPEN_ACTION_DOWN=211, UP=212, MOVE=213, CANCEL=214 converted when 'com.sec.feature.spen_usp' is present. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/android/motion_event_android.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/events/pointer_event_factory.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/SPenSupport.java ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/advanced-stylus-features) => 2.0's sample record can be {x, y, t, pressure, tiltX, tiltY} - twist is unavailable on the primary device; clamp pressure to [0,1] in the app; pressure==0 during hover is expected, so hover samples must not be treated as zero-width ink.
- [high] Palm-rejection signals available to a web app on Android are limited to pointerType ('touch' vs 'pen'/'eraser') and contact size: Chromium does not surface Android's FLAG_CANCELED (API 33) - a palm-rejected ACTION_POINTER_UP arrives as an ordinary pointerup - and does not expose MotionEvent classifications; only ACTION_CANCEL becomes pointercancel. width/height default to 1 when contact geometry is unknown. - motion_event_android.cc: 'cached_flags_(ToEventFlags(android_meta_state, android_button_state))' - only meta and button state are read; FLAG_CANCELED is not referenced anywhere in the Android event path. MotionEvent.java FLAG_CANCELED: 'only set for events with ACTION_POINTER_UP and ACTION_CANCEL. It indicates that the pointer going up was an unintentional user touch ... typically set when the user was accidentally touching the screen, such as by gripping the device, or placing the palm on the screen.' Pointer Events spec width: 'For inputs that typically lack contact geometry ... the user agent MUST return a default value of 1'; Chromium fills width/height from touchMajor/touchMinor (EventForwarder passes touchMajor/Minor; CreateWebTouchPoint radius_x/y = minor/major /2) and 'If width/height is unknown we let PointerEventInit set it to 1'. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:ui/events/android/motion_event_android.cc ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/MotionEvent.java ; https://w3c.github.io/pointerevents/) => Palm rejection must be app-level: while a pen pointer is active (pointerdown..pointerup) ignore all pointerType==='touch' pointers, and treat a touch pointer with large width/height (finger/palm) that starts within ~200 ms before a pen-down as a palm; the OS may still cancel a palm with ACTION_CANCEL (pointercancel) which the app must survive without losing the pen stroke (pointercancel carries the touch pointerId, not the pen's).
- [high] pointercancel causes on Android/WebView: (a) Chromium starts a pan/zoom because touch-action permits it (spec: 'Right before starting to pan or zoom, the user agent MUST suppress a pointer event stream'); (b) Android sends ACTION_CANCEL - parent ViewGroup interception in WebView, system gesture navigation, or palm rejection; (c) stylus handwriting: Chromium disables Android's automatic handwriting on the container view and starts handwriting only when an editable element is focused for stylus writing, but Blink retargets a pen pointerdown to the nearest 'stylus writable' node within the platform handwriting radius. - AwContents.java onTouchEvent: 'If the stylus is above an editable element, prevent the parent element from intercepting the scroll event. if (event.getToolType(0) == MotionEvent.TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS && getLastHitTestResult().hitTestResultType == 9 /* HitTestDataType::kEditText */) mContainerView.getParent().requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(true);' - i.e. only for stylus over an edit field; for other content a scrollable parent may intercept and Android will deliver ACTION_CANCEL (Chromium: Action::CANCEL -> kTouchCancel -> pointercancel). AndroidStylusWritingHandler.onWebContentsChanged: 'view.setAutoHandwritingEnabled(false)'; onEditElementFocusedForStylusWriting: 'mInputMethodManager.startStylusHandwriting(view)'. pointer_event_manager.cc: ShouldAdjustPointerEvent for kPen/kEraser pointerdown -> AdjustPointerEvent with 'adjustment_width = adjustment_height = handwriting_radius_ * (device_scale_factor / page_scale_factor)' and 'BestNodeForHitTestResult(TouchAdjustmentCandidateType::kStylusWritable, ...) ... this is intended only for stylus handwriting'. Android: 'Stylus handwriting is enabled by default on Android 14 (API level 34)'; 'The handwriting bounds include 40 dp of vertical padding and 10 dp of horizontal padding around the view.' pointer_event_manager.cc also dispatches pointercancel on kTouchScrollStarted, which is suppressed when touch-action is none. Windows: pen contact is a touch event (see Windows finding), so the same touch-action rule applies there. (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:android_webview/java/src/org/chromium/android_webview/AwContents.java ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:components/stylus_handwriting/android/java/src/org/chromium/components/stylus_handwriting/AndroidStylusWritingHandler.java ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/input/pointer_event_manager.cc ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/stylus-input-in-text-fields ; https://w3c.github.io/pointerevents/) => The inking surface needs touch-action:none (both platforms); the Android host must not wrap the WebView in a ScrollView/ViewPager that can intercept (or must call requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent itself); and text inputs/contenteditable must not sit within the handwriting radius of where pen strokes begin, otherwise pen-downs are retargeted and may launch system handwriting instead of inking.
- [high] A canvas created with {desynchronized: true} changes input scheduling: when a pointerdown's hit target is that canvas, Blink asks the main-thread queue to stop rAF-aligning mousemove/touchmove/wheel until the pointer goes up, so pointermove itself arrives as soon as possible for the rest of the stroke. - html_canvas_element.cc: 'if (context_->CreationAttributes().desynchronized) { if (!CreateLayer()) return nullptr; SetNeedsUnbufferedInputEvents(true); ...}'. pointer_event_manager.cc DispatchPointerEvent: 'if (event_type == kPointerdown) { auto* html_canvas_element = DynamicTo<HTMLCanvasElement>(target->ToNode()); if (html_canvas_element && html_canvas_element->NeedsUnbufferedInputEvents()) frame_->GetChromeClient().RequestUnbufferedInputEvents(frame_); }' -> WebFrameWidgetImpl::RequestUnbufferedInputEvents -> MainThreadEventQueue::RequestUnbufferedInputEvents: 'needs_low_latency_until_pointer_up_.store(true)'; IsRafAlignedEvent then returns false for moves; the flag is cleared on kMouseUp/kTouchEnd/kTouchCancel/kPointerUp/kPointerCancel. (This is renderer-side scheduling only; it does not request Android unbuffered dispatch.) (https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/core/input/pointer_event_manager.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc) => In the current architecture the pointerdown often lands on a stroke <div> inside #world rather than on the full-stage desynchronized canvas, so this low-latency mode is not engaged. In 2.0 the desynchronized preview canvas should be the element that receives pointerdown (or be raised to the top while the pen hovers/writes) - then even the fallback pointermove path becomes unbuffered for the stroke.
- [high] MDN's guidance: pointerrawupdate 'is not more spatially or temporally precise' than pointermove - both carry identical property values for the same movement; its only benefit is dispatch timing, and listening to it 'can impact performance'. - MDN: pointerrawupdate 'Fires as frequently as the browser can produce events' vs pointermove 'Browser may delay for performance optimization'; 'pointerrawupdate is not more spatially or temporally precise than pointermove - both carry identical property values'; 'The target of dispatched events is the same as the last coalesced event'; 'For most use cases, other pointer event types should suffice.' getCoalescedEvents MDN: 'un-coalesced position changes are desirable in drawing applications ... where having access to all events helps to build smoother curves.' (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/pointerrawupdate_event ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/PointerEvent/getCoalescedEvents) => Reinforces the single-channel design: rawupdate buys latency, not extra points; the extra points come from getCoalescedEvents() and, on Android WebView, from unbuffered dispatch in the host.
- [low] Samsung's published S-Pen latency figures are 9 ms (Galaxy Tab S7), 6.2 ms (Tab S8) and 2.8 ms (Tab S8+/Ultra, Samsung Notes only, achieved with on-device prediction); the digitizer report rate is not published by Samsung, and Android documents touchscreen sampling rates as ranging '60 to 1000Hz'. - HotHardware review (quoting Samsung): 'Samsung claims that it was able to reduce tracking latency from 9ms on the S7 to 6.2ms on the S8' and 'The latency is even lower for the S8+ and Ultra at 2.8ms, down from 5.6ms on the S7+'; the tablets run at 120 Hz and the pen reports 4,096 pressure levels. Search-engine summaries of Samsung's newsroom attribute the 2.8 ms to 'a prediction algorithm' in Samsung Notes. Android stylus docs: 'Touch sampling rate (hardware): The number of times per second a touchscreen checks whether a pointer is touching the surface, ranging from 60 to 1000Hz.' Samsung's own newsroom pages could not be fetched (timeouts) and Wacom's report-rate pages returned 403, so no primary figure for the S-Pen report rate (commonly cited as ~240 Hz for Wacom EMR) was verified. (https://hothardware.com/reviews/samsung-galaxy-tab-s8-review ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/advanced-stylus-features) => The 2.8 ms number is a native-app + prediction figure and is not attainable in any browser path (a 120 Hz frame alone is 8.3 ms); set expectations around ~1-2 frames plus prediction. Measure the actual sample rate on the device (median timeStamp delta of coalesced samples) instead of assuming 240 or 360 Hz.

Recommendations:
- Adopt a single-channel sample collector per stroke: if ('onpointerrawupdate' in window && window.isSecureContext), register pointerrawupdate on the captured target and treat its samples as the ONLY geometry source: for each event take `const list = (typeof e.getCoalescedEvents === 'function' && e.getCoalescedEvents()) || []; if (!list.length) list = [e];` and append every entry {clientX, clientY, pressure, tiltX, tiltY, timeStamp} in order. Register pointermove on the same target only to read getPredictedEvents() for the preview tail - never append its coalesced samples. Otherwise (Safari, Firefox < 148, insecure context) use pointermove + the same coalesced-list logic. This removes the need for any cross-channel de-duplication; the current timestamp-based de-dup is what invites subtle loss and should be deleted, not tuned.
- Start the stroke from pointerdown (pointerType 'pen' or 'eraser', isPrimary) and call target.setPointerCapture(pointerId); end on pointerup/pointercancel of that pointerId. Chromium does not emit a pointerrawupdate for the pen-down itself, so the pointerdown sample is appended exactly once by the down handler. Ignore all pointerType==='touch' pointers while a pen pointer is active and never let a touch pointercancel terminate the pen stroke (compare pointerId).
- Fix the Android app (WebView host) at the platform layer: subclass the WebView (or the AwContents container) and in dispatchTouchEvent(MotionEvent ev) call `if (ev.getActionMasked()==MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN && ev.getToolType(0)==MotionEvent.TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS) requestUnbufferedDispatch(ev);` before super.dispatchTouchEvent (API 21+; on API 30+ optionally requestUnbufferedDispatch(InputDevice.SOURCE_STYLUS|SOURCE_TOUCHSCREEN) once). This mirrors what Chrome for Android does and stops Android from batching pen samples per vsync - the only way to recover the samples Chromium's WebView path discards. Verify with a debug overlay showing samples-per-frame and median timeStamp delta (~4.2 ms expected at 240 Hz vs ~8.3 ms if still batched).
- Make the desynchronized preview canvas the element that receives the pen's pointerdown (raise it above #world while pointerType==='pen' is hovering, or route hit-testing through it), and give it `touch-action: none; user-select: none; -webkit-user-select: none`. This both engages Chromium's low-latency (non-rAF-aligned) scheduling for the remainder of the stroke and prevents pan-induced pointercancel on Android and Windows (where pen contact is routed through the touch pipeline).
- Do no painting inside pointerrawupdate handlers. Append to a preallocated Float32Array ring buffer and schedule one paint per requestAnimationFrame that (a) commits new segments to the desynchronized canvas incrementally (only the new segment's dirty rect, not the whole stroke) and (b) redraws a short tail: last real segment + getPredictedEvents() positions (<= 25 ms ahead) with the parent's pressure. Never commit predicted points to the stroke data.
- Serve the app in a secure context on every platform: WebViewAssetLoader (https://appassets.androidplatform.net) or file:// on Android, a virtual https host in WebView2 / file:// on Windows, https on the web. Feature-detect pointerrawupdate and getCoalescedEvents at runtime and surface a visible 'reduced input mode' indicator when the fallback ladder is in use, so the 'preview sometimes missing' symptom can be attributed correctly.
- Keep the Android host view hierarchy free of touch-intercepting parents (ScrollView, ViewPager, SwipeRefreshLayout) around the WebView, or call getParent().requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(true) on stylus ACTION_DOWN; keep <input>/<textarea>/contenteditable elements at least ~40 dp away from pen-down zones (or hidden while inking) so Blink's stylus-writable retargeting and Android 14 handwriting cannot capture a stroke.
- Treat the Ink API as a Windows-only progressive enhancement: `if (navigator.ink) presenter = await navigator.ink.requestPresenter({presentationArea: canvas})` and call presenter.updateInkTrailStartPoint(lastTrustedPointerEvent, {color, diameter}) once per frame from the pointermove handler; on Android/WebView it resolves but draws nothing, so gate it on navigator.userAgentData.platform === 'Windows' (or measure presenter.expectedImprovement) and keep the app-drawn preview as the primary path.
- Normalise attributes at ingestion: clamp pressure to [0,1] (Android may exceed 1), expect pressure 0 while hovering (buttons===0), store tilt as integers, ignore twist (always 0 on Android, capped at 179 on Windows). On Windows do not derive velocity solely from timeStamp deltas (they are processing times); use the sample index or a smoothed dt.
- Log a per-device input profile once (browser, platform, secure context, rawupdate support, samples per frame, median dt, isPrimary/pointerType distribution) to settle the open questions below empirically on the actual Samsung tablet and on the Windows pen hardware.
Open questions:
- What is the actual S-Pen report rate on the user's tablet model (Wacom EMR ~240 Hz vs Samsung's newer digitizers)? No primary source was verifiable (Samsung newsroom timed out, Wacom pages returned 403); measure median coalesced-sample timeStamp delta on-device.
- Does the shipped Android app use the system WebView (assumed, given the synchronous-compositor analysis) or a custom Chromium/GeckoView engine? If it is a WebView, the requestUnbufferedDispatch fix applies; if it is a Trusted Web Activity/Custom Tab it already runs Chrome's unbuffered path and the dropped-sample cause must lie elsewhere.
- Will Chromium's experimental kUseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch (currently disabled) ship, and if so will Chromium start converting historical MotionEvent samples into coalesced events? Today no such conversion exists; if the flag ships without it, Chrome-for-Android would regress to the WebView behaviour.
- Is the Windows build hosted in WebView2/Electron (Chromium, WM_POINTER pen-as-touch path, Ink API available) or in another engine? The Windows findings assume Chromium.
- How does the LG/Android<=11 exemption in Chromium's requestUnbufferedDispatch generalise - are there Samsung firmware versions where unbuffered stylus dispatch misbehaves? Needs on-device testing across Android 13/14/15.
- Does the 'preview sometimes missing entirely' symptom correlate with the fallback ladder (insecure context or missing getCoalescedEvents) or with main-thread stalls from DOM stroke elements? The input profile log recommended above should distinguish the two.

## canvas
Research on low-latency/high-throughput 2D canvas in Chromium, verified against current Chromium and Skia source (GitHub mirror, Sept 2026), WHATWG/MDN, Chrome engineering posts, and the renderers of Excalidraw/tldraw/perfect-freehand. Key results: (1) `desynchronized:true` in Chromium does three things — gives the canvas its own SurfaceLayer/CompositorFrameSink, submits a compositor frame directly at the end of the JS task that drew (bypassing the page's BeginMainFrame/commit), and switches pointer/touch move delivery from rAF-aligned to unbuffered; the fourth effect, true single-buffered scanout (front buffer), engages on Android only when GPU raster is used, SurfaceControl is enabled (Chrome: on by default; WebView: off by default), the `LowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas` flag is on, the GPU reports scanout-capable shared images, and viz can promote the canvas quad to an overlay (opacity 1, kSrc/kSrcOver blend, axis-aligned transform, no mask/backdrop-filter, not occluded unless opaque). If any of that fails, Chromium silently strips the SCANOUT/CONCURRENT_READ_WRITE flags. (2) `getContextAttributes().desynchronized` only echoes the requested attribute (both 2D and WebGL); it is not evidence that low latency engaged — even on macOS where the attribute is a documented no-op it returns true. (3) In single-buffer mode the display reads the buffer while the page writes, so clear-and-repaint of the wet stroke can be scanned out half-drawn; this is consistent with the user's 'preview sometimes missing'. Wet ink must be composed off-screen and blitted, or drawn append-only. (4) Blink's canvas is a paint-op recorder rasterised in the GPU process: recorded ops are flushed mid-frame once they exceed 2 MB (6 MB under Graphite) or 32 MB of pinned images; Path2D paint-caching in the GPU process is off by default (`kPath2DPaintCache` disabled), and Skia's Ganesh only caches triangulations for non-AA paths and refuses GPU path rendering above 16,384 verbs — so re-filling every committed stroke as a path each frame scales with page content in both IPC bytes and GPU tessellation. Per-frame cost independent of content requires committed ink to be bitmaps (tiles), blitted with drawImage/ImageBitmap. (5) Hard limits: canvas area 268,435,456 px (32768×8192), dimension 65,535 (Chrome; measured 16,384² on Android 8+, 14,188² on Android 7); readback heuristics flip an accelerated canvas to CPU after 2 getImageData calls unless `willReadFrequently` is set (and `willReadFrequently:true` forces CPU raster, which disables the low-latency path). (6) OffscreenCanvas in workers is available on Chrome/WebView 69+, Safari 16.4+, Firefox 105+; `transferFromImageBitmap` is zero-copy ownership transfer. Excalidraw uses three stacked full-viewport canvases (static/interactive/new-element) plus a per-element bitmap cache sized to bounds×DPR×zoom and renders freedraw via perfect-freehand→Path2D fill; tldraw renders each draw shape as SVG DOM. The recommended 2.0 pipeline: a fixed, untransformed, topmost desynchronized wet canvas fed from an off-screen stroke accumulator (append-only blits, predicted tail via getPredictedEvents/Ink API), committed ink rasterised in a Worker into world-space tiles at zoom buckets and blitted per frame, selection UI on a separate interactive canvas, no per-stroke DOM.

Findings:
- [high] In Chromium, `desynchronized:true` on an HTML canvas triggers three concrete mechanisms regardless of platform: a dedicated SurfaceLayer + CompositorFrameSink, direct frame submission at the end of the drawing task, and unbuffered input. - html_canvas_element.cc: when the context is created with `CreationAttributes().desynchronized`, Chromium runs `if (!CreateLayer()) return nullptr; SetNeedsUnbufferedInputEvents(true); GetOrCreateResourceDispatcher();`. `CreateLayer()` builds a `SurfaceLayerBridge` (the same mechanism video/OffscreenCanvas use) rather than a normal composited texture layer, and `CanvasRenderingContext2D::IsComposited()` returns false when `element->LowLatencyEnabled()`. In `PostFinalizeFrame`, `if (LowLatencyEnabled() && (IsWebGL() || IsRenderingContext2D()) && frame_dispatcher_ && !dirty_rect_.IsEmpty())` the canvas resource is exported and `frame_dispatcher_->DispatchFrame(std::move(exported_resource), dirty_rect_, IsOpaque())` is called; `CanvasResourceDispatcher::DispatchFrame` immediately does `sink_->SubmitCompositorFrame(...)`. The source comment on `LowLatencyEnabled()` notes it 'just checks whether the desynchronized attribute is set on the context'. The 2019 Intent to Ship describes the same split: 'All platforms support the desynchronization between input events and compositor frame, CrOs supports also front-buffer rendering'. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc (LowLatencyEnabled, PostFinalizeFrame, CreateLayer, GetOrCreateResourceDispatcher); https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_resource_dispatcher.cc; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/nxjWgMIeC1Q/m/GfwjbzeVAwAJ) => Even when true front-buffer scanout does not engage on the tablet, a desynchronized wet canvas still skips the page's BeginMainFrame/commit and gets unbuffered pointer events. The wet canvas must therefore be its own element (not painted through the DOM/#world) and drawing should happen directly in the pointer handler; each JS task that draws produces a compositor frame.
- [high] The single-buffered (front-buffer/overlay) path on Android is gated on GPU raster + SurfaceControl + a default-on Blink feature + scanout-capable shared images; when any gate fails Chromium silently removes the low-latency usage flags and falls back to a normal canvas. - canvas_utils.cc: `bool LowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas2D(RasterMode raster_mode) { ... if (raster_mode == RasterMode::kCPU) return false; #if IS_WIN return ...shared_image_swap_chain; #elif IS_ANDROID return LowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas(); #elif IS_CHROMEOS return true; #else /* Mac */ return false; }` and `#if IS_ANDROID bool LowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas() { // Low-latency usage on Android is possible only with SurfaceControl. return ::features::IsAndroidSurfaceControlEnabled() && base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(features::kLowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas); }`. blink features.cc: `#if IS_ANDROID // Allow low latency canvas (both 2D and WebGL) to be in overlay (generally meaning scanned out directly to display), even if regular canvases are not in overlay. BASE_FEATURE(kLowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas, FEATURE_ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT)`. gpu_finch_features.cc: `BASE_FEATURE(kAndroidSurfaceControl, FEATURE_ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT)`. canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc then adds `SHARED_IMAGE_USAGE_SCANOUT` and `SHARED_IMAGE_USAGE_CONCURRENT_READ_WRITE`. canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc: `bool is_overlay_supported = is_mappable_shared_image_allowed && (!is_accelerated || shared_image_caps.supports_scanout_shared_images); ... // If we cannot use overlay, we have to remove the scanout flag and the concurrent read write flag. if (!is_overlay_supported) shared_image_usage_flags.RemoveAll(CONCURRENT_READ_WRITE | SCANOUT);` and the image pool is created with `is_single_buffered ? 0 : kMaxRecycledCanvasResources` (kMaxRecycledCanvasResources = 3). (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/canvas_utils.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/common/features.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/gpu/config/gpu_finch_features.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc) => On Chrome for Android (Samsung tablet) the front-buffer path can engage, but only if the canvas is GPU-rasterised (so never `willReadFrequently:true`, never trigger the getImageData CPU fallback) and the device reports scanout shared images. There is no web-visible signal when the flags are stripped.
- [medium] In the Android WebView (the native Android build) hardware overlays via SurfaceControl are disabled by default, so the front-buffer canvas path is not expected to engage there; the direct-frame-dispatch and unbuffered-input effects still apply. - gpu_finch_features.cc: `// Use android SurfaceControl API for managing display compositor's buffer queue and using overlays on Android. Also used by webview to disable surface SurfaceControl. BASE_FEATURE(kAndroidSurfaceControl, FEATURE_ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT); // Hardware Overlays for WebView. BASE_FEATURE(kWebViewSurfaceControl, FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT);`. Since `LowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas()` requires `IsAndroidSurfaceControlEnabled()`, WebView's default configuration cannot satisfy it (inferred from the two flag comments; the WebView-specific override logic itself was not read). html_canvas_element.cc also shows WebView-specific canvas caveats: `// Webview crashes with accelerated small canvases (crbug.com/1004304)` gated by `features::kWebviewAccelerateSmallCanvases`. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/gpu/config/gpu_finch_features.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/canvas_utils.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc) => The Android app (WebView) and the website in Chrome will have different latency floors with identical code. Design the pipeline to be fast without front-buffer mode (append-only wet drawing, prediction) and treat front-buffer mode as a bonus on Chrome.
- [high] `getContextAttributes().desynchronized` in Chromium simply echoes the attribute you passed; it does not indicate whether low-latency/single-buffer mode actually engaged. Chrome's own blog advice to use it as 'feature detection' is therefore only a check that the browser parses the attribute. - base_rendering_context_2d.cc: `CanvasRenderingContext2DSettings* BaseRenderingContext2D::getContextAttributes() const { return ToCanvasRenderingContext2DSettings(CreationAttributes()); }`. webgl_rendering_context_base.cc: `result->setDesynchronized(Host()->LowLatencyEnabled());` where `HTMLCanvasElement::LowLatencyEnabled() const { return context_ && context_->CreationAttributes().desynchronized; }`. A grep of all fetched Chromium canvas sources finds no assignment that rewrites `desynchronized` after platform checks. canvas_utils.cc explicitly says 'currently setting the desynchronized attribute on a canvas is a no-op on Mac', yet the getter would still report true there. The Chrome blog says 'Call getContextAttributes() and check if the returned object has a desynchronized property set to true'; the Intent to Ship says 'a client can just read back the |desynchronized| ... to verify if the UA actually supports lowLatency' — both describe attribute acceptance, not engagement. The WHATWG spec only says the getter returns 'true if the context can be desynchronized'. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/base_rendering_context_2d.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/webgl/webgl_rendering_context_base.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/canvas_utils.cc; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized; https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/canvas.html#canvasrenderingcontext2dsettings) => Do not branch app behaviour on this flag. Always request `desynchronized:true` (harmless when not honoured) and verify engagement empirically on the target device (chrome://tracing overlay events, or camera-measured pen-to-pixel latency).
- [high] Viz will only promote the canvas to a hardware overlay (required for true single-buffer scanout) if the quad has opacity 1, blend mode kSrc/kSrcOver, an axis-aligned transform (SurfaceControl additionally allows flip/90-degree rotations), no gradient mask filter, no backdrop filter on its render pass, no nearest-neighbour filtering, and it is either on top of everything or (as an underlay) opaque and not occluded by filtered content. - overlay_candidate.h lists the rejection reasons: `kFailNotOverlay, kFailNotAxisAligned, kFailNotAxisAligned3dTransform, kFailNotAxisAligned2dShear, kFailNotAxisAligned2dRotation, kFailColorMatrix, kFailOpacity, kFailBlending, kFailQuadNotSupported, kFailVisible, kFailFormat, kFailNearFilter, kFailPriority, kFailRoundedDisplayMasksNotSupported, kFailMaskFilterNotSupported, kFailHasTransformButCantClip, kFailRpdqWithTransform, kFailBackdropFilter`. overlay_candidate_factory.cc: `if (!context_.is_delegated_context && !cc::MathUtil::IsWithinEpsilon(sqs->opacity, 1.0f)) return kFailOpacity;` `// We support only kSrc (no blending) and kSrcOver (blending with premul). if (!(sqs->blend_mode == SkBlendMode::kSrc || sqs->blend_mode == SkBlendMode::kSrcOver)) return kFailBlending;` `if (sqs->mask_filter_info.HasGradientMask()) return kFailMaskFilterNotSupported;` `if (!quad->backdrop_filters.IsEmpty()) return kFailBackdropFilter;` `if (quad->nearest_neighbor) return kFailNearFilter;` and transform checks `if (transform.HasPerspective() || !transform.IsFlat()) return kFailNotAxisAligned3dTransform` with shear/rotation detection. overlay_strategy_single_on_top.cc only accepts a candidate `if (!OverlayCandidateFactory::IsOccluded(...))`; overlay_strategy_underlay.cc skips candidates when `opaque_mode_ == RequireOpaqueCandidates && !candidate.is_opaque` or when `IsOccludedByFilteredQuad`, and replaces the quad with a transparent hole (`ReplaceExistingQuadWithSolidColor(..., SkColors::kTransparent, SkBlendMode::kSrcOver)`). overlay_processor_surface_control.cc: `IsOverlaySupported() { return true; }`, `SupportsFlipRotateTransform() { return true; }`, and rejects candidates whose colour space `!gfx::SurfaceControl::SupportsColorSpace(...)`. The Chrome blog adds the page-level rule: a translucent canvas 'must not have any other DOM elements above it' to remain desynchronized. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/overlay_candidate.h; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/overlay_candidate_factory.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/overlay_strategy_single_on_top.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/overlay_strategy_underlay.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/overlay_processor_surface_control.cc; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized) => Established rules at the quad level; the CSS mapping is inferred but standard: the wet canvas must not sit inside the CSS-transformed `#world` (rotation/3D/shear kills promotion; an ancestor with opacity<1, filter, mask, clip-path, mix-blend-mode or backdrop-filter forces the canvas into a render surface and off the overlay path). Keep it a fixed, untransformed, full-viewport sibling with nothing above it while inking (SingleOnTop), or make it opaque (`alpha:false`) if content must overlap it (Underlay).
- [medium] In single-buffer mode the display controller reads the same buffer the page is writing, so a clear-then-repaint of the live stroke can be scanned out in its cleared state; Chrome documents this flicker for WebGL, and Chromium's 2D/WebGL low-latency code acknowledges the display may read partial frames. - Chrome blog: 'Some browsers including Chrome clear WebGL canvases between frames. It's possible for the display controller to read the buffer while it's empty causing the image being drawn to flicker. To avoid this set preserveDrawingBuffer to true ... If you must clear, draw to an offscreen framebuffer then copy that to the screen.' drawing_buffer.cc (low-latency export): 'Restart SharedImage access on the back buffer to ensure a write fence is generated on it to guarantee display reads this frame completely. Display may still read parts of subsequent frames, which is okay.' The WHATWG spec: 'this mode ... might introduce visible tearing artifacts' and describes front buffer rendering as reducing latency 'at the price of potentially introducing tearing artifacts'. The 2D single-buffer provider is created with `SHARED_IMAGE_USAGE_CONCURRENT_READ_WRITE` and `CHECK(!IsSingleBuffered() || !resource->IsInitialized())` — one resource, reused every frame. (https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/drawing_buffer.cc; https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/canvas.html#canvasrenderingcontext2dsettings; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc) => This is the most plausible mechanism for complaint (1) 'live preview sometimes missing entirely': the current code clears and repaints the whole stroke on a desynchronized canvas. The 2.0 wet layer must never be in a cleared state on-screen — either draw append-only, or compose the stroke off-screen and blit only the changed rectangle (a drawImage replaces pixels; it never leaves them empty).
- [high] With a desynchronized canvas present, Blink stops rAF-aligning mouse/touch move events on the main thread and dispatches them as they arrive; `pointerrawupdate` events are a separate type that is always pulled ahead of rAF-aligned events. - main_thread_event_queue.cc: `bool MainThreadEventQueue::IsRafAlignedEvent(...) { switch (type) { case kMouseMove: case kMouseWheel: case kTouchMove: return allow_raf_aligned_input_ && !needs_low_latency_.load() && !needs_low_latency_until_pointer_up_.load() && !needs_unbuffered_input_for_debugger_.load(); ...` and `IsRawUpdateEvent` returns true for `WebInputEvent::Type::kPointerRawUpdate`, which `DispatchEvents` removes from the queue ahead of rAF-aligned items ('Do not pass a non-rAF-aligned event to avoid delivering raw move events and down/up events out of order to js'). chrome_client_impl.cc `SetNeedsLowLatencyInput(frame, needs_low_latency)` → web_frame_widget_impl.cc `...input_event_queue()->SetNeedsLowLatency(needs_low_latency)`. html_canvas_element.cc calls `SetNeedsUnbufferedInputEvents(true)` when the desynchronized context is created (the Element→ChromeClient hop was not read; inferred). MDN: pointerrawupdate 'events are dispatched as soon and as frequently as the browser can produce them', is secure-context only, and 'listening to pointerrawupdate events can affect performance'. BCD: getCoalescedEvents Chrome/Android/WebView 58, Safari 18.2; getPredictedEvents Chrome/Android/WebView 77, Firefox 89, Safari 18.2. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/page/chrome_client_impl.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/frame/web_frame_widget_impl.cc; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/pointerrawupdate_event; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/PointerEvent.json) => Explains why merging `pointerrawupdate` with `pointermove.getCoalescedEvents()` by timestamp is fragile: once the desynchronized canvas exists, pointermove itself is unbuffered and the two streams interleave. Use exactly one stream per stroke (pointerrawupdate + its own getCoalescedEvents when available; else pointermove + getCoalescedEvents), and treat the sample sequence, not timestamps, as identity.
- [high] Platform matrix for `desynchronized`: Chrome/Android/WebView accept it since 75 (full single-buffer semantics only on ChromeOS 75+, Windows 81+ via DXGI swap chain, Android via SurfaceControl); macOS is a documented no-op; Firefox has never implemented it; Safari accepts it since 15 with unverified effect. - BCD HTMLCanvasElement.json (getContext desynchronized): Chrome '81, notes: ChromeOS and Windows' (75–81 partial on ChromeOS), Chrome Android 75, WebView/Samsung mirror, Firefox false, Safari 15. canvas_utils.cc: 'crbug.com/41435781 would need to be resolved in order to support low-latency usage on Mac (currently setting the desynchronized attribute on a canvas is a no-op on Mac)'; Windows: 'Low-latency usages are supported on Windows if it's possible to back SharedImages by the D3D swapchain'; ChromeOS: 'Low-latency usage is always supported for Canvas2D on ChromeOS.' The graphics-dev thread on Mac: the mode 'uses the same SharedImage to update the Canvas content each time' and 'IOSurfaceImageBacking backend... doesn't support concurrent read/write under Metal'. Chromium Blog Chrome 75 Beta announced the feature. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/HTMLCanvasElement.json; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/canvas_utils.cc; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/graphics-dev/c/20qDm3ZD2f8; https://blog.chromium.org/2019/05/chrome-75-beta-low-latency-canvas.html) => The same wet-canvas code path gives the best result on the Samsung tablet (Chrome) and Windows; on Mac/Firefox/Safari it degrades gracefully to the normal composited path. Do not build a separate pipeline per platform.
- [high] Blink's 2D canvas is a paint-op recorder rasterised in the GPU process (OOP-R); recorded ops are auto-flushed mid-frame once they exceed 2 MB (Ganesh) or 6 MB (Graphite), or 32 MB of pinned image bytes — thresholds tuned for MotionMark, not inking. - canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc: 'The following parameters attempt to reach a compromise between not flushing too often, and not accumulating an unreasonable backlog. Flushing too often will hurt performance due to overhead costs. Accumulating large backlogs, in the case of OOPR-Canvas, results in poor parallelism and janky UI... They were selected to maximize the MotionMark score on desktop computers.' `kMaxRecordedOpKB(..., "max_recorded_op_kb", 2 * 1024)`, `kMaxPinnedImageKB(..., 32 * 1024)`, `kMaxRecordedOpGraphiteKB(..., 6 * 1024)` ('Graphite can generally handle more ops'). canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc `FlushIfRecordingLimitExceeded()`: `if (recorder->ReleasableOpBytesUsed() > max_recorded_op_bytes() || recorder->ReleasableImageBytesUsed() > max_pinned_image_bytes()) FlushCanvas(FlushReason::kOther);` called from every `GetOrCreatePaintCanvas()`. kSkiaGraphite is `FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT` except Apple in current main (field trials may differ). (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/gpu/config/gpu_finch_features.cc) => Per-frame JS draw calls are cheap to record but every byte is serialised to the GPU process each frame. A perfect-freehand outline of a ~500-sample stroke is on the order of 1,000 outline points ≈ 16 KB of SkPath per fill (inferred), so redrawing ~100+ committed strokes as paths per frame hits the 2 MB mid-frame flush. Committed ink must not be re-filled as paths every frame.
- [high] Path2D reuse does not, by default, avoid re-serialising the path to the GPU process on every fill: the Path2D paint cache is a disabled-by-default feature, and immediate-mode `fill()`/`stroke()` are never cached. - blink features.cc: '// Enables the use of the PaintCache for Path2D objects that are rasterized out of process. Has no effect when kCanvasOopRasterization is disabled. BASE_FEATURE(kPath2DPaintCache, base::FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT);'. canvas_2d_recorder_context.cc: `path2d_use_paint_cache_(base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(features::kPath2DPaintCache) ? UsePaintCache::kEnabled : UsePaintCache::kDisabled)`; `FillPathImpl(Path2D*)` → `DrawPathInternal(*dom_path, ..., path2d_use_paint_cache_)`, while `fill()`/`stroke()` on the current path pass `UsePaintCache::kDisabled`. paint_op_writer.cc `Write(const SkPath& path, UsePaintCache use_paint_cache)`: `auto id = path.getGenerationID(); ... if (use_paint_cache == kEnabled && options_.paint_cache->Get(kPath, id)) { Write(kCached); return; }` else the path bytes are inlined. paint_cache.h: 'PaintCache is used to cache high frequency small paint data types, like SkTextBlob and SkPath in the GPU service.' (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/common/features.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/canvas_2d_recorder_context.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/cc/paint/paint_op_writer.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/cc/paint/paint_cache.h) => Path2D still saves JS-side path construction and is the right object to build once per stroke, but it is not a rendering cache. Caching must happen at the bitmap level (tiles/ImageBitmaps) to make per-frame cost independent of stroke count.
- [high] Skia Ganesh (Chrome's GPU raster backend) chooses a path renderer per draw; anti-aliased concave fills are triangulated on the CPU inside the GPU process without cross-frame caching, and any path above 16,384 verbs is refused by the GPU path renderers and falls to the software path renderer. - PathRenderer.h: '// Used to guard TriangulatingPathRenderer and DefaultPathRenderer from excessively complex paths, which may cause OOM or other stalls. static constexpr int kMaxGPUPathRendererVerbs = 1 << 14;'. TriangulatingPathRenderer.cpp `onCanDrawPath`: 'It can do convex and concave paths, but we'll leave the convex ones to simpler algorithms'; `if (verbCount > kMaxGPUPathRendererVerbs) return CanDrawPath::kNo;`; for non-AA/MSAA: 'the real advantage of this path renderer comes from caching the tessellated geometry' (keyed via `GrThreadSafeCache`, invalidated 'When the SkPathRef genID changes'); the cached mesh path asserts `SkASSERT(!fAntiAlias)`, i.e. coverage-AA fills are re-triangulated each draw. AtlasPathRenderer.cpp: `constexpr static int kAtlasMaxPathWidth = 1024;` and `pathFitsInAtlas` requires the device-space bounds' largest dimension ≤ atlas max path width and area ≤ atlasMaxPathHeight². PathRendererChain.cpp order: DashLine, AAConvex, AAHairline, AALinearizing, Atlas, Small, Triangulating, Tessellation, then 'We always include the default path renderer (as well as SW), so we can draw any path'. SurfaceDrawContext.cpp: 'falling back to' `getSoftwarePathRenderer()` when no GPU renderer accepts. (https://github.com/google/skia/blob/main/src/gpu/ganesh/PathRenderer.h; https://github.com/google/skia/blob/main/src/gpu/ganesh/ops/TriangulatingPathRenderer.cpp; https://github.com/google/skia/blob/main/src/gpu/ganesh/ops/AtlasPathRenderer.cpp; https://github.com/google/skia/blob/main/src/gpu/ganesh/PathRendererChain.cpp; https://github.com/google/skia/blob/main/src/gpu/ganesh/SurfaceDrawContext.cpp) => Facts are established; the mapping to strokes is inferred: a perfect-freehand outline emits one quadratic verb per outline point (~2 per input sample), so a single very long stroke (>~8,000 samples) or any Path2D that concatenates several strokes can cross 16,384 verbs and drop to software rasterisation. Never merge strokes into one Path2D; keep one Path2D per stroke and rasterise committed ink once into bitmaps.
- [medium] A full-canvas `clearRect` is recorded as an overdraw op that lets Blink discard earlier recorded ops for the frame; on tile-based mobile GPUs a start-of-pass clear is nearly free, whereas mid-pass clears and load/store round-trips of large attachments cost bandwidth proportional to pixel count. - canvas_2d_recorder_context.cc `clearRect`: `if (RectContainsTransformedRect(rect, clip_bounds)) { CheckOverdraw(&clear_flags, kNoImage, OverdrawOp::kClearRect); ... c->drawRect(...clear_flags); } else { ... ComputeDirtyRect ... }`. memory_managed_paint_recorder.h: 'Drop all the draw ops recorded in the current layer level. This is used when we are about to draw over the whole canvas or layer, masking away all previously recorded paint ops at that level.' Vulkan TBR best-practices guide: 'If you are starting a new frame or a fresh pass, use VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_CLEAR. This is significantly faster than using explicit clear commands like vkCmdClearAttachments' and 'use VK_ATTACHMENT_STORE_OP_DONT_CARE for any attachment you don't need after the pass'; bandwidth between tile memory and main memory is the primary bottleneck; 'Multisampling is particularly efficient on tilers because the resolve ... can often happen entirely on-chip.' (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/canvas_2d_recorder_context.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/memory_managed_paint_recorder.h; https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/tile_based_rendering_best_practices.html) => Whether Skia turns a given clear into a load-op clear is inferred, not verified. The safe cost model: every full-viewport canvas you touch per frame costs at least one 4.1 Mpx (Tab S9 @ DPR 2) write plus the compositor read of 16.4 MB; clearing per-frame a small dirty rectangle is far cheaper than clearing the whole stage, and the wet layer should touch only its dirty rect.
- [medium] Quantitative buffer sizes for the target device: Samsung Galaxy Tab S9 exposes an 800×1280 CSS viewport at devicePixelRatio 2, so a full-viewport canvas is 1600×2560 = 4,096,000 device pixels ≈ 16.4 MB RGBA8 per buffer; a normal (non-single-buffered) accelerated canvas can hold up to 3 recycled resources plus the one in flight. - 1440px.com device sheet: 'The Samsung Galaxy Tab S9 has a CSS viewport of 800 × 1280 px, native resolution of 1600 × 2560, and a 2× pixel ratio'; Tab S9+ 876×1400 CSS, Tab S9 Ultra 924×1480 CSS (all DPR 2). canvas_2d_resource_provider.h: '// The maximum number of in-flight resources waiting to be used for recycling. static constexpr int kMaxRecycledCanvasResources = 3;' and `kUnusedResourceExpirationTime = base::Seconds(5)`; the pool is created with `is_single_buffered ? 0 : kMaxRecycledCanvasResources`. (https://1440px.com/screen-sizes/samsung-galaxy-tab-s9/; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_2d_resource_provider.h; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_2d_resource_provider.cc) => The current 'dpr up to 2.5' cap does nothing on the tablet (DPR is 2) and only matters on DPR-3 phones (saves ~31% pixels). Budget: three full-viewport canvases (static ink, wet ink, interactive) ≈ 50 MB of visible buffers plus up to ~3× that in recycled resources for the non-single-buffered ones — acceptable, but do not add more full-viewport canvases, and keep tile caches bounded (e.g. ≤ 2× viewport worth of tiles).
- [high] Hard canvas size limits in Chromium are 268,435,456 pixels of area (32768×8192) and 65,535 per dimension; exceeding them loses the context.
