Measured Android limits are lower on older OS versions (16,384² on Android 8–11, 14,188² on Android 7, 11,180² on Android 5). - canvas_rendering_context_host.cc: `static constexpr int kMaxCanvasArea = 32768 * 8192;` and `static constexpr int kMaxSkiaDim = 65535;` used by `IsValidImageSize()`; canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc: `if (!canvas()->IsValidImageSize())` triggers context loss with `kInvalidCanvasSize`. canvas-size test results: 'Chrome 91+ (Android 8 - 11) | 65,535 | 65,535 | 16,384 x 16,384 (268,435,456)'; 'Chrome 91+ (Android 7) | 65,535 | 65,535 | 14,188 x 14,188 (201,299,344)'; 'Chrome 91+ (Android 5) | ... 11,180 x 11,180 (124,992,400)'; 'Mobile Safari 9+ | 4,194,303 | 8,388,607 | 4,096 x 4,096 (16,777,216)'; 'Firefox 122+ | 32,767 | 32,767 | 23,168 x 23,168'. Excalidraw's per-element cache guard: 'const AREA_LIMIT = 16777216; // ~ safari mobile canvas area limit' and 'const WIDTH_HEIGHT_LIMIT = 32767;' with rescaling to fit. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/canvas_rendering_context_host.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/canvas_rendering_context_2d.cc; https://github.com/jhildenbiddle/canvas-size/blob/master/docs/index.md; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/element/src/renderElement.ts) => A single world-sized canvas is impossible for an infinite canvas; committed ink must be tiled. Keep every cache canvas ≤ 16,777,216 px area and ≤ 4,096 px per side if Safari/iOS support matters; 512–1024 px tiles are comfortably inside every limit and small enough for the ImageBitmap/Worker path.
- [high] Readback heuristics: an accelerated 2D canvas without `willReadFrequently` falls back to CPU raster after 2 getImageData calls (with a console warning), except desynchronized canvases which never fall back; `willReadFrequently:true` forces CPU raster from creation, which in turn disables the low-latency path. - base_rendering_context_2d.h: `static constexpr unsigned kFallbackToCPUAfterReadbacks = 2;`. base_rendering_context_2d.cc (getImageData): warning text 'Canvas2D: Multiple readback operations using getImageData are faster with the willReadFrequently attribute set to true'; '// GetImageData is faster in Unaccelerated canvases. // In Desynchronized canvas disabling the acceleration will break putImageData: crbug.com/1112060. if (IsAccelerated() && !IsDesynchronized()) { read_count_++; if (read_count_ >= kFallbackToCPUAfterReadbacks || ShouldDisableAccelerationBecauseOfReadback()) DisableAcceleration(); }'. html_canvas_element.cc: `RasterModeHint hint = ShouldAccelerate() && !will_read_frequently ? RasterModeHint::kPreferGPU : RasterModeHint::kPreferCPU;` plus a document-wide heuristic (`kDisableAccelerationThreshold = 100`, `kDisableAccelerationPercent = 95`: once 100+ canvases exist and 95% are unaccelerated, new ones start unaccelerated). canvas_utils.cc: `if (raster_mode == RasterMode::kCPU) return false;` for low-latency support. BCD: willReadFrequently Chrome 99, Firefox 28, Safari 18. Chrome blog: 'The new API gives you a way of explicitly marking a canvas for reading back'. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/base_rendering_context_2d.h; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/canvas/canvas2d/base_rendering_context_2d.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/gpu/canvas_utils.cc; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/HTMLCanvasElement.json; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/canvas2d) => Never call getImageData/toDataURL/toBlob on the wet or static-ink canvases (thumbnail/export code is a classic offender). If pixel readback is needed (e.g. exports), render into a separate OffscreenCanvas created with `willReadFrequently:true`.
- [high] OffscreenCanvas with `transferControlToOffscreen()` and worker-side `requestAnimationFrame` is universally available on the target platforms: Chrome/Chrome Android/WebView/Samsung Internet 69+, Firefox 105+, Safari 16.4+ (2d and bitmaprenderer; WebGL from Safari 17). The `desynchronized` attribute has no code path for OffscreenCanvas in Chromium. - BCD OffscreenCanvas.json: interface and getContext version_added Chrome 69 (Android/WebView/Samsung mirror), Firefox 105, Safari 16.4 ('Supports 2d, bitmaprenderer; webgl/webgl2 added in 17'). BCD HTMLCanvasElement.json transferControlToOffscreen: Chrome 69, Firefox 105, Safari 16.4. MDN: transferControlToOffscreen throws InvalidStateError if getContext was already called or control already transferred. web.dev: 'Operations applied to OffscreenCanvas will be rendered on the source canvas automatically'; 'the busy main thread does not influence the animation running on a worker'; caution: 'Examples in this video use the deprecated commit() call. Use worker.requestAnimationFrame instead.' Chromium offscreen_canvas.cc contains no reference to desynchronized/LowLatency (grep of the fetched file), and the direct-dispatch code lives only in HTMLCanvasElement::PostFinalizeFrame; canvas_resource_dispatcher.h caps OffscreenCanvas in-flight frames at `kMaxPendingCompositorFrames = 2`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/OffscreenCanvas.json; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/HTMLCanvasElement.json; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLCanvasElement/transferControlToOffscreen; https://web.dev/articles/offscreen-canvas; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/offscreencanvas/offscreen_canvas.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/canvas_resource_dispatcher.h) => Workers are fine for the committed-ink tile rasteriser (and for lasso hit-testing), but the wet stroke must stay on a main-thread HTMLCanvasElement to get the desynchronized/unbuffered path; a worker-driven wet canvas would add the worker hop and lose the low-latency mode (inferred from the absence of an OffscreenCanvas code path).
- [high] ImageBitmap is the right transport for tiles: `createImageBitmap`/`transferToImageBitmap` produce GPU-friendly bitmaps, `ImageBitmapRenderingContext.transferFromImageBitmap()` transfers ownership without a copy, and `drawImage` accepts ImageBitmap as a source; all are available in workers and baseline since 2020. - MDN: 'The ownership of the ImageBitmap is transferred to the canvas as well'; 'If the value is null, the canvas is reset to blank'; 'Baseline Widely available ... since January 2020'; 'This feature is available in Web Workers.' WHATWG canvas: 'DrawImage supports CanvasImageSource which includes ImageBitmap objects as valid image sources'. MDN canvas optimisation: 'Pre-render similar primitives or repeating objects on an offscreen canvas'; 'Avoid floating-point coordinates and use integers instead – Sub-pixel rendering forces the browser to do extra calculations for anti-aliasing'; 'Don't scale images in drawImage'; 'Use multiple layered canvases for complex scenes'; 'Render screen differences only, not the whole new state'. (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/ImageBitmapRenderingContext/transferFromImageBitmap; https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/canvas.html; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Canvas_API/Tutorial/Optimizing_canvas) => Tiles rasterised in a Worker should be handed to the main thread as ImageBitmaps (transferable, no copy) and blitted with `drawImage(tile, integerX, integerY)` at 1:1 device scale onto the static-ink canvas; scaled blits are only for the transient pinch-zoom state.
- [high] Excalidraw's shipped architecture is the closest open-source reference for the target: three stacked full-viewport canvases (static scene, interactive/selection UI, new element) plus a per-element bitmap cache; the in-progress element is redrawn on its own canvas per animation frame, and only visible elements are painted. - NewElementCanvas.tsx renders `<canvas className="excalidraw__canvas" width={appState.width * props.scale} height={appState.height * props.scale}>` and calls `renderNewElementScene(..., isRenderThrottlingEnabled())`; renderNewElementScene.ts does `bootstrapCanvas` (clear), `context.scale(appState.zoom.value, ...)`, then `renderElement(newElement, ...)`, wrapped in `throttleRAF`. staticScene.ts paints `visibleElements.forEach(...)` only. renderElement.ts: `export const elementWithCanvasCache = new WeakMap<ExcalidrawElement, ExcalidrawElementWithCanvas>()`; `generateElementCanvas` creates a canvas of `cappedElementCanvasSize` where `width = elementWidth * window.devicePixelRatio + padding * 2` (freedraw padding `element.strokeWidth * 12`), `scale = zoom.value`, then `context.translate(padding * scale, padding * scale); context.scale(window.devicePixelRatio * scale, window.devicePixelRatio * scale)`. The cache is regenerated when `prevElementWithCanvas.zoomValue !== zoom.value && !appState?.shouldCacheIgnoreZoom` or theme/crop/frame-opacity change; `ShapeCache.generateElementShape` calls `elementWithCanvasCache.delete(element)` whenever the geometry is regenerated. `drawElementFromCanvas` does `context.scale(1 / dpr, 1 / dpr)` then `context.drawImage(elementWithCanvas.canvas, (x1 + scrollX) * dpr - padding..., ..., canvas.width / scale, canvas.height / scale)` (non-integer positions accepted). helpers.ts: 'An opaque fill repaints every pixel, so clearRect would be redundant.' (https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/excalidraw/components/canvases/NewElementCanvas.tsx; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/renderNewElementScene.ts; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/staticScene.ts; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/element/src/renderElement.ts; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/helpers.ts) => Validates the layer split (committed / selection / wet) and the bitmap-cache idea. Excalidraw's per-element cache still costs one drawImage per visible element per frame and re-rasterises on every zoom change; for 'cost independent of page content' a tile cache (fixed count of blits) is the step beyond Excalidraw. Excalidraw also does not use `desynchronized` and clears its wet canvas every frame — fine for double-buffered canvases, not for a single-buffered one.
- [high] Excalidraw renders freehand ink with perfect-freehand using fixed options and `last: true` for both the in-progress and the committed stroke, converting the outline to an SVG path string and filling it via `context.fill(new Path2D(shape))`; the geometry is cached per element in a WeakMap. - shape.ts: `import { getStroke, getStrokePoints } from "perfect-freehand"`; `VARIABLE_WIDTH_FREEDRAW = { SIZE_FACTOR: 4.25, THINNING: 0.6, SMOOTHING: 0.5 }` ('tuned empirically by visually comparing rendered strokes'); `getStroke(inputPoints, { simulatePressure: element.simulatePressure, size: element.strokeWidth * 4.25, thinning: 0.6, smoothing: 0.5, streamline: getFreedrawStreamline(element), easing: (t) => Math.sin((t * Math.PI) / 2), last: true })`; `getSvgPathFromStroke` builds `M p0 Q ... ` with midpoints and closes with `L arr[0] Z`; `// NOTE not cached (-> for SVG export)` for the raw path builder, while `ShapeCache.cache = new WeakMap<ExcalidrawElement, { shape, theme }>()` caches the generated shape per element. renderElement.ts freedraw case: 'Draw directly to canvas ... context.fill(new Path2D(shape))'. perfect-freehand README: `last` (default true) means 'the line's end will be drawn at the last input point, rather than slightly behind it'; the outline is rendered as quadratic Béziers between averaged points, and 'For Canvas rendering, pass the resulting SVG path string to the Path2D constructor'. (https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/element/src/shape.ts; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/blob/master/packages/element/src/renderElement.ts; https://github.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand/blob/main/README.md) => Pixel-identical wet vs committed output is achievable by construction: same input samples, same getStroke options (including `last` and `streamline`, which change geometry), same path builder, same fill rule/colour, and the same device-space transform. Any difference in transform sub-pixel phase or a scaled blit will change anti-aliased edge pixels (inferred from the AA behaviour MDN describes).
- [medium] tldraw takes the opposite approach for committed ink — one SVG DOM element per draw shape, with perfect-freehand geometry, a simplified 'solid' path variant, single-point dots, and an `isComplete` flag that changes rendering when the stroke finishes. - DrawShapeUtil.tsx (as fetched): returns JSX inside `<SVGContainer>`; `const showAsComplete = shape.props.isComplete || last(shape.props.segments)?.type === 'straight'`; dash-style 'draw' uses `svgInk()` with `strokeLinecap="round"`, other styles use `getSvgPathFromStrokePoints()`; `const isDot = strokePoints.length < 2` draws a circle; values like `forceSolid` are memoised on `[editor, sw, zoomOverride]`. (https://github.com/tldraw/tldraw/blob/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/draw/DrawShapeUtil.tsx) => This is essentially Notes Gallery's current architecture (DOM/SVG per stroke). It is workable for tldraw because it relies on React memoisation and viewport culling, but it is exactly the model whose per-frame cost (style recalc, layout, compositing of thousands of layers, class toggling on selection) grows with page content — the source of complaint (3). The Excalidraw/tile model is the one that meets the 2.0 goal.
- [medium] The Ink API (delegated ink trails) is available in Chromium 94+ including Chrome Android and WebView per BCD, and viz contains a Skia-based renderer that draws the predicted trail in the display compositor; whether the trail is actually presented on Android is not verified. - BCD Ink.json: Ink and requestPresenter version_added Chrome 94 (chrome_android/webview mirror), Edge 93, Firefox/Safari false. MDN: 'The compositor draws the ink trail ahead of the app's own rendering until the next pointer event arrives'; example `navigator.ink.requestPresenter({ presentationArea: canvas })` then `(await presenter).updateInkTrailStartPoint(evt, style)` in pointermove. Chromium delegated_ink_point_renderer_skia.h: 'This class handles drawing the delegated ink trail when the Skia renderer is in use by filtering everything out with timestamps before the metadata, predicting another point or two, and drawing the points with bezier curves between them with Skia commands onto the canvas provided by the Skia renderer'; base class: 'used for rendering delegated ink trails on the end of strokes to reduce user perceived latency'. The graphics-dev thread notes the Windows OS compositor variant and Ken Russell's remark that front-buffer rendering 'provided a good speed boost on ChromeOS'. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/main/api/Ink.json; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Ink_API; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/components/viz/service/display/delegated_ink_point_renderer_skia.h; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/graphics-dev/c/cErEIr4NECg/m/qgidUJHvAwAJ) => Cheap to adopt opportunistically: call `updateInkTrailStartPoint` from the pointer handler with the current brush colour/diameter; when honoured, the compositor draws the last few millimetres of the stroke ahead of your canvas. Falls back silently where unsupported. Test on the Samsung tablet before relying on it.
- [medium] Canvas GPU memory is not budgeted per page in Blink; the mitigations that exist are canvas hibernation (currently disabled by default) and per-document acceleration heuristics, so tile-cache memory must be bounded by the app itself. - blink features.cc: '// Temporarily disabled due to issues: - PDF blank previews - Canvas corruption on ARM64 macOS ... BASE_FEATURE(kCanvas2DHibernation, FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT)'; hibernation, when on, snapshots hidden accelerated canvases to CPU (`Height() * Width() < 128 * 128` excluded when `kCanvas2DHibernationNoSmallCanvas`). html_canvas_element.cc: `constexpr int kMinimumAccelerated2dCanvasSize = 128 * 129;` (only relevant when `AcceleratedSmallCanvases` is off; runtime_enabled_features.json5 marks it `status: "stable"`), and the 100-canvas / 95% heuristic. Excalidraw bounds its per-element canvases only by the 16,777,216 px area cap. No Blink constant for total accelerated canvas bytes was found in the fetched sources. (https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/common/features.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc; https://github.com/chromium/chromium/blob/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/runtime_enabled_features.json5) => Keep an explicit tile LRU (e.g. cap at ~2–3 viewports of tiles ≈ 100–150 MB at DPR 2) and release ImageBitmaps with `.close()`; listen for `contextlost`/`contextrestored` (Chrome blog) and rebuild tiles from stroke data, which is cheap because the data model, not the bitmaps, is the source of truth.
- [high] Spec and Chrome guidance agree that `desynchronized` is a hint the UA may ignore and that context attributes are fixed at the first `getContext()` call. - WHATWG: 'the user agent may optimize the rendering of the canvas to reduce latency, as measured from input events to rasterization, by desynchronizing the canvas paint cycle from the event loop, bypassing the ordinary user agent rendering algorithm, or both.' Intent to Ship: 'The |desynchronized| attribute is a hint to the UA to configure itself in a certain mode, but the UA is not forced to honour it in any way.' Chrome blog: 'Context attributes cannot be changed after the first getContext() call—subsequent calls return the same object with original settings' and 'devices with front buffer rendering such as ChromeOS for example may have tearing.' (https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/canvas.html#canvasrenderingcontext2dsettings; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/nxjWgMIeC1Q/m/GfwjbzeVAwAJ; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized) => Create the wet canvas element once with the final attribute set (`{desynchronized:true, alpha:true}`) and never re-create it mid-session; resizing keeps the context. Tearing on a wet stroke is acceptable because only the newest segment is ever in flight.

Recommendations:
- Wet (live) stroke layer: one dedicated full-viewport <canvas> for in-progress ink, a sibling of #world (never inside the CSS-transformed world), position:fixed at 0,0, sized to viewport*devicePixelRatio (integers; drop the 2.5 cap logic, it is a no-op on the Tab S9 at DPR 2), no CSS transform/opacity/filter/mix-blend-mode/border-radius/clip-path on it or any ancestor, topmost in z-order while a stroke is active (temporarily hide or pointer-events:none any floating toolbars that overlap it). Create the context once with getContext('2d', {desynchronized:true, alpha:true}); never pass willReadFrequently and never call getImageData/toDataURL on it. Map world->screen with ctx.setTransform per draw, not with CSS.
- Never clear-and-repaint the wet stroke on the desynchronized canvas. Maintain an off-screen stroke accumulator (a plain OffscreenCanvas/HTMLCanvas in device pixels covering the stroke's bounding box + brush radius). On each pointer batch: append the new samples, recompute the perfect-freehand outline (or the tail region only), clearRect+fill inside the accumulator, then drawImage the accumulator's dirty rectangle onto the desynchronized canvas at integer device coordinates. The on-screen buffer therefore only ever receives finished pixels (no visible cleared state under single-buffer scanout) and per-event cost is bounded by the dirty rect, not the stroke length. For opaque brushes an even cheaper append-only variant (fill only the new outline slice) is acceptable; keep the accumulator path for translucent brushes to avoid overlap seams.
- Drive wet drawing from the pointer events themselves, not requestAnimationFrame: with a desynchronized canvas Chromium delivers move events unbuffered and submits a compositor frame at the end of the task that drew, so painting in the handler is the lowest-latency path. Use exactly one input stream per stroke — pointerrawupdate + event.getCoalescedEvents() when the event type exists (secure context), else pointermove + getCoalescedEvents() — and never merge the two streams by timestamp; process the samples in order and de-dup only exact repeats of the previous sample. Coalesce all samples of one task into one outline update.
- Hide the remaining latency with prediction: draw a short predicted tail from event.getPredictedEvents() into the accumulator (so it is replaced by real ink on the next blit), and opportunistically call navigator.ink.requestPresenter({presentationArea: wetCanvas}) then presenter.updateInkTrailStartPoint(evt, {color, diameter}) in the handler when navigator.ink exists; measure on the Samsung tablet whether the delegated trail appears and disable it if it double-draws.
- Committed ink: no DOM element per stroke. Keep strokes as data (points, pressures, style, bbox, r-tree/grid index). Rasterise them into world-space tiles (512x512 device px, keyed by tileX, tileY, zoomBucket where zoomBucket steps by sqrt(2)) in a Web Worker via OffscreenCanvas, return ImageBitmaps (transferToImageBitmap / createImageBitmap, transferable) and blit visible tiles onto a full-viewport 'static ink' canvas with drawImage at integer device offsets. Per-frame cost = number of visible tiles (about 20-35 at DPR 2 on a 1600x2560 screen), independent of stroke count. During pinch-zoom, draw the existing bucket's tiles scaled (drawImage with scale) and regenerate at the new bucket after the gesture ends (Excalidraw's shouldCacheIgnoreZoom pattern). Bound the tile LRU (e.g. 2-3 viewports of tiles) and call ImageBitmap.close() on eviction; rebuild from data on contextlost/contextrestored.
- Commit path and pixel identity: on pointerup, hand the final sample list to the tile rasteriser and only remove the stroke from the wet canvas when the affected tiles have been re-blitted (swap in the same frame). Use one shared renderStroke(ctx, stroke, transform) function for wet and committed ink with identical perfect-freehand options (size, thinning, smoothing, streamline, easing, simulatePressure and last:true), identical fill rule and colour, and an identical device-space transform including sub-pixel phase: render the wet accumulator in the same tile raster space as the current zoom bucket (integer device-pixel origin) so its pixels are what the tile will contain. Keep one Path2D per stroke; never concatenate strokes into a single path (Skia refuses GPU rendering above 16,384 verbs).
- Selection, move and lasso: keep selection state in the data model and render highlights/handles on a third full-viewport 'interactive' canvas, cleared and redrawn per frame (this canvas is double-buffered, so clearing is fine). For a drag of many selected strokes, rasterise the selected subset once into an ImageBitmap, mark their tiles dirty and re-render those tiles without them, then each frame draw the bitmap translated by the drag delta (one drawImage); re-rasterise into tiles on drop. Lasso hit-testing runs against the stroke index (bbox prefilter, then point-in-polygon on simplified points) in the Worker; no DOM measurement. This makes complaint (3) a constant-cost operation.
- Avoid per-frame path re-filling anywhere: Blink serialises every draw op to the GPU process each frame (2 MB auto-flush threshold), the Path2D paint cache is off by default, and Skia re-triangulates anti-aliased concave fills every draw. Paths are for rasterising into bitmaps once; bitmaps (tiles/ImageBitmaps) are for per-frame display.
- Clearing and DPR hygiene: on the wet accumulator and interactive canvas, clear only dirty rectangles; on the static-ink canvas, redraw only when the viewport/zoom changed or a tile was invalidated (otherwise skip the frame entirely). Do not scale canvases with CSS; do not use shadowBlur on ink; round all blit coordinates to integers; keep canvas backing sizes at exactly viewport*DPR so the compositor samples 1:1.
- Verification plan on the Samsung tablet (Chrome and the WebView build): record a chrome://tracing session while inking and look for CanvasResourceDispatcher::DispatchFrame and overlay-strategy events to confirm the desynchronized/overlay path; measure pen-to-pixel latency with a 240 fps camera before/after; check chrome://gpu for 'Canvas: Hardware accelerated' and SurfaceControl status. Treat getContextAttributes().desynchronized === true as meaningless for engagement.
- Autosave (adjacent to this topic): move to append-only per-stroke records with periodic compaction so committing a stroke never re-serialises the whole workspace on the main thread; large JSON writes during inking would otherwise stall the very tasks that must draw the wet stroke.
Open questions:
- Does the single-buffer (SurfaceControl overlay) path actually engage on the user's Samsung tablet in Chrome, and is it definitively unavailable in the Android WebView build? No web API exposes this; it must be verified with chrome://tracing / about:gpu on the device.
- Is there a documented Chromium bug for blank or flickering desynchronized 2D canvases on Android/Samsung? None was found in public trackers during this research; the blank-preview symptom is explained by single-buffer scanout of a cleared buffer (inferred) and should be confirmed by switching to the off-screen-accumulator blit and observing whether it disappears.
- Which Skia backend the tablet's Chrome build uses (Ganesh vs Graphite is field-trial controlled; kSkiaGraphite is disabled by default outside Apple in current main), since it changes the auto-flush threshold (2 MB vs 6 MB) and path-rendering behaviour.
- Whether the delegated ink trail (navigator.ink) is presented on Android — viz has a Skia renderer for it, but Android enablement was not verified.
- Whether Samsung Internet (Chromium-based, often the tablet default) follows Chrome's defaults for kAndroidSurfaceControl and kLowLatencyUsageSupportedForCanvas.
- Safari's actual behaviour for desynchronized (BCD says accepted since 15; no evidence of a front-buffer implementation) if the web build must perform well on iPad.
- The real GPU memory headroom for canvas resources on the device (no Blink-side budget constant exists), which sets the safe tile-cache size.

## apps
Every mature inking app read here (Xournal++, Rnote, Krita/MyPaint, Windows Ink, Android's low-latency stack, Concepts, Figma) uses the same split: a "wet" in-progress stroke drawn incrementally (only the newest segment) on a small, low-latency surface, and "dry" committed strokes rasterised once into a cached bitmap (page buffer, tiles, or per-stroke images) that is merely translated/scaled during pan/zoom and re-rasterised lazily (dirty rects/tiles, off-thread) when zoom settles. None of them keep committed strokes as retained DOM/SVG nodes; the two web whiteboards that do use DOM (tldraw) or immediate-mode Canvas 2D over all visible elements (Excalidraw) both rely on an R-tree/viewport cull, per-element bitmap caches, zoom-gesture cache reuse, and are actively moving UI overlays (selection indicators, handles, brush) from SVG to a single overlay canvas because DOM overlays were 2-25x slower. Hit-testing and lasso selection are done purely geometrically: broad phase via a spatial index (rbush in tldraw, rstar in Rnote) on AABBs, narrow phase via distance-to-polyline / point-in-polygon on the stroke points, never by measuring DOM. Selected strokes being dragged are rasterised once into an offscreen bitmap and that bitmap is transformed per frame (Xournal++ EditSelectionContents, Rnote translate_strokes_images). Undo is diff/command based (tldraw RecordsDiff, Excalidraw inverse deltas, Xournal++ command objects) or Arc-shared snapshots (Rnote, cap 100). On the input side, the W3C spec and Chromium code show pointermove is rAF-aligned with the intermediate samples reachable via getCoalescedEvents(), pointerrawupdate is a separate raw copy that MAY itself be coalesced, and Chromium's Android glue keeps MotionEvent historical samples only "for tracking velocity" - the conversion sites read do not turn them into extra web samples (inferred, needs a device measurement). perfect-freehand (used by tldraw and Excalidraw) recomputes the whole outline polygon per update, low-pass filters input (t = 0.15 + (1-streamline)*0.85), inserts a 13-point cap arc at any >90 degree corner, and simulates pressure from velocity - three behaviours that plausibly explain "brush spreads across the gap" when samples are sparse. Recommendation: keep the feature set and file format, but rebuild the ink layer as (1) tiled raster cache of dry strokes + spatial index, (2) incremental wet-stroke canvas with prediction and no DOM above it, (3) single overlay canvas for selection UI, (4) bitmap-drag for moving selections, (5) diff-based undo driving tile invalidation.

Findings:
- [high] Excalidraw renders with three stacked canvases (static, interactive, new-element) and redraws all visible elements of the static canvas each frame, throttled to requestAnimationFrame with last-call-wins semantics. - staticScene.ts: `context.scale(appState.zoom.value, appState.zoom.value)`, iterates `visibleElements`, exports `renderStaticSceneThrottled` built with `throttleRAF()`. interactiveScene.ts: "Interactive scene is the ui-canvas where we render bounding boxes, selections and other ui stuff." renderNewElementScene.ts is a separate canvas that renders only `newElement` (the stroke being drawn), also `throttleRAF`. throttleRAF (packages/common/src/utils.ts) stores `lastArgs` and runs `fn(...lastArgs)` once per rAF, with `flush()`/`cancel()`. Note isRenderThrottlingEnabled() (reactUtils.ts) returns true only when `window.EXCALIDRAW_THROTTLE_RENDER === true` AND React >= 18 - throttling is opt-in. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/staticScene.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/interactiveScene.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/renderNewElementScene.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/common/src/utils.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/reactUtils.ts) => Established pattern: the in-progress stroke gets its own canvas so the committed layer is never touched while inking; one rAF-throttled draw per frame regardless of how many pointer events arrived. Notes Gallery already has the wet canvas; it lacks the throttled, separate static layer.
- [high] Excalidraw makes the per-frame static redraw cheap with a per-element offscreen-canvas cache rasterised at zoom x devicePixelRatio, and it deliberately reuses stale-zoom bitmaps during pinch/wheel zoom gestures, re-rasterising only when the gesture stops. - renderElement.ts: `elementWithCanvasCache` is a `WeakMap<ExcalidrawElement, ExcalidrawElementWithCanvas>`; canvas size = `elementWidth * window.devicePixelRatio + padding * 2`, capped by `AREA_LIMIT` 16,777,216 ("~ safari mobile canvas area limit") and `WIDTH_HEIGHT_LIMIT` 32,767; freedraw padding = `element.strokeWidth * 12`; context translated by `padding * scale` then scaled by `window.devicePixelRatio * scale`. Regeneration condition: `shouldRegenerateBecauseZoom = prevElementWithCanvas && prevElementWithCanvas.zoomValue !== zoom.value && !appState?.shouldCacheIgnoreZoom`, plus theme/crop/frame-opacity changes. PR #1103 (2020): "This reuses the cached canvas while the gesture is happening. Once it has stop updating, then recompute the cache with the proper zoom." Tested on iPhone 6: "not 60fps but is responsive, unlike prod which completely freezes the entire browser". StaticCanvas.tsx sizes the backing store `props.canvas.width = props.appState.width * props.scale` with CSS width in px; helpers.ts bootstrapCanvas does `context.setTransform(1,0,0,1,0,0)` then `context.scale(scale, scale)`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/element/src/renderElement.ts ; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/pull/1103 ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/components/canvases/StaticCanvas.tsx ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/helpers.ts) => Directly applicable recipe for high-DPI + zoom: rasterise at zoom*dpr, cap bitmap area (the 16.7M px limit matters on a 2.5-dpr tablet), pad freedraw bitmaps generously, and treat zoom-gesture frames as 'scale the old bitmap' not 're-rasterise'.
- [high] Excalidraw's own maintainers document that its viewport culling is O(n) per frame with no spatial index and that the dual static/interactive passes duplicate work; they propose quadtree indexing and dirty-region redraw. - Renderer.ts filters via `isElementInViewport` for every element, memoised on `${sceneNonce}${newElement.versionNonce}`. Issue #10063 lists: "Separate `renderStaticSceneThrottled` and `renderInteractiveSceneThrottled` calls", "O(n) viewport checking with `isElementInViewport` for every element", no spatial acceleration; proposes unified pipeline, quadtree, dirty region tracking, memory pooling, targeting "30-50% rendering performance improvement" with "1000+ elements". Blog year-three: "Some pens were firing pointer events much more frequently than needed, causing a bottleneck in rendering. So we've throttled them to not exceed the framerate of your screen (commonly 60Hz)." (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/scene/Renderer.ts ; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/issues/10063 ; https://plus.excalidraw.com/blog/year-three) => Cautionary: an immediate-mode 'draw everything visible each frame' canvas without an index or tile cache is the known weak point of Excalidraw with large drawings; Notes Gallery 2.0 should not copy that part. Also confirms the industry answer to pen event floods is to coalesce to one render per frame, not to drop samples.
- [high] tldraw renders every shape as a DOM element inside one CSS-transformed layer, and keeps pan/zoom cost bounded by culling off-screen shapes with `display:none` chosen via an rbush R-tree spatial index; position changes are applied with direct style mutation, not React re-render. - Docs: "Culled shapes stay in the DOM with `display: none`, so they cost nothing to render." "a canvas with 10,000 shapes might only render 50 if the rest are out of view." "Selected shapes and the shape being edited are never culled." package.json lists `rbush: ^3.0.1`. SpatialIndexManager.ts: items `{minX,minY,maxX,maxY,id}` of page bounds, incrementally updated from store diffs (dirty set expanded through parent/child, binding endpoints, ancestor groups), "Bulk load for efficiency" on page load, `getShapeIdsInsideBounds` "Optimized for viewport culling queries", `getShapeIdsAtPoint(point, margin)`. Release 4.4.0: "Shape queries now use an R-tree (RBush) for O(log n) lookups instead of O(n) iteration". notVisibleShapes.ts: "If canCull is the default (always-true), skip per-shape subscription entirely. >99% of shapes hit this path". Shape.tsx: "Rendering the 'inside' of a shape is more expensive than positioning it or changing its color, so we use memo to wrap the inner shape"; transform/width/height/clip-path set via `setStyleProperty` in a reactor. DefaultCanvas.tsx CullingController: "This single reactor replaces per-shape subscriptions for O(1) instead of O(N)". Options: `debouncedZoom` true with `debouncedZoomThreshold` 500 shapes, `maxShapesPerPage` 4000, `textShadowLod` 0.35. Issue #7436 admits the culling derivation still "recomputes whenever the viewport bounds change... on every frame where the camera moves" and proposes ~20% hysteresis bounds. (https://tldraw.dev/sdk-features/culling ; https://tldraw.dev/sdk-features/performance ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/package.json ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/editor/managers/SpatialIndexManager/SpatialIndexManager.ts ; https://tldraw.dev/releases/v4.4.0 ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/editor/derivations/notVisibleShapes.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/components/Shape.tsx ; https://github.com/tldraw/tldraw/issues/7436) => This is the closest architecture to Notes Gallery today (DOM per stroke in a transformed #world). tldraw makes it survive only with an R-tree cull, a hard 4000-shape page cap, LOD switches and a 500-shape zoom debounce - i.e. DOM-per-stroke is workable for hundreds of shapes, not for handwriting pages with thousands of strokes. If any DOM is kept (text, tables, cards), the rbush cull + style-mutation pattern is the way to do it.
- [high] tldraw has been migrating its interaction UI (selection indicators, and planned: brush, handles, selection box, snap lines, cursors) from per-component SVG/HTML overlays to a single 2D canvas overlay, reporting up to 25x faster rendering. - 4.4 blog/release: "Shape indicators now render using 2D canvas instead of SVG, making it much faster to select or hover over many shapes (up to 25x faster rendering in some cases)." Issue #8314 lists eight overlays still as DOM ("selection brush, scribbles, snap indicators, handles, selection outlines, selection backgrounds, collaborator hints, and cursors") and proposes to "Consolidate all overlay rendering onto the existing canvas element (or a small number of canvas layers), replacing the per-component SVG/HTML elements" to "eliminate individual DOM nodes and their CSS transforms" and "reduce paint overhead from many absolutely-positioned SVG elements"; challenges: handle hit-testing must become geometric. CanvasOverlays.tsx renders `<canvas className="tl-canvas-overlays">`, scales for DPR and camera, then `for (const { util, overlays } of editor.overlays.getActiveOverlayEntries()) { ctx.save(); util.render(ctx, overlays); ctx.restore() }`. (https://tldraw.dev/blog/tldraw-sdk-4.4 ; https://github.com/tldraw/tldraw/issues/8314 ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/components/default-components/CanvasOverlays.tsx) => Notes Gallery's lasso artefacts and select-many lag come from toggling classes and measuring DOM per stroke. The team that built the most polished DOM canvas found that even the selection chrome must move to a canvas; selection outlines for N strokes should be drawn in one canvas pass from stored geometry.
- [high] tldraw's draw tool records pen points with a per-zoom minimum spacing, consumes coalesced pointer events, and splits very long strokes into multiple shapes; its draw shape is a filled SVG path built from perfect-freehand output and switches to a cheap solid style when zoomed out. - Drawing.ts: for pen input a point is recorded only `if (Vec.Dist(currentPagePoint, this.lastRecordedPoint) >= 1 / this.zoomOnEnter)`; pressure `z * 1.25` for pen else 0.5; `mergeNextPoint` merges sub-threshold moves taking `Math.max(lastPoint.z, newPoint.z)`; new shape when `cachedPoints.length > this.util.options.maxPointsPerShape`; shape marked `isComplete: true` on pointer up; no timer throttling ("updates occur per pointer event"). useCanvasEvents.ts: "For tools that benefit from a higher fidelity of events, we dispatch the coalesced events" via `e.getCoalescedEvents()` (skipped on iOS per summary because of the secure-context requirement); pointermove listener attached to `ownerDocument.body`; no pointerrawupdate usage found. DrawShapeUtil.tsx: `forceSolid = zoomLevel < 0.5 && zoomLevel < 1.5 / sw`; geometry is `Polyline2d` of stroke points (Polygon2d if closed, Circle2d for a dot); path via `svgInk()`. getPath.ts: realPressureSettings `thinning: 0.62, streamline: 0.62`; simulatePressureSettings `thinning: 0.5, streamline: modulate(strokeWidth, [9,16], [0.64,0.74]), smoothing: 0.62`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/draw/toolStates/Drawing.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/hooks/useCanvasEvents.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/draw/DrawShapeUtil.tsx ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/draw/getPath.ts) => Establishes that a production web inking tool relies on getCoalescedEvents alone (no pointerrawupdate), applies a zoom-relative minimum point distance (not a timestamp filter), and caps points per stroke. Notes Gallery's timestamp de-dup across two event sources is not what anyone else does.
- [high] perfect-freehand computes the whole outline polygon from all input points on every call; it low-pass filters input positions (so the drawn end lags the pen unless `last` is true), skips outline points closer than (size*smoothing)^2, inserts a 13-point arc at any corner sharper than 90 degrees, and can synthesise pressure from velocity. - README: getStroke "will generate the points for a polygon based on an array of points"; "When the `last` property is `true`, the line's end will be drawn at the last input point, rather than slightly behind it." getStrokePoints.ts: `const t = MIN_STREAMLINE_T + (1 - streamline) * STREAMLINE_T_RANGE` with `MIN_STREAMLINE_T = 0.15`, `STREAMLINE_T_RANGE = 0.85`; point = `isComplete && i === max ? [pts[i][0], pts[i][1]] : lrp(prev.point, pts[i], t)`; `if (runningLength < size) continue` (initial points inside one stroke-diameter are dropped); `DEFAULT_FIRST_PRESSURE = 0.25` ("Lower than subsequent points to prevent fat starts"). getStrokeOutlinePoints.ts: `const minDistance = Math.pow(size * smoothing, 2)`; `isPointSharpCorner = prevDpr < 0 && !isPrevPointSharpCorner`; `CORNER_CAP_SEGMENTS = 13`, `START_CAP_SEGMENTS = 13`, `END_CAP_SEGMENTS = 29`, `END_NOISE_THRESHOLD = 3` px skipped at the end, `MIN_RADIUS = 0.01`, `FIXED_PI = PI + 0.0001` ("to fix browser rendering artifacts"). simulatePressure.ts: `sp = min(1, distance/size); rp = min(1, 1 - sp); return min(1, prevPressure + (rp - prevPressure) * (sp * RATE_OF_PRESSURE_CHANGE))` with `RATE_OF_PRESSURE_CHANGE = 0.275`. Excalidraw options (shape.ts): `VARIABLE_WIDTH_FREEDRAW = { SIZE_FACTOR: 4.25, THINNING: 0.6, SMOOTHING: 0.5 }`, streamline `DEFAULT_STROKE_STREAMLINE` 0.5 (0.2 'precise'), `easing: (t) => Math.sin((t * Math.PI) / 2)`, `last: true`; path built as quadratic curves through midpoints (`med()`), plus a constant-width variant `SIZE_FACTOR: 1.4`. tldraw svgInk.ts partitions the stroke at 'elbows' when the direction dot product < -0.8 ("use the extended input point as the elbow point for swooshiness in fast zaggy lines") and draws each track "as quadratic curves through the midpoints of consecutive points". (https://github.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand/main/packages/perfect-freehand/src/getStrokePoints.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand/main/packages/perfect-freehand/src/getStrokeOutlinePoints.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand/main/packages/perfect-freehand/src/constants.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/steveruizok/perfect-freehand/main/packages/perfect-freehand/src/simulatePressure.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/element/src/shape.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/shared/freehand/svgInk.ts) => Explains two of the user's symptoms if Notes Gallery uses a perfect-freehand-like outline: (a) O(n) re-outline per event grows with stroke length -> latency creeps up during long strokes; (b) when samples are sparse on a fast curve, a >90 degree turn between two far-apart samples triggers a 13-point corner cap and velocity-simulated pressure changes width abruptly -> the 'brush spreads across the gap'. With a real S-Pen, `simulatePressure` must be false; and the wet preview should be drawn incrementally rather than by recomputing the whole polygon (established that both libraries do recompute; the cost consequence is inferred).
- [high] tldraw and Excalidraw hit-test and lasso-select purely geometrically (no DOM measurement): broad phase with an AABB index or rotated-bbox early-out, narrow phase with distance-to-polyline / point-in-polygon in shape-local space, with margins expressed in screen pixels divided by zoom. - tldraw Geometry2d.ts: `if (this.isClosed && (this.isFilled || hitInside) && pointInPolygon(point, this.vertices)) return true`, else distance to edge <= margin; `distanceToPoint` is negative inside filled closed shapes; bounds/vertices lazily cached. Erasing blog: "We wrap the segment in a bounding box, pad it by the hit test margin, and ask the editor's spatial index for the shapes inside"; "the margin is defined in screen pixels, so when you're zoomed out we make it larger in page space"; endpoints moved "into the shape's own space, where the shape is axis-aligned and unscaled". Brushing.ts: `const candidateIds = editor.getShapeIdsInsideBounds(brush)`, wrap-vs-intersect via `isWrapping = isWrapMode ? !ctrlKey : ctrlKey`, four brush edges tested with `geometry.hitTestLineSegment(A, B, 0)`; comment: "On a page with ~5000 shapes, on-screen hit tests are about 2x faster than testing all shapes." Excalidraw collision.ts: "Bail out early if the point is not even in the rotated bounding box or not hitting the frame name (saves 99%)"; threshold is "page units scaled by zoom"; interior via ray casting; freedraw decomposed into segments. Excalidraw lasso/utils.ts: lasso path first `simplify(lassoPath, simplifyDistance)`, then per-element outline segments tested with `polygonIncludesPointNonZero` ("contain" = every point inside, else intersection via `intersectElementWithLineSegment`); no throttling in that module. Xournal++ Stroke.cpp: `isInSelection` requires every point inside the container; `distanceTo` projects onto each segment and subtracts `.5 * width`; `calcSize` pads the bbox by half thickness because "The size is needed to only redraw the requested part instead of redrawing the whole page (performance reason)". Rnote stroke_comp.rs: broad phase `key_tree` (rstar), narrow phase `path_polygon.contains(...)` / `intersects` on per-segment hitboxes loosened by `stroke_width * 0.5` (brushstroke.rs `gen_hitboxes_int`). (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/primitives/geometry/Geometry2d.ts ; https://tldraw.dev/blog/erasing ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/tools/SelectTool/childStates/Brushing.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/element/src/collision.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/lasso/utils.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/model/Stroke.cpp ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/store/stroke_comp.rs) => Blueprint for replacing 'toggle classes and measure DOM': store each stroke's AABB in rbush/flatbush, keep points (and optionally per-segment hitboxes) in memory, and run lasso as bbox query + point-in-polygon. Padding the AABB by half the stroke width is the established convention and also fixes the lasso-artefact class of bugs (partial repaint missing a stroke's outer edge).
- [high] Xournal++ renders each page's committed strokes into one Cairo raster buffer produced by a background render job, repaints only merged dirty rectangles, re-renders on zoom mismatch while scaling the stale buffer, and draws overlays (selection, tool views) on top in page coordinates. - PageView.cpp: buffer guarded by `drawingMutex`; `rerenderPage()` sets `rerenderComplete = true`; `rerenderRect()` accumulates and merges rectangles because "its faster to redraw only one rect than repaint twice the same area"; `elementChanged()` skips work when "the element was already painted to the buffer via a call to drawAndDeleteToolView"; `if (this->buffer.getZoom() != zoom) { rerenderPage(); }` while `cairo_scale(cr, zoom, zoom)` paints the existing buffer; then `for (const auto& v: this->overlayViews) v->draw(cr);` with the contract "The given cairo context is in page coordinates: no further scaling/offset is ever required." (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/gui/PageView.cpp) => The canonical 'dry layer' design: committed ink is a bitmap; pan is free; zoom shows a scaled bitmap until the async re-render lands; a stroke commit dirties only its rectangle. Per-frame cost is independent of stroke count. This is the model to adopt for Notes Gallery's committed strokes (tiles instead of one page buffer because the canvas is infinite).
- [high] Xournal++ draws the in-progress stroke by painting only the newest segment into a Cairo mask and repainting a rectangle the size of that segment plus half the stroke width; on pen-up the mask is blitted into the page buffer instead of re-rendering the page. - StrokeToolView.cpp: `mask = createMask(cr)` on first draw; `flushBuffer()` returns new points and "Keep the last point in the buffer - to be used in the next iteration"; dirty region `Range rg(lastPoint.x, lastPoint.y); rg.addPoint(addedPoint.x, addedPoint.y); rg.addPadding(0.5 * width)`; highlighter composited with `CAIRO_OPERATOR_MULTIPLY`, pen with `CAIRO_OPERATOR_OVER`; FINALIZATION_REQUEST -> `this->parent->drawAndDeleteToolView(this, rg)`; `this->mask.blitTo(cr)`. StrokeHandler.cpp: "Some devices emit a move event with pressure 0 when lifting the stylus tip / Ignore those events"; `if (distance < PIXEL_MOTION_THRESHOLD) return;`; long segments with large pressure change are subdivided respecting `MAX_WIDTH_VARIATION`; a stabilizer smooths input and `stabilizer->finalizeStroke()` is called to "Fill this gap" at pen-up; commit creates `InsertUndoAction`, adds the stroke to the layer and dispatches FINALIZATION_REQUEST with a `Range`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/view/overlays/StrokeToolView.cpp ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/control/tools/StrokeHandler.cpp) => Direct answer to 'slight latency while writing': the wet stroke should be drawn incrementally (append segment, repaint its bbox), never by clearing and repainting the whole stroke on a full-stage canvas. Also shows the pressure-0-on-lift filter, the subdivision of long segments to avoid width jumps (relevant to the 'spread across the gap' artefact), and committing by blitting the wet pixels rather than re-rasterising.
- [high] Xournal++ moves a selection by rasterising the selected elements once into an offscreen surface and transforming that bitmap per frame; the elements are modified only when the drag ends. - EditSelectionContents.cpp: `if (this->crBuffer == nullptr) { this->crBuffer = cairo_image_surface_create(CAIRO_FORMAT_ARGB32, ...)` rendered once via `ElementContainerView`; `paint()` composites it with `cairo_translate(... ) cairo_scale(cr2, fx, fy); cairo_translate(cr2, -dx, -dy); cairo_scale(cr2, zoom, zoom)` and `cairo_set_source_surface()`; `deleteViewBuffer()` destroys it on zoom change; `makeMoveEffective()` applies `e->move(mx, my)`, `e->scale(...)`, `e->rotate(...)` on completion. Rnote does the same at the cache level: `translate_strokes()` updates geometry and `key_tree.update_with_key(key, stroke.bounds())`, while `translate_strokes_images()` does `for image in render_comp.images.iter_mut() { image.translate(offset); }` with no re-render. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/control/tools/EditSelectionContents.cpp ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/store/stroke_comp.rs) => Directly addresses 'heavy lag when many strokes are selected and moved': the drag should transform one bitmap (and one AABB set), not N DOM elements or N re-rasterisations; geometry and index are updated once on release, after which only the affected tiles are re-rendered.
- [high] Rnote caches a rasterised image per stroke at image_scale = zoom x display scale factor, renders them off the main thread with Rayon, only for a viewport extended by 40%, drops images for off-screen strokes, splits large strokes into per-segment images, appends images for new segments while drawing, and shows a translucent placeholder while a render is pending. - render_comp.rs: `RenderCompState` = Complete | ForViewport(Aabb) | BusyRenderingInTask | Dirty; `regenerate_rendering_in_viewport_threaded` checks `viewport.extend_by(viewport.extents() * image::VIEWPORT_EXTENTS_MARGIN_FACTOR)`; off-viewport strokes get `render_comp.images = vec![]` and Dirty; `VIEWPORT_EXTENTS_MARGIN_RERENDER_THRESHOLD: f64 = 0.7`; `append_rendering_last_segments` renders only new segments; pending strokes drawn as rectangles `color::GNOME_BRIGHTS[1].with_alpha(0.13)`. image.rs: `VIEWPORT_EXTENTS_MARGIN_FACTOR` = 0.4 ("a larger value will consume more memory, a smaller value will mean more stuttering"); Cairo renders "bgra8-premultiplied, but we convert it to rgba8-premultiplied"; images drawn with bilinear interpolation. brushstroke.rs: `IMAGES_SIZE_THRESHOLD: f64 = 1000.0` and `IMAGES_STROKE_WIDTH_BOUNDS_THRESHOLD: f64 = 0.2` decide single image vs one image per segment. camera.rs: image_scale = `self.zoom * self.scale_factor`; `ZOOM_MIN = 0.2`, `ZOOM_MAX = 6.0`; a `temporary_zoom` overlays the permanent zoom during pinch and total_zoom is used for the viewport. keytree.rs: `rstar` R-tree "with [StrokeKey]'s as associated data. Used for faster spatial queries", `update_with_key` "Has to be called when the geometry of the stroke has changed", `rebuild_from_vec` uses `bulk_load`. brush.rs: path builders (Simple/Curved/Modeled) emit segments via `BuilderProgress::{InProgress, EmitContinue, Finished}`; finalization calls `update_geometry_for_stroke` then `store.record(Instant::now())`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/store/render_comp.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/image.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/strokes/brushstroke.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/camera.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/store/keytree.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/pens/brush.rs) => A modern infinite-canvas handwriting app (GTK/Rust) built exactly around the user's goals: per-stroke or per-tile bitmaps at zoom*dpr, background rasterisation with a viewport margin, cheap pinch-zoom via a temporary scale, memory bounded by dropping off-screen bitmaps, incremental wet rendering, and an R-tree. The concrete constants (0.4 margin, 0.7 rerender threshold, 1000 px split, zoom 0.2-6) are good starting values for Notes Gallery.
- [high] Rnote's undo is snapshot-based using Arc-shared slotmaps compared by pointer, capped at 100 entries, and it rebuilds the spatial index after undo while retaining existing rasters so strokes keep displaying during re-render. - store/mod.rs: `HistoryEntry { stroke_components: Arc<SlotMap<StrokeKey, Arc<Stroke>>>, trash_components: Arc<SecondaryMap<..>>, chrono_components: Arc<SecondaryMap<..>>, chrono_counter: u32 }`; `record()` only when different from last (`Arc::ptr_eq`), truncates redo, `HISTORY_MAX_LEN: usize = 100` ("Max length of the history"), `pop_front()` when exceeded; after `import_history_entry()` it calls `rebuild_rtree()` and `rebuild_retain_valid_keys_render_components()`: "Rebuild but retain the render components for the strokes that are found in the history entry. This ensures that we are able to continue displaying the strokes after undo/redo while they are rerendered." (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/flxzt/rnote/main/crates/rnote-engine/src/store/mod.rs) => Shows the render cache and spatial index must be treated as derived state that is repaired (not rebuilt from scratch visually) after undo; snapshot undo is viable when stroke objects are immutable and shared.
- [high] tldraw and Excalidraw store undo history as diffs (not snapshots): tldraw squashes RecordsDiffs between 'stop' marks and undoes by applying the reversed diff; Excalidraw stores inverse StoreDeltas and skips version fields when replaying. - HistoryManager.ts: entries are `{type:'diff', diff}` or `{type:'stop', id}`; `squashRecordDiffsMutable(diffToUndo, [reverseRecordsDiff(undo.diff)])`; `this.store.applyDiff(diffToUndo, { ignoreEphemeralKeys: true })`; batch modes `record`, `record-preserveRedoStack`, `ignore`. Excalidraw history.ts: `const historyDelta = HistoryDelta.inverse(delta)`; "we don't want to apply the version and versionNonce properties for history as we always need to end up with a new version due to collaboration"; redo stack is reset only on "non empty elements changes!"; undo loops past entries that "result in no visible changes". Xournal++ InsertUndoAction: undo `this->elementOwn = this->layer->removeElement(this->element).e; this->page->fireElementChanged(this->element);` redo `this->layer->addElement(std::move(this->elementOwn));`. Lorien uses Godot UndoRedo: `undo_redo.create_action("Stroke")`, `add_undo_method()`, `add_do_method()`, `commit_action()`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/editor/managers/HistoryManager/HistoryManager.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/history.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xournalpp/xournalpp/master/src/core/undo/InsertUndoAction.cpp ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mbrlabs/Lorien/main/lorien/InfiniteCanvas/InfiniteCanvas.gd) => Diff-based undo pairs naturally with a tile cache: the same record diff that is pushed to history can be used to compute which AABBs/tiles to invalidate and which index entries to update, giving one code path for edits, undo, and (later) incremental autosave.
- [medium] Lorien (Godot) keeps each stroke as a Line2D node with a pressure width curve, culls with the engine's on-screen notifier groups, and thins points with a distance/angle optimiser; it caps pressure jumps between consecutive points. - BrushStroke.gd: `@onready var _line2d: Line2D = $Line2D`; `_line2d.width_curve.add_point(Vector2(curve_step * p_idx, pressure / MAX_PRESSURE_VALUE))`; `if abs(pressure_diff) > MAX_PRESSURE_DIFF: pressure = last_pressure + sign(pressure_diff) * MAX_PRESSURE_DIFF`; bbox tracked in `refresh()` for the visibility notifier. InfiniteCanvas.gd: on-screen strokes from `get_tree().get_nodes_in_group(BrushStroke.GROUP_ONSCREEN)`; `if _use_optimizer: _optimizer.optimize(_current_stroke)`. BrushStrokeOptimizer.gd: `const MIN_DISTANCE := 4.0`, `const ANGLE_THRESHOLD := 0.5`, `if s.points.size() < 8: return`; removes points with `distance <= MIN_DISTANCE` and `angle_diff < ANGLE_THRESHOLD`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mbrlabs/Lorien/main/lorien/BrushStroke/BrushStroke.gd ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mbrlabs/Lorien/main/lorien/InfiniteCanvas/InfiniteCanvas.gd ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mbrlabs/Lorien/main/lorien/BrushStroke/BrushStrokeOptimizer.gd) => A retained-scene-graph (GPU) approach that works because Godot batches Line2D meshes on the GPU - the analogue on the web would be WebGL, not DOM. The pressure-delta clamp is a cheap fix for width spikes on sparse samples; the optimiser thresholds are reasonable defaults for point thinning at commit time.
- [high] libmypaint renders strokes as dabs spaced by distance and time so fast motion never leaves gaps, and paints into 64px tiles: each dab is queued to the tiles its bounding box touches, processed at end_atomic (in parallel when >3 tiles), with a dirty bounding box returned for repaint. - mypaint-brush.c stroke_to: `count_dabs_to()` sums `dist / ACTUAL_RADIUS * DABS_PER_ACTUAL_RADIUS`, `dist / base_radius * DABS_PER_BASIC_RADIUS`, `dt * DABS_PER_SECOND`; loop `while (dabs_moved + dabs_todo >= 1.0)` linearly interpolating position/pressure/tilt/time with `frac = step_ddab / dabs_todo`; `if (dtime <= 0) dtime = 0.0001`; `max_dtime = 5` resets on long gaps; slow-tracking filter `x = STATE(self,X) + (x - STATE(self,X)) * fac`. mypaint-tiled-surface.c: `r_fringe = radius + 1.0f; tx1 = floor(floor(x - r_fringe) / MYPAINT_TILE_SIZE)` ... `operation_queue_add(self->operation_queue, tile_index, op_copy)`; `#pragma omp parallel for schedule(static) if(self->threadsafe_tile_requests && tiles_n > 3)`; `render_dab_mask(mask, op->x - tx*MYPAINT_TILE_SIZE, ...)`; dirty bboxes merged in `end_atomic`. Krita wiki: `TILEWIDTH 64`, `TILEHEIGHT 64`, KisTileHashTable "used as a factory for lazy creation of tiles". Krita kis_opengl_image_textures.cpp: GPU texture tiles `m_texturesInfo.width = qMin(preferredTextureSize, maxTextureSize)` with `border = cfg.textureOverlapBorder()` and `effectiveWidth = width - 2 * border`; `updateCache(dirtyRect)` then `recalculateCache()` re-uploads only intersecting tiles; configurable mipmap levels for zoomed-out filtering. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mypaint/libmypaint/master/mypaint-brush.c ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mypaint/libmypaint/master/mypaint-tiled-surface.c ; https://community.kde.org/Krita/Tile_Data_Format ; https://invent.kde.org/graphics/krita/-/raw/master/libs/ui/opengl/kis_opengl_image_textures.cpp) => Two lessons: (1) raster painters interpolate along the segment so a fast stroke is continuous by construction - the web equivalent is to interpolate/curve between sparse samples rather than draw a straight chord; (2) tiles with an overlap border and dirty-rect re-upload are the standard way to make repaint and zoom cost proportional to the changed area, not the document. Tile size 64 is for CPU pixel storage; screen-texture tiles are 256+ px.
- [high] Figma rejected DOM/SVG/Canvas 2D for a custom WebGL tile-based renderer specifically because browser retained-mode graphics re-tessellate on zoom and Canvas 2D re-uploads geometry every frame; Concepts (Android/ChromeOS) uses front-buffer rendering and five renderer variants over OpenGL ES 3.1. - Figma: "HTML and SVG contain a lot of baggage and are often much slower than the 2D canvas API due to DOM access"; they are "usually optimized for scrolling, not zooming, and geometry is often re-tessellated after every scale change"; Canvas 2D: "all geometry has to be re-uploaded to the graphics card every frame"; result is a "highly-optimized tile-based engine" with "our own DOM, our own compositor, our own text layout engine". Concepts case study: "The team found its solution through front-buffer rendering... to allow pixels to be copied the absolute minimum number of times"; "five variations of its rendering engine... around 2,500 devices after establishing Android 7 and OpenGL ES 3.1 as minimum requirements"; goal "the delay from receiving touch input to rendering strokes on screen was as minimal as possible". No Miro, Microsoft Whiteboard or Jamboard engineering primary sources were found (Miro's engineering blog covers a code-editor widget only; a secondary source claims Miro built its own canvas text layout). (https://www.figma.com/blog/building-a-professional-design-tool-on-the-web/ ; https://developer.android.com/stories/apps/concepts ; https://medium.com/miro-engineering/how-we-integrated-a-code-editor-on-the-miro-canvas-a41e0eff7f21) => Confirms from the highest-end web canvas that retained DOM/SVG is the wrong substrate for zoomable vector documents, and that tiles are the accepted mitigation. WebGL is the ceiling; Canvas 2D tiles (Xournal++/Rnote style) are the pragmatic middle for a personal notes app. Miro/MS/Google internals are undocumented publicly - do not cite them in the decision.
- [high] Android's platform guidance and Windows Ink both formalise the wet/dry split: in-progress strokes render on a low-latency path (front buffer / background ink thread) covering a small screen area, completed strokes are handed to the normal composited layer; front-buffer rendering is explicitly not for panning/zooming or full-screen updates. - Android advanced stylus: low-latency library "avoiding multi-buffer rendering and leveraging a front-buffer rendering technique, which means writing directly to the screen"; "While front-buffer rendering is a great technique to render a small area of the screen, it is not designed to be used for refreshing the entire screen"; Do: "Small portions of the screen, handwriting, drawing, sketching"; Don't: "Fullscreen update, panning, zooming. Can result in tearing." Callbacks: `onDrawFrontBufferedLayer()` for in-progress strokes, `onDrawDoubleBufferedLayer()` for completed strokes, `commit()` on ACTION_UP. "Latency = hardware and OS input processing + app processing + system compositing + hardware rendering"; touch sampling "60 to 1000Hz". Motion prediction (Kalman): "predicted data must be replaced with actual MotionEvent data once it is received"; Do: "Remove prediction points when a new predicted point is added"; Don't: "use prediction points for final rendering". `requestUnbufferedDispatch()` "asks that the input system doesn't batch motion events". Windows Ink: "ink input is processed on a low-latency background thread and rendered in-progress, or 'wet', as it is drawn. When the stroke is completed... rendered 'dry' to the InkCanvas layer"; custom drying is recommended for "More efficient management of large, or complex, collections of ink strokes" and "More efficient panning and zooming support on large ink canvases" using a VirtualSurfaceImageSource "larger than the screen for performant panning and zooming". Jetpack Ink API: "uses the Jetpack Graphics Library for low latency", core is Google's Ink library which "produces smoothed, modeled stroke shapes... as mesh-based vector graphics" with an `InProgressStroke` builder. (https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/advanced-stylus-features ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/apps/design/input/pen-and-stylus-interactions ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/about-ink-api ; https://github.com/google/ink) => Platform vendors codify what the open-source apps do: wet stroke = small incremental low-latency surface; dry strokes = cached composited layer; prediction only for the visible tail. On the web the analogue is a desynchronized canvas for the wet stroke (small clears, incremental segments) and tiled Canvas 2D for dry ink; the native Android wrapper could later use the Jetpack low-latency/Ink stack for the wet layer if WebView latency remains unacceptable.
- [high] Chrome aligns pointermove/touchmove dispatch to requestAnimationFrame (one event per frame) and exposes the intermediate samples via getCoalescedEvents(); the spec requires pointerrawupdate to be dispatched before its pointermove but allows pointerrawupdate itself to be coalesced, and warns it can hurt performance. - Chrome blog (Chrome 60): will "delay dispatching continuous events (wheel, mousewheel, touchmove, pointermove, mousemove) and dispatch them right before the requestAnimationFrame() callback occurs"; "we perform 35% less hit tests"; touch input "60-120Hz", mice "100Hz (but can be anywhere up to 2000Hz)", monitors "60Hz"; getCoalescedEvents (Chrome 58) lets you "retrieve the full path of the pointer even while it's receiving fewer events". Intent to ship: "Coalescing already happens inside the input pipeline when the main thread is busy"; Android's `getHistorySize()` cited as the native analogue. W3C spec: pointerrawupdate fires "when a pointer changes any properties that don't fire pointerdown or pointerup events", "as soon as possible and as frequently as the JavaScript can handle"; "MUST dispatch the pointerrawupdate event before the corresponding pointermove"; "the user agent MAY coalesce the new pointerrawupdate with that event instead of creating a new task"; predicted events list "will, for performance reasons, usually be an empty list"; "Adding listeners for the pointerrawupdate event might negatively impact the performance". MDN: "pointerrawupdate coalesces less, so its listeners run more often"; "An application that cannot keep up will feel less responsive rather than more"; secure context only (Chrome 142/143 per blink-dev). Chromium main_thread_event_queue.cc: when `has_pointerrawupdate_handlers_` a `kPointerRawUpdate` copy is queued per mouse/touch move; `IsRawUpdateEvent` items never flush the queue; `IsRafAlignedEvent` returns true for kMouseMove/kMouseWheel/kTouchMove unless `needs_low_latency_` or `needs_low_latency_until_pointer_up_`; `kMaxRafDelay = base::Milliseconds(5 * 1000)` fallback timer. (https://developer.chrome.com/blog/aligning-input-events ; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/5OBK4Uew2Js/m/VlYpo_JcAgAJ ; https://w3c.github.io/pointerevents/#the-pointerrawupdate-event ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Element/pointerrawupdate_event ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc) => Listening to both pointerrawupdate and pointermove guarantees every sample is seen twice (the raw copy and the coalesced copy in the frame-aligned pointermove), so a de-dup is mandatory; a timestamp-keyed de-dup is fragile because pointerrawupdate events can themselves carry a coalesced list and two distinct samples may share a timeStamp after OS resampling (the sharing is inferred, not verified). The established approach (tldraw) is pointermove + getCoalescedEvents only, with rendering once per frame; pointerrawupdate should only be used to feed the wet renderer if the handler does nothing but push coordinates.
- [low] Chromium's Android glue reads MotionEvent historical (batched) samples but, in the conversion code inspected, uses them only for timestamps and velocity, not to emit extra web pointer samples; Android documents that ACTION_MOVE events may batch several samples. - AOSP MotionEvent javadoc: "For efficiency, motion events with ACTION_MOVE may batch together multiple movement samples within a single object... To process all coordinates in the batch in time order, first consume the historical coordinates then consume the current coordinates." Chromium EventForwarder.java sendTouchEvent: `final int historySize = event.getHistorySize();` with comment "Android may batch multiple events together for efficiency. We want to use the oldest event time as hardware time stamp. Unless we're using Android buffered input, in which case we use MotionEvent.getEventTime[Nanos](), which the OS already resampled based on historical events. Note that we still keep the historical events for tracking velocity." and a feature flag `InputFeatureMap.USE_ANDROID_BUFFERED_INPUT_DISPATCH`; native call passes `(mNativeEventForwarder, event, oldestEventTime, latestEventTime, eventAction, touchMajor..., gestureClassification, isTouchHandleEvent, isLatestEventTimeResampled)`. motion_event_android.cc exposes `GetHistorySize()/GetHistoricalX()/GetHistoricalEventTime()` ("While the spec states that only Action::MOVE events should contain historical entries..."). blink_event_util.cc `CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent` builds one WebTouchEvent from the current sample only (no GetHistorySize loop found); render_widget_host_view_android.cc `OnTouchEvent` calls it once per MotionEvent and routes via `input_helper_->RouteOrForwardTouchEvent(web_event)`. ContentView.java (components/embedder_support) does not call `requestUnbufferedDispatch`. Inference: web pages on Chrome Android see one position per delivered MotionEvent; coalesced events come from separate MotionEvents queued behind a busy main thread/rAF alignment, not from Android's intra-event history. Not confirmed by a bug or design doc; could be handled in a file not read. (https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/MotionEvent.java ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/EventForwarder.java ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/ui/events/android/motion_event_android.cc ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/chromium/chromium/main/content/browser/renderer_host/render_widget_host_view_android.cc) => If correct, the 'skipped points on fast curves' on the Samsung tablet is partly an input-supply problem that no JS de-dup change can fix: the maximum sample rate reaching the page is bounded by Android's delivery cadence to Chrome (vsync-batched unless Chrome requests unbuffered dispatch, which the files read do not show). The design consequence is to make the wet renderer robust to ~60 samples/s: interpolate smooth curves between samples (quadratic through midpoints), use getPredictedEvents for the visible tail, and never draw straight chords with corner caps. This must be measured on the device before being relied upon (see open questions).
- [high] Chrome's `desynchronized` canvas hint bypasses the compositor queue to cut input-to-display latency, but only remains effective when nothing overlaps the canvas (for alpha canvases), may tear, and full hardware-overlay support exists only on ChromeOS; the delegated Ink API is not broadly available. - Chrome blog: the hint "tells the underlying system to skip as much compositing as it is able and in some cases, the canvas's underlying buffer is sent directly to the screen's display controller"; "latencies longer than 50 milliseconds can interfere with a user's hand-eye coordination"; "A translucent canvas element, one where alpha is set to true, can still be desynchronized, but it must not have any other DOM elements above it"; "You cannot change the context attributes after the first call to canvas.getContext()"; WebGL needs `preserveDrawingBuffer: true` and "if you must clear, draw to an offscreen framebuffer then copy that to the screen"; verify with `ctx.getContextAttributes().desynchronized`; "devices with front buffer rendering such as ChromeOS for example may have tearing". blink-dev intent: works on "Windows, Mac, Linux, Chrome OS, and Android" but "hardware overlays are only fully supported on Chrome OS", others "might react badly to the app trying to render to the front buffer"; "Hardware overlay buffers must be available"; apps should "draw onto its own back buffer and then blit onto the front buffer". MDN getContext: desynchronized "hints the user agent to reduce the latency by desynchronizing the canvas paint cycle from the event loop"; `alpha:false` lets the browser know "the backdrop is always opaque, which can speed up drawing". MDN Ink API: browsers "pass such rendering instructions directly to OS-level compositors" via `navigator.ink.requestPresenter({presentationArea})` / `updateInkTrailStartPoint(evt, style)`; "Limited availability". getPredictedEvents: Chromium intent reports Kalman-based prediction with "pretty good results in 95% and ok results in 99%". (https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized ; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/jWBA5ooXNIU ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLCanvasElement/getContext ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Ink_API ; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/emvtXAXtqWs) => The 'live preview sometimes missing entirely' symptom is consistent with the documented fragility of desynchronized canvases: DOM above the wet canvas (selection handles, toolbars, the #world DOM strokes if z-ordered above) can silently disable the fast path or cause missed presents (inferred). 2.0 should keep the wet canvas topmost and free of overlapping DOM during inking, check `getContextAttributes().desynchronized` at runtime, draw incrementally (no full clears), and use getPredictedEvents for the tail; the Ink API is not a dependable option.
- [high] Excalidraw keeps selection chrome crisp and constant-size at any zoom by dividing every line width and handle radius by the zoom on the interactive canvas; tldraw's canvas overlay applies DPR and camera transforms once per frame for all overlays. - interactiveScene.ts: `context.lineWidth = (activeEmbeddable ? 4 : 1) / appState.zoom.value`; handle radii `radius / appState.zoom.value`; dashed borders via `context.setLineDash([lineWidth, spaceWidth + (lineWidth + spaceWidth) * (count - 1)])`; draws selection borders, transform handles, binding highlights, snap lines, remote cursors, scrollbars. CanvasOverlays.tsx: one canvas, DPR scaling and zoom transform, all overlays in z order. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/packages/excalidraw/renderer/interactiveScene.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/components/default-components/CanvasOverlays.tsx) => Concrete recipe for the overlay canvas in 2.0: selection frame, N per-stroke outlines, lasso polygon and handles are all drawn in one pass with screen-constant widths (1/zoom), avoiding N DOM class toggles and layout.
- [high] tldraw exposes explicit level-of-detail hooks so per-frame cost during camera movement does not scale with content fidelity: a debounced 'efficient zoom level', solid-only freehand strokes at low zoom, power-of-two image resolution stepping, and dropped shadows. - Docs: `getEfficientZoomLevel()` "returns a stable value" during camera movement when there are >500 shapes; "Dashed and dotted freehand strokes render as solid lines" and sticky notes drop shadows at low zoom; text shadows off below `textShadowLod` 0.35; image assets resolved by `steppedScreenScale` rounded to a power of two ("A 4000px-wide photo zoomed out to take up 200px on screen has a screen scale of 0.05, which steps up to 0.0625, so you'd serve a 250px-wide image"); `PerformanceManager` reports `fps` and `p95FrameTime` on 'interaction-end'. DrawShapeUtil: `forceSolid = zoomLevel < 0.5 && zoomLevel < 1.5 / sw`. (https://tldraw.dev/sdk-features/performance ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/shapes/draw/DrawShapeUtil.tsx) => For images and nested-card thumbnails in Notes Gallery, cache bitmaps at power-of-two zoom buckets and reuse across nearby zooms; instrument p95 frame time during pen-down and pan as the acceptance metric for 2.0.

Recommendations:
- Adopt the wet/dry split used by every inking engine read (Xournal++, Rnote, Windows Ink, Android low-latency stack): keep a small, topmost, desynchronized wet canvas for the stroke in progress, and move all committed ink out of the DOM into a raster cache. Committed strokes should never be DOM nodes; text, tables, checkboxes and card widgets can remain DOM (tldraw pattern) as long as they are rbush-culled and positioned by direct style mutation, and are never stacked above the wet canvas while a pen is down.
