- Dry layer = tiled Canvas 2D bitmaps, not one full-stage canvas: tiles of ~512 device px keyed by (tileX, tileY, zoomBucket), rendered at zoom x devicePixelRatio (Excalidraw/Rnote), each tile drawn by querying a spatial index (rbush or flatbush) for strokes intersecting the tile (with AABBs padded by half stroke width plus perfect-freehand's overshoot) and drawing cached Path2D objects in chrono order. Pan = translate existing tiles; commit = invalidate only the tiles under the new stroke's bbox (Xournal++ rerenderRect); off-screen tiles are evicted with an LRU and a memory cap respecting the ~16.7M px per-canvas area limit Excalidraw enforces; render tiles for a viewport extended by ~40% (Rnote) in idle time or in a Worker via OffscreenCanvas so per-frame cost is independent of page content.
- Zoom: during a pinch/wheel gesture scale the existing tiles (Excalidraw `shouldCacheIgnoreZoom`, Rnote `temporary_zoom`, Xournal++ stale-buffer scaling) and re-rasterise the visible tiles at the new zoom bucket only when the gesture settles; use power-of-two zoom buckets so tiles are reused across nearby zooms (tldraw image LOD). Clamp zoom (Rnote 0.2-6.0) so cache memory is bounded.
- Wet stroke rendering: draw incrementally - append only the newest segment(s) each animation frame and repaint only that segment's rectangle padded by half the width (Xournal++ StrokeToolView), instead of clearing and re-outlining the whole stroke. If a perfect-freehand style variable-width outline is kept, either (a) render the stable prefix incrementally and re-outline only the last ~8 points each frame, or (b) render simple pressure-scaled round-joined segments while wet and swap in the perfect-freehand outline at commit into the tile cache. Disable `simulatePressure` for pen input, clamp per-point pressure deltas (Lorien MAX_PRESSURE_DIFF), subdivide long segments so width cannot jump (Xournal++ MAX_WIDTH_VARIATION), and interpolate curves through sample midpoints (Excalidraw/tldraw quadratic path) so a sparse fast curve never renders as a straight chord with a 13-point corner cap - this is the likely 'brush spreads across the gap' artefact.
- Input pipeline: use one event source. Prefer `pointermove` + `getCoalescedEvents()` (tldraw does exactly this; Chrome coalesces since 58 and rAF-aligns since 60) and render once per frame. If pointerrawupdate is retained for the wet tail, its handler must only push coordinates (spec/MDN warning) and de-dup must not be by timestamp alone - de-dup by object identity is impossible across the two streams, so the robust choice is to drop one stream. Use `getPredictedEvents()` (Kalman in Chrome) to draw a predicted tail that is erased and replaced every frame and never persisted (Android guidance). Filter pressure-0 move events emitted on lift (Xournal++). Apply a zoom-relative minimum point spacing (tldraw: 1/zoom page units) rather than time-based filtering.
- Selection and lasso without DOM: keep every stroke's AABB in rbush/flatbush; lasso = simplify the lasso polygon (Excalidraw), broad-phase bbox query, narrow-phase point-in-polygon on stroke points or per-segment hitboxes (Xournal++/Rnote), computed on pointer-up or throttled to one evaluation per frame; draw lasso, selection outlines and handles on a single overlay canvas with widths divided by zoom (Excalidraw interactive canvas; tldraw's 25x indicator win). Hit-test handles geometrically.
- Moving/scaling many selected strokes: rasterise the selection once into an offscreen bitmap (or just exclude them from the dry tiles and draw the bitmap), transform that single bitmap per frame with drawImage/CSS transform, and apply the geometric transform to the strokes, the spatial index and the affected tiles only on release (Xournal++ EditSelectionContents `makeMoveEffective`, Rnote `translate_strokes_images`).
- Undo/redo: store record diffs (tldraw RecordsDiff with squashing and 'stop' marks, or Excalidraw inverse deltas) and derive tile invalidation and spatial-index updates from the same diff; if strokes are immutable objects, Rnote-style shared snapshots capped at ~100 entries are an acceptable simpler alternative. Retain existing tile bitmaps across undo while dirty tiles re-render (Rnote 'retain render components').
- Keep the same file format but make the in-memory model raster-friendly: immutable stroke records (points with pressure, style, AABB) in a Map keyed by id, a chrono order list per canvas/card, an rbush per canvas; nested card canvases get their own tile cache and index that are built lazily when opened. Autosave granularity is outside this topic but the diff stream from the undo system is the natural hook for incremental saves later.
- Instrument before and after: log `getContextAttributes().desynchronized` on the Samsung tablet, the number of coalesced events per second while drawing fast (to confirm or refute the Chromium-drops-Android-history inference), and p95 frame time during pen-down, pan and lasso (tldraw PerformanceManager pattern). Set acceptance targets: wet-stroke draw <= 1 frame behind input at 60/120 Hz, pan cost flat from 100 to 10,000 strokes, lasso of 2,000 strokes < 16 ms.
Open questions:
- Does Chrome/WebView on the Samsung tablet expose S-Pen samples above the display refresh rate to web content? The Chromium files read (EventForwarder.java, blink_event_util.cc, render_widget_host_view_android.cc) suggest Android MotionEvent history is used only for velocity/timestamps and one web sample is produced per MotionEvent, and no `requestUnbufferedDispatch` call was found; this is inferred from absence and must be measured (count coalesced + raw events per second while drawing fast, compare with the digitiser rate).
- Is the desynchronized fast path actually granted on the tablet (`ctx.getContextAttributes().desynchronized`), and is any DOM element ever layered above the wet canvas during inking (Chrome documents that overlapping DOM breaks it for alpha canvases)? If yes, this may be the whole 'preview missing' bug.
- Tile size, zoom-bucket scheme and memory budget for the dry cache on a 2.5-dpr Android tablet: what total canvas memory the WebView tolerates before eviction/blank tiles (Excalidraw caps a single canvas at 16,777,216 px for Safari; Chrome Android limits are undocumented here).
- Whether to rasterise dry tiles on the main thread in idle slices or in a Worker with OffscreenCanvas (Rnote uses threads; Xournal++ a background render job) - depends on whether the native wrappers (Android WebView, Windows) support OffscreenCanvas transfer reliably.
- Whether the wet-stroke appearance may differ slightly from the committed stroke (simple segments while wet, perfect-freehand outline at commit), or must be pixel-identical (which forces incremental outline rendering).
- Whether to keep DOM at all for non-ink objects (text, tables, checkboxes, connectors, card frames) with an rbush cull, or render them to the tile cache too and only mount DOM for the object being edited (tldraw keeps DOM; Figma/Miro render everything themselves). This decides how nested card canvases and their thumbnails are cached.
- Windows/desktop path: whether desynchronized canvas and getPredictedEvents behave the same in the Windows wrapper as in Chrome, and whether the Android app should eventually move the wet layer to the Jetpack low-latency/Ink API instead of WebView.
- No primary engineering sources were found for Miro, Microsoft Whiteboard (web) or Google Jamboard/Keep drawing; only Windows Ink platform docs and Android's Ink API docs were available as vendor design references.

## android
The decisive finding for the Samsung-tablet complaints is in the input path, not the paint path. Android batches stylus samples into one MotionEvent per Choreographer frame (history accessible via getHistoricalX/Y). Chromium's Android event builders only ever read the CURRENT sample of a MotionEvent (ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc, render_widget_host_view_android.cc, EventForwarder.java) - historical samples are never turned into coalesced events. Chrome sidesteps this by calling View.requestUnbufferedDispatch() on ACTION_DOWN, but that call is gated by `!UsingSynchronousCompositing()` in WebContentsViewAndroid, and AwContentClient::UsingSynchronousCompositing() returns true - so Android System WebView (hence Tauri) receives buffered, per-vsync input and drops the extra digitiser samples. At 60/120 Hz that is 1 sample per frame from a ~360 Hz pen: exactly 'points skipped on fast curvy strokes, stroke jumps to where the pen ended'. pointerrawupdate cannot help here because Chromium creates one raw event per incoming MotionEvent. A desynchronized canvas only switches off rAF-alignment inside the renderer (MainThreadEventQueue::RequestUnbufferedInputEvents) and gives the canvas its own frame sink; the GpuMemoryBuffer/overlay path (kLowLatencyCanvas2dImageChromium) is ChromeOS-only. The fix is available from the Tauri host: WryActivity exposes `onWebViewCreate(webView)`, and calling `webView.requestUnbufferedDispatch(event)` on ACTION_DOWN from an OnTouchListener replicates what Chrome does (API 21). On rendering: WebView is neither SurfaceView nor TextureView - it draws through a private draw functor inside the app's HWUI RenderThread frame with a synchronous compositor driven by Android's Choreographer; WebView engineers state it is expected to be slower than Chrome and cannot use Chrome's newer input-on-Viz path. Per-frame costs the app currently pays that scale with page content: re-rasterisation of every stroke's tiles on script-driven transform scale (Chrome 53+ unless will-change: transform), render surfaces forced by mix-blend-mode on the live ink canvas, filter/backdrop-filter, opacity groups and 2D-scale transforms with composited descendants (cc RenderSurfaceReason), plus a per-WebView tile budget of roughly 20x the visible area. Feature availability is not the problem: WebView mirrors Chrome (coalesced 58, OffscreenCanvas 69, desynchronized 75, pointerrawupdate + getPredictedEvents 77, LoAF 123; pointerrawupdate becomes secure-context-only at 142; Delegated Ink Trails never shipped on Android/WebView). 120 Hz follows the window's display mode (Samsung Power saving forces 60 Hz; the host can hint preferredRefreshRate/Surface.setFrameRate). Everything needed for an in-app diagnostics overlay is measurable from JS: samples/s, `performance.now() - e.timeStamp` (Chromium stamps events with the oldest hardware time), rAF cadence, LoAF entries, `getContextAttributes().desynchronized`, UA 'wv' + Chrome version, prefers-reduced-motion.

Findings:
- [high] Android batches ACTION_MOVE samples into a single MotionEvent per frame; earlier samples are only reachable via getHistoricalX/Y, and batched input is held until the receiver consumes it just before drawing a frame. - MotionEvent javadoc: "For efficiency, motion events with ACTION_MOVE may batch together multiple movement samples within a single object. The most current pointer coordinates are available using getX(int) and getY(int). Earlier coordinates within the batch are accessed using getHistoricalX(int, int) and getHistoricalY(int, int)... To process all coordinates in the batch in time order, first consume the historical coordinates then consume the current coordinates." InputEventReceiver javadoc: "The batched input event will continue to accumulate additional movement samples until the recipient calls consumeBatchedInputEvents or an event is received that ends the batch" and consumeBatchedInputEvents "should be called just before animating or drawing a new frame in the UI" (i.e. the Choreographer frame). (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/android.views.motionevent (Android javadoc mirror) and https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/view/InputEventReceiver.java) => Established: on a 60/120 Hz Galaxy Tab a ~360 Hz S-Pen produces 3-6 samples per frame that arrive as ONE MotionEvent with history. Whatever consumes that event must read the history or the samples are gone.
- [medium] Chromium's Android event conversion uses only the current sample of each MotionEvent; historical samples are not expanded into coalesced events. - ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent "processes only the current pointer data... does not reference any history-related methods like GetHistorySize() or GetHistoricalX()". content/browser/renderer_host/render_widget_host_view_android.cc OnTouchEvent: "blink::WebTouchEvent web_event = ui::CreateWebTouchEventFromMotionEvent(event, result.moved_beyond_slop_region, false)" and "Historical motion data is never referenced in this file". EventForwarder.java reads `event.getHistorySize()` only to pick the timestamp: "Android may batch multiple events together for efficiency. We want to use the oldest event time as hardware time stamp"; "Historical coordinates themselves aren't explicitly passed to native". MotionEventAndroid exposes GetHistoricalX/Y but nothing on the web-event path calls them. Caveat: I read the three files on the touch path; a whole-repo grep (grep.app/Sourcegraph) was rate-limited/blocked, so a hidden expansion elsewhere cannot be 100% excluded. (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/ui/events/blink/blink_event_util.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/content/browser/renderer_host/render_widget_host_view_android.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/EventForwarder.java ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/refs/heads/main/ui/events/android/motion_event_android.cc) => Inferred consequence (high confidence): whenever Android hands Chromium batched MotionEvents, only 1 sample per frame reaches JS regardless of pointerrawupdate or getCoalescedEvents. This, not the app's timestamp de-dup, is the most likely cause of complaint (2) on the tablet.
- [high] Chrome requests unbuffered dispatch from Android on ACTION_DOWN, but the request is disabled when synchronous compositing is used - which is exactly Android WebView. WebView therefore runs on Android's buffered, vsync-batched input. - content/browser/web_contents/web_contents_view_android.cc: "// True if we want to disable Android native event batching and use compositor event queue." `bool ShouldRequestUnbufferedDispatch() { static bool should_request_unbuffered_dispatch = !GetContentClient()->UsingSynchronousCompositing() && !base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(input::features::kUseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch); ... }` and in OnTouchEvent: `if (event.GetAction() == ui::MotionEventAndroid::Action::DOWN && ShouldRequestUnbufferedDispatch()) view_.RequestUnbufferedDispatch(event);`. android_webview/common/aw_content_client.cc: `bool AwContentClient::UsingSynchronousCompositing() { return true; }`. components/input/features.cc: `BASE_FEATURE(kUseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch, base::FEATURE_DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT);` (so Chrome = unbuffered by default). EventForwarder.java comment confirms the Chrome norm: "In the case of unbuffered input that chrome uses usually history_size == 0 and oldest_time == latest_time". ViewAndroidDelegate.requestUnbufferedDispatch forwards to `container.requestUnbufferedDispatch(event)` with an LG-stylus workaround (crbug.com/1064161). No requestUnbufferedDispatch call was found in AwContents.java (file truncated in fetch) or via search. (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/content/browser/web_contents/web_contents_view_android.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/common/aw_content_client.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/components/input/features.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/ViewAndroidDelegate.java) => This is the root architectural difference between 'Chrome on the same tablet' and 'the Tauri app': Chrome sees every pen sample, WebView sees one per vsync. It is fixable from the host app (next finding).
- [high] View.requestUnbufferedDispatch(MotionEvent) (API 21) makes Android deliver every sample immediately until ACTION_UP; a host Activity can call it on the WebView itself. - Android javadoc: "Until this View receives a corresponding MotionEvent#ACTION_UP, ask that the input system not batch MotionEvents but instead deliver them as soon as they're available. This method should only be called for touch events." Warning: "This API is not intended for most applications. Buffered dispatch provides many of benefits, and just requesting unbuffered dispatch on most MotionEvent streams will not improve your input latency. Side effects include: increased latency, jittery scrolls and inability to take advantage of system resampling." requestUnbufferedDispatch(int source) (API 30) "does not automatically terminate, and allows the specification of arbitrary input source classes". Android's stylus guide recommends it for drawing: "You can use requestUnbufferedDispatch() to ask that the input system doesn't batch motion events but instead delivers them as soon as they're available." (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/android.views.view.requestunbuffereddispatch ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/advanced-stylus-features) => Concrete 2.0 change: in MainActivity.onWebViewCreate set `webView.setOnTouchListener { v, e -> if (e.actionMasked == MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN && e.getToolType(0) == MotionEvent.TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS) v.requestUnbufferedDispatch(e); false }` - the same thing Chrome does, restricted to the pen so finger scrolling keeps Android resampling. (Inferred: with one MotionEvent per sample, Chromium will emit one pointerrawupdate per sample.)
- [high] pointerrawupdate is created once per incoming move event before rAF alignment, is never rAF-aligned, may itself be coalesced when the main thread is busy, and carries a coalesced list but no predicted events. - main_thread_event_queue.cc: for kMouseMove / moved touch points a `WebCoalescedInputEvent(... kPointerRawUpdate ...)` is created and `QueueEvent(QueuedWebInputEvent::CreateForRawEvent(...))`; `AreCoalescablePointerRawUpdateEvents()` coalesces consecutive raw updates; `ShouldFlushQueue`: "if (IsRawUpdateEvent(item)) return false;"; `IsRafAlignedEvent()` covers only kMouseMove, kMouseWheel, kTouchMove. pointer_event_factory.cc Create(): `if (type == kPointermove || type == kPointerrawupdate) coalesced_pointer_events = CreateEventSequence(...coalesced_events...)`; `if (type == kPointermove) predicted_pointer_events = CreateEventSequence(...predicted_events...)`. HandleEventResampling applies only to rAF-aligned events, so raw updates are never resampled. (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/core/events/pointer_event_factory.cc) => Keep iterating getCoalescedEvents() on pointerrawupdate (a busy main thread folds several raw updates into one). Prediction is only on pointermove - use it for the predicted tail only. Both channels carry the same WebTouchEvents, so a 'raw as source of truth, pointermove for prediction only' design removes the timestamp de-dup entirely.
- [medium] A desynchronized canvas in Chromium (a) gets its own compositor layer + CanvasResourceDispatcher (separate frame sink), and (b) asks the renderer to stop rAF-aligning move events until pointer-up. It does not change Android-level batching, and the GpuMemoryBuffer/overlay fast path is ChromeOS-only. - html_canvas_element.cc: `if (context_->CreationAttributes().desynchronized) { if (!CreateLayer()) return nullptr; SetNeedsUnbufferedInputEvents(true); GetOrCreateResourceDispatcher(); }`; PostFinalizeFrame dispatches frames directly: `frame_dispatcher_->DispatchFrame(std::move(exported_resource), dirty_rect_, IsOpaque())`. main_thread_event_queue.cc: `void MainThreadEventQueue::RequestUnbufferedInputEvents() { needs_low_latency_until_pointer_up_.store(true, ...); }` and IsRafAlignedEvent returns false while that flag is set, so "mouse moves and touch moves bypass the rAF-aligned queue path and dispatch immediately". blink features.cc: kLowLatencyCanvas2dImageChromium "Enables the use of GpuMemoryBuffer images for low latency 2d canvas" is FEATURE_ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT only on ChromeOS Ash, disabled elsewhere. Chrome blog: "Call getContextAttributes(). If the returned attributes object has a desynchronized property, then test it"; "A translucent canvas element, one where alpha is set to true, can still be desynchronized, but it must not have any other DOM elements above it." Chromestatus: shipped M75 desktop/Android/WebView. (The link WebFrameWidgetImpl -> MainThreadEventQueue was not read directly; endpoints were.) (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/core/html/canvas/html_canvas_element.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/common/features.cc ; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized ; https://chromestatus.com/feature/6360971442388992) => Keep `desynchronized: true` (it is what makes pointermove immediate in WebView) but do not expect front-buffer latency on Android; verify with getContextAttributes(). The current #ink-live canvas is alpha:true and sits under UI chrome - any DOM element above it can defeat the hint.
- [high] Android WebView draws through a private 'draw functor' inside the app's HWUI display list on the RenderThread, with a synchronous compositor whose frame timing is dictated by Android, not by Chromium's scheduler. - BlinkOn 3 WebView architecture slides: draw functor = "Private API. The WebView injects a callback onto the display list. When the painting reaches that step, the callback is called and the WebView runs arbitrary code to produce arbitrary GL, then returns."; compositor merged with UI thread so "nothing can be async"; "CC doesn't make its own scheduling decisions. It draws immediately whenever Android decides."; redraws are requested via View.invalidate(); a 'pull' model where Android controls scroll/matrix at the last moment vs Chrome's 'push'. begin_frame_source_webview.h: the root source "observes ExternalBeginFrameSourceAndroid to provide actual BeginFrames from Android Choreographer"; "Choreographer only uses the built-in display for frame timing." browser_view_renderer.h: "Approximates whether render thread functor has a frame to draw." RenderingNG doc: "On Android WebView, there is a separate OS-level render thread for drawing because of how WebViews are embedded into a native app." The slides are from 2014; the functor API was reworked in Android Q (Vulkan support) but the model persists. (https://docs.google.com/presentation/d/1pYAGn2AYJ7neFDlDZ9DmLHpwMIskzMUXjFXYR7yfUko ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/browser/gfx/begin_frame_source_webview.h ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/browser/gfx/browser_view_renderer.h ; https://developer.chrome.com/docs/chromium/renderingng-architecture) => WebView is neither SurfaceView nor TextureView: it is a normal View rasterised into the activity's window surface each HWUI frame. Any web-side frame that misses the RenderThread deadline slips a whole Android frame; nothing in JS can shorten the HWUI->SurfaceFlinger->display tail.
- [high] Chromium/WebView engineers state WebView is expected to be slower than Chrome for architectural reasons, and Chrome-only input-path work (InputVizard) does not apply to WebView. - Bo Liu (chromium-dev, 1 Apr 2016): "In general, I'd expect webview to be slower than chrome": "Frame scheduling is dictated by android, which generally do not match chrome's scheduling assumptions, which generally leads to worse performance"; "The EGLImage cross context/thread texture sharing can only slow things down"; "The draw functor GL state save/restore could be a lot more efficient if webview cooporated with HWUI". Bo Liu (android-webview-dev, 2 Jan 2018): "The work on Chrome_InProcGpu is definitely blocking RenderThread frame, which is when webview updates."; "There isn't any better way to put webview on top of a surface view either." Google blog (2026): "Chrome on Android now receives both input events and VSync signals on the Viz compositor thread in the GPU process"; "we reduced the frequency of janky scrolls in Chrome on Android by 48%" - stated for Chrome on Android only. components/input/features.cc: kInputOnViz ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT (Chrome); WebView runs GPU service in-process (architecture.md: "WebView runs other services (ex. GPU service, Network Service) in-process on all OS versions"). No quantitative WebView-vs-Chrome latency figures exist in primary sources. (https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/chromium-dev/c/wMiF0L2RdX8 ; https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/android-webview-dev/c/fu40t1ha2Tc ; https://blog.google/chromium/smoother-scrolling-how-we-halved-scroll-jank-in-chrome-on-android/ ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/docs/architecture.md) => Set expectations: 2.0 can remove the app's own per-frame costs and the sample loss, but a WebView will not match Chrome's or a native Ink surface's latency floor. If 'as good as Samsung Notes' is required, only a native overlay (Jetpack Ink / front-buffer SurfaceView via a Tauri plugin) gets there.
- [high] WebView's renderer runs as a separate sandboxed process since Android 8.0; renderer priority is settable; render-thread priority tuning is deprecated. - Android 8.0 changes: "Your app's WebView objects now run in multiprocess mode. Web content is handled in a separate, isolated process from the containing app's process for enhanced security." architecture.md: browser code "runs in the app's process"; renderer "is sandboxed so it actually has even fewer permissions". WebView.java: `setRendererPriorityPolicy(@RendererPriority int rendererRequestedPriority, boolean waivedWhenNotVisible)` with RENDERER_PRIORITY_WAIVED=0 (BIND_WAIVE_PRIORITY), BOUND=1 (default service priority), IMPORTANT=2 (BIND_IMPORTANT). Android guide: a `true` waived flag "indicates that your app doesn't care whether the system keeps the renderer process alive". WebSettings.setRenderPriority: "Sets the priority of the Render thread... only needs to be called once per process" - deprecated: "It is not recommended to adjust thread priorities, and this will not be supported in future versions." (https://developer.android.com/about/versions/oreo/android-8.0-changes ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/webkit/WebView.java ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/webkit/WebSettings.java ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/layout/webapps/managing-webview) => setRendererPriorityPolicy(RENDERER_PRIORITY_IMPORTANT, false) is the only supported 'priority' knob and only matters under memory pressure; do not use setRenderPriority. Every input event crosses the app<->renderer process boundary in WebView too.
- [medium] WebView tile memory is a fixed per-WebView budget derived from the visible area, with no global limit. - Chromium WebView tile-memory doc: "bytes_limit_when_visible = visible rect size x constant" where the visible rect is "the portion of the webview intersecting with the screen"; multiplier 10 on Android K, 20 on L; results "rounded up to a multiple of 5M"; "no global limit, and no attempt to guess how much memory is available on the system"; tile priority CUTOFF_ALLOW_NICE_TO_HAVE rasters visible tiles plus those visible 'soon'; detached WebView -> "bytes_limit_when_visible is set to 0 so no tiles are allowed". Document is from the K/L era; constants may have changed. (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tlthhJmaRVc9_tsEIHdvBChP0Zm3n9qezcGj9uJumY4) => A single giant CSS-transformed #world full of SVG strokes rasterises into tiles that compete for roughly 20x-screen-area of tile memory at the current raster scale; when exceeded you get low-res/checkerboard tiles - one plausible source of the lasso-selection artefacts on a big area.
- [high] Compositor render surfaces (an extra offscreen pass every frame) are forced by backdrop-filter, filter, non-normal blend modes, translucent groups, rounded corners/masks, and by a 2D scale transform with two or more composited descendants. - cc/trees/effect_node.h RenderSurfaceReason: kBackdropScope ("Defines the scope of the backdrop for child blend mode or backdrop filter"), kBlendMode, kOpacity, kOpacityAnimation, kFilter, kFilterAnimation, kBackdropFilter, kBackdropFilterAnimation, kRoundedCorner, kClipPath, kMask, k2DScaleTransformWithCompositedDescendants, ... property_tree_builder.cc: `if (!layer->backdrop_filters().IsEmpty()) return kBackdropFilter;` `if (!layer->filters().IsEmpty()) return kFilter;` `if (layer->blend_mode() != SkBlendMode::kSrcOver) return kBlendMode;` and kOpacity when `may_have_transparency && at_least_two_layers_in_subtree_draw_content`. Blink property_tree_manager.cc: `if (... effect.needs_effect_for_2d_scale_transform && effect_layer_counts[id] >= 2 && !has_text[id] && !has_video[id]) effect.render_surface_reason = k2DScaleTransformWithCompositedDescendants;`. web.dev: "restrict animations to opacity and transform to keep animations on the compositing stage"; use will-change "only if you notice graphics issues". (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/cc/trees/effect_node.h ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/cc/trees/property_tree_builder.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/graphics/compositing/property_tree_manager.cc ; https://web.dev/articles/animations-guide) => The app sets `inkCv.style.mixBlendMode` on the full-stage live canvas for the highlighter (kBlendMode surface + kBackdropScope on the parent = a full-screen readback each frame while writing); backdrop-filter toolbars and drop-shadow filters on cards each add a pass; a CSS-scaled #world with composited children can itself acquire a surface. Cost is per pixel of the surface, not per stroke - avoid these on large elements.
- [high] Since Chrome 53 content is re-rastered whenever a script-driven transform scale changes, unless will-change: transform pins the raster scale (then it stays a fixed, possibly blurry bitmap). - Chrome blog: "Starting in Chrome 53, all content is re-rastered when its transform scale changes, if it does not have the will-change: transform CSS property." will-change: transform means content is "rastered into a fixed bitmap, which subsequently never changes under transform updates"; recommendation: add will-change only "when you need very fast (in other words, 60fps) transform animation speeds", e.g. Google-Maps-style zoom toggles it during the gesture. cc/layers/picture_layer_impl.cc: "Avoid frequent raster scale changes if we have an animating transform."; "Don't update will-change: transform layers if the raster contents scale is bigger than the minimum scale."; ideal contents scale capped at 10000. (https://developer.chrome.com/blog/re-rastering-composite ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/cc/layers/picture_layer_impl.cc) => Pinch-zooming a DOM #world holding thousands of SVG strokes re-rasters every tile at each scale step (cost proportional to on-screen content). Either toggle will-change: transform for the gesture and drop it at the end, or - the architecture-level answer - stop storing committed strokes as DOM/SVG.
- [high] Hardware acceleration is on by default for the Tauri Android app; WRY sets no View layer type and only six WebSettings; hardware layers add texture memory cost. - Android: "Hardware acceleration is enabled by default if your Target API level is >=14"; LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE "rendered in hardware into a hardware texture", "Because hardware layers consume video memory, it is highly recommended that you enable them only for the duration of the animation". Tauri's template AndroidManifest sets configChanges/launchMode/theme but "does not include android:hardwareAccelerated" (so the default applies). wry RustWebView.kt sets only: javaScriptEnabled, domStorageEnabled, setGeolocationEnabled(true), databaseEnabled, mediaPlaybackRequiresUserGesture=false, javaScriptCanOpenWindowsAutomatically; "No explicit layer type, debugging settings, or background color configurations". AwContents falls back to auxiliary-bitmap (software) rendering when `!AwContentsJni.get().hasRequiredHardwareExtensions()`. (https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/hardware-accel ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/dev/crates/tauri-cli/templates/mobile/android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/wry/blob/dev/src/android/kotlin/RustWebView.kt ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/java/src/org/chromium/android_webview/AwContents.java) => Nothing to fix here; explicitly do NOT call setLayerType(LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE) on the WebView (a full-screen extra texture copy per frame). The manifest flag can stay implicit or be set true for clarity.
- [high] Tauri exposes the Android WebView to app code through WryActivity.onWebViewCreate(webView); TauriActivity extends WryActivity and the generated MainActivity extends TauriActivity (this project already rewrites MainActivity in its build script). - wry WryActivity.kt: `open fun onWebViewCreate(webView: WebView) { }` ("allows subclasses to customize the WebView after it's created"), `open val handleBackNavigation: Boolean = true`. Tauri android-codegen TauriActivity.kt "extends WryActivity()" with `handleBackNavigation: Boolean = false` and lifecycle forwarding to PluginManager. wry main_pipe.rs creates `new RustWebView(activity, initScripts, id)`, calls `setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(devtools)` "conditional on debug or devtools feature", `setBackgroundColor`, `setUserAgentString`, then `activity.setContentView(webview)`. Tauri config: WindowConfig.devtools default true in debug / false in release; Tauri debug guide: "To enable the devtools in production builds, you must enable the `devtools` Cargo feature"; BundleConfig.android.minSdkVersion default 24. Local: Software/app/build-android.ps1 regenerates gen/android, then overwrites MainActivity.kt with `class MainActivity : TauriActivity()` (immersive bars, targetSdk 35). (https://github.com/tauri-apps/wry/blob/dev/src/android/kotlin/WryActivity.kt ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/blob/dev/crates/tauri/mobile/android-codegen/TauriActivity.kt ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/wry/blob/dev/src/android/main_pipe.rs ; https://v2.tauri.app/reference/config/ ; https://v2.tauri.app/develop/debug/ ; G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Software\app\build-android.ps1) => All native-side recommendations (unbuffered dispatch, renderer priority, refresh-rate hint, debugging toggle) go into an `override fun onWebViewCreate(webView: WebView)` in the MainActivity that build-android.ps1 already writes - no Tauri fork needed. tauri.conf.json itself has no WebView performance knobs for Android.
- [high] Remaining WebSettings of interest: offscreenPreRaster only matters for hidden WebViews; algorithmic darkening is off by default and prefers-color-scheme follows the app theme; force-dark is a no-op at targetSdk >= 33; enableSlowWholeDocumentDraw has 'significant performance cost'. - WebSettings.setOffscreenPreRaster: "Sets whether this WebView should raster tiles when it is offscreen but attached to a window." androidx WebSettingsCompat: "WebView always sets the media query prefers-color-scheme according to the app's theme attribute isLightTheme" (light if true/unspecified, else dark); algorithmic darkening "disallowed by default"; setForceDark deprecated: "The 'force dark' model previously implemented by WebView was complex and didn't interoperate well with current Web standards"; "In apps with targetSdkVersion >= TIRAMISU this API is a no-op". WebView.enableSlowWholeDocumentDraw disables rendering optimisations with "significant performance cost". setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled: "a security liability and should not be enabled in production builds". (https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/webkit/WebSettings.java ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/androidx-main/webkit/webkit/src/main/java/androidx/webkit/WebSettingsCompat.java ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/core/java/android/webkit/WebView.java) => Leave darkening disallowed (the app has its own dark theme and should declare <meta name=color-scheme content="dark light">); never call enableSlowWholeDocumentDraw; gate setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled behind a diagnostics setting rather than always-on in release.

Hit Record, then Stop"; recommended systrace categories for input/rendering: gfx (SurfaceFlinger/Vsync), view, input (--systrace gfx,view,input,freq,sched). (https://developer.chrome.com/docs/devtools/remote-debugging/webviews ; https://www.chromium.org/developers/how-tos/trace-event-profiling-tool/recording-tracing-runs/ ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/docs/developer-ui.md ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/docs/commandline-flags.md) => Measurement setup for 2.0: a release APK with a 'diagnostics' toggle that calls setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(true), then chrome://inspect Performance recordings during a stroke (see input -> main thread -> commit -> functor draw) and chrome://inspect?tracing with input, cc, viz, gpu + systrace gfx,view,input to see MotionEvent -> RenderThread -> SurfaceFlinger timing.
