(https://chromestatus.com/feature/6041426311774208 ; https://chromestatus.com/feature/5151468306956288 ; https://chromestatus.com/feature/5765569655603200 ; https://chromestatus.com/feature/6360971442388992 ; https://chromestatus.com/feature/5961434129235968 ; https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/PointerEvent.json ; https://github.com/mdn/browser-compat-data/blob/main/api/OffscreenCanvas.json ; https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2024/12/user-agent-reduction-on-android-webview.html ; https://www.apkmirror.com/apk/google-inc/android-system-webview/) => Any tablet with an updated WebView has every API 2.0 needs; the web Ink API is not an option on Android. Warning: from WebView 142 pointerrawupdate exists only in secure contexts - verify `window.isSecureContext` under Tauri's Android origin (tauri://localhost / http://tauri.localhost) or set useHttpsScheme; otherwise the raw channel silently vanishes.
- [medium] Chromium generates predicted pointer events unconditionally with a Kalman predictor, but resampling of pointer events is off by default; prediction attaches only to pointermove. - main_thread_event_queue.cc constructor: `event_predictor_ = std::make_unique<InputEventPrediction>(base::FeatureList::IsEnabled(blink::features::kResamplingInputEvents));`. input_event_prediction.cc default `selected_predictor_type_ = kScrollPredictorTypeKalman`; predicted points added "while (success)" until `last_event_timestamp_ + mouse_predictor_->MaxPredictionTime()`, step `touch_time_interval / touches_length` for touch. blink features.cc: kResamplingInputEvents DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT, kResamplingScrollEvents ENABLED_BY_DEFAULT (max resample 20 ms), kInputPredictorTypeChoice DISABLED_BY_DEFAULT. (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/main_thread_event_queue.cc ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/third_party/blink/renderer/platform/widget/input/input_event_prediction.cc ; https://source.chromium.org/chromium/chromium/src/+/main:third_party/blink/common/features.cc) => getPredictedEvents() on pointermove is populated on Android WebView and is the cheapest way to hide one frame of latency (draw a light predicted tail, erase on next real sample). Do not rely on Chromium resampling pen samples - it is off.
- [medium] WebView frame pacing follows the Android Choreographer, i.e. the window's current display mode; Samsung 'Power saving' forces Standard (60 Hz); the host can hint a preferred refresh rate. - begin_frame_source_webview.h: BeginFrames come "from Android Choreographer". Samsung US: Adaptive "will dynamically adjust the screen refresh rate, up to 120 Hz"; "When Power saving mode is enabled the screen will be set to Standard" (60 Hz). WindowManager.LayoutParams.preferredRefreshRate: "Starting API 34, this value is not limited to the supported refresh rates... Setting this value is the equivalent of calling Surface#setFrameRate with (preferred_frame_rate, FRAME_RATE_COMPATIBILITY_DEFAULT, CHANGE_FRAME_RATE_ONLY_IF_SEAMLESS)"; before API 34 it must be a supported rate. Surface.setFrameRate (API 30; 3-arg API 31): "the system may choose a display refresh rate to better match this surface's frame rate... calls to this function may result in changes to Choreographer callback timings"; 0 = accept system choice. Android 11 blog: at 90 Hz an app has "approximately 11.1ms per frame (versus 16.6ms at 60Hz)". Whether Samsung's Adaptive policy keeps a WebView app at 120 Hz during pen input without a hint is not documented (a Chromium desktop bug about 60 vs 120 Hz was behind a login wall). (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/android_webview/browser/gfx/begin_frame_source_webview.h ; https://www.samsung.com/us/support/answer/ANS10003396/ ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/android.views.windowmanagerlayoutparams.preferredrefreshrate ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/android.views.surface.setframerate ; https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2020/04/high-refresh-rate-rendering-on-android.html) => At 120 Hz the whole JS + style + paint + commit budget is ~8.3 ms; measure rAF cadence in the overlay and, in MainActivity, set `window.attributes = window.attributes.apply { preferredRefreshRate = 120f }` while a stroke is in progress (seamless-only), reverting afterwards to spare battery.
- [medium] Battery saver / reduced motion: Samsung Power saving also caps CPU performance; Android's Battery Saver policy can disable animations; Chromium Android maps prefers-reduced-motion to animator duration scale == 0 (the 'Remove animations' accessibility toggle). - Samsung HK: 'CPU power saving' = "Limit the maximum performance of the CPU to conserve battery power" (the 70% figure appears only in secondary sources). AOSP BatterySaverPolicy: KEY_DISABLE_ANIMATION "true if animation is disabled in battery saver mode", device-resource configurable (R.bool), along with brightness adjustment, quick doze, force-all-apps-standby. Chromium AccessibilityState.java: `@CalledByNative public static boolean prefersReducedMotion() { return getAnimatorDurationScale() == 0.0; }`; ui/gfx/animation/animation_android.cc: `prefers_reduced_motion_ = ui::AccessibilityState::PrefersReducedMotion();`. (https://www.samsung.com/hk_en/support/mobile-devices/how-do-i-configure-the-power-saving-mode-on-my-device/ ; https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/base/+/refs/heads/main/services/core/java/com/android/server/power/batterysaver/BatterySaverPolicy.java ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/ui/accessibility/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/accessibility/AccessibilityState.java) => When the user reports 'sudden' lag, Power saving (60 Hz + CPU cap) is a likely confounder; the overlay should show effective Hz and `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')`. Nothing in JS reveals battery-saver directly (navigator.getBattery is not a proxy).
- [high] Native alternatives that beat WebView latency exist only outside the WebView: Jetpack Ink API / front-buffer rendering on a SurfaceView; TextureView adds an extra composition. - Android: the Ink API "helps developers add beautiful, low-latency freehand drawing", uses the Jetpack Graphics library (front-buffer) and prediction, is Compose/Views native - not exposed to WebView JS. Front-buffered rendering: "The low-latency library enables apps to render directly to the front buffer... preventing buffer swapping"; do: "Small portions of the screen, handwriting, drawing"; don't: "Fullscreen update, panning, zooming". AOSP graphics doc: with TextureView "every visible pixel is composited twice" and it goes through the app's UI rendering; SurfaceView uses a separate layer/overlay; "implementing SurfaceView in API 24+ for most use cases". Chromestatus: Delegated Ink Trails not shipped on Android/WebView. (https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/stylus-input/about-ink-api ; https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/views/touch-and-input/stylus-input/advanced-stylus-features ; https://source.android.com/docs/core/graphics/arch-tv) => If after the 2.0 web rewrite the pen still feels behind Samsung Notes, the next step is a Tauri Android plugin that overlays a SurfaceView (Jetpack Ink / GLFrontBufferedRenderer) for the live stroke and hands finished strokes to the web layer - the only way to reach front-buffer latency from this stack.
- [high] What an in-app diagnostics overlay can measure from JS in WebView. - (1) Input arrival latency: EventForwarder stamps events with the oldest hardware time ("We want to use the oldest event time as hardware time stamp"), so `performance.now() - e.timeStamp` in a pointerrawupdate handler = OS batching + IPC + queue delay per sample; (2) samples per second and coalesced-list sizes (detects buffered vs unbuffered dispatch: ~60-120/s vs pen rate); (3) rAF timestamp deltas = effective refresh rate / dropped frames; (4) Long Animation Frames: `new PerformanceObserver(...).observe({type:'long-animation-frame', buffered:true})` for frames >50 ms with `blockingDuration`, `renderStart`, `styleAndLayoutStart`, `scripts[]` attribution (Chrome/WebView 123+); (5) Event Timing (`type:'event', durationThreshold:16`) for pointerdown/up but NOT pointermove/pointerrawupdate - MDN: "NOT covered: mousemove, pointermove, pointerrawupdate, touchmove, wheel, drag"; `duration` is rounded to 8 ms; (6) `ctx.getContextAttributes().desynchronized`; (7) `navigator.userAgent` 'wv' + 'Chrome/NNN' for WebView version (UA-CH in WebView 116+); (8) `matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')`, devicePixelRatio, `document.getAnimations().length`. Not measurable from JS: display refresh mode name, battery saver state, RenderThread/SurfaceFlinger timing (use chrome://inspect?tracing + systrace). (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/ui/android/java/src/org/chromium/ui/base/EventForwarder.java ; https://developer.chrome.com/docs/web-platform/long-animation-frames ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/PerformanceEventTiming ; https://developer.chrome.com/blog/desynchronized ; https://android-developers.googleblog.com/2024/12/user-agent-reduction-on-android-webview.html) => Defines the 2.0 diagnostics overlay: pen samples/s, per-sample input latency histogram (p50/p95), frame rate + dropped frames, LoAF count and worst blockingDuration, longest pointer handler, desynchronized flag, dpr, WebView version, reduced-motion flag - enough to prove or disprove each of the three complaints on the user's tablet before and after each change.
- [high] The current app already uses the right web APIs but in a way that cannot survive WebView's input model. - Interface/js/app.js: `inkCtx = inkCv.getContext('2d', { desynchronized: true, alpha: true })` (line 4132), `inkDpr = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2.5)` (4140), `inkCv.style.mixBlendMode = st.blend` on the full-stage live canvas (beginInkStroke), `paintLiveStroke()` rebuilds `new Path2D(inkStrokeD(pts,...))` for the whole stroke on every paint (scheduleLivePaint: immediate paint + one trailing rAF), samples from `pointerrawupdate` (7972-7973) and `pointermove.getCoalescedEvents()` with `if (!fromRaw && inking.rawSeen && t <= inking.lastT) continue;` de-dup (4222-4258). Given Chromium delivers identical WebTouchEvents on both channels, the de-dup is logically sound; the missing samples come from Android batching upstream. Whole-stroke repaint per sample makes per-sample cost O(stroke length) rather than constant. (G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Interface\js\app.js (lines 4124-4260, 7970-7974)) => 2.0 should: request unbuffered dispatch natively; treat pointerrawupdate as the single sample source; paint only the new segment (dirty-rect) on the live canvas; drop mix-blend-mode from the live layer (apply blend when committing); keep the DPR cap.

Recommendations:
- Native input fix (highest value, smallest change): in the MainActivity that build-android.ps1 writes, `override fun onWebViewCreate(webView: WebView)` and add `webView.setOnTouchListener { v, e -> if (e.actionMasked == MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN && e.getToolType(0) == MotionEvent.TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS) v.requestUnbufferedDispatch(e); false }` (API 21; the same call Chrome makes and WebView omits because it uses synchronous compositing). Confirm with the diagnostics overlay that pen samples/s rises from the frame rate to the digitiser rate and that `getCoalescedEvents().length` on pointerrawupdate drops to ~1. Keep fingers on Android's default buffered/resampled path.
- Sample intake in 2.0: make pointerrawupdate (iterating getCoalescedEvents) the only source of committed points; use pointermove solely for getPredictedEvents() to draw an erasable predicted tail. Remove the timestamp de-dup. Guard against WebView 142+ hiding pointerrawupdate in insecure contexts: check `window.isSecureContext` on the Android build and set `useHttpsScheme`/fix the origin if false, with a pointermove-only fallback.
- Live ink surface: keep `desynchronized: true` (in WebView it disables rAF-alignment of move events and gives the canvas its own frame sink; check `getContextAttributes().desynchronized`). Paint incrementally - only the new segment / dirty rect - so per-sample cost is constant; never rebuild the whole Path2D per sample. Do not put mix-blend-mode, filter or opacity on the full-stage canvas (each forces a compositor render surface = full-screen extra pass per frame); apply highlighter blending when the stroke is committed. Keep DPR capped (2.5 or lower on 120 Hz).
- Committed content: stop representing each stroke as a DOM <div><svg><path>. Rasterise committed strokes into tiled canvases (or one OffscreenCanvas-backed tile set per card/viewport region, WebView 69+), keep the vector data in the file, and redraw tiles only when their content or the zoom level changes. This makes per-frame cost proportional to visible pixels, not to stroke count, and sidesteps Chrome 53+ re-rasterisation of every SVG tile during script-driven pinch zoom. If parts of #world must stay DOM, toggle `will-change: transform` on for the duration of a pinch and remove it at gesture end so text re-rasters crisp.
- Selection and move: draw the lasso and selection highlight on an overlay canvas instead of toggling classes on hundreds of DOM elements; during a drag apply one transform to a single group layer (or redraw the selected strokes on the overlay) and commit positions once on release; never call getBoundingClientRect per element per frame (layout thrash). Avoid backdrop-filter, filter: drop-shadow and translucent groups on large or numerous elements - they add render surfaces whose cost scales with area on the tablet GPU.
- Android host configuration (all in onWebViewCreate / MainActivity): `WebView.setWebContentsDebuggingEnabled(BuildConfig.DEBUG || diagnosticsEnabled)`; `webView.setRendererPriorityPolicy(WebView.RENDERER_PRIORITY_IMPORTANT, false)`; do NOT call setRenderPriority (deprecated), setLayerType(LAYER_TYPE_HARDWARE) or enableSlowWholeDocumentDraw; leave algorithmic darkening off and ship <meta name="color-scheme" content="dark light">; keep android:hardwareAccelerated at its default (true); while a stroke is active set `window.attributes.preferredRefreshRate = 120f` (seamless-only semantics from API 34; equivalent to Surface.setFrameRate) and restore 0 afterwards. offscreenPreRaster is irrelevant for a single visible WebView.
- Measurement setup: (a) ship a hidden diagnostics overlay that reports pen samples/s, `performance.now() - e.timeStamp` p50/p95 per sample, effective Hz from rAF deltas and dropped-frame count, LoAF entries (count, worst blockingDuration, top script attribution), longest pointer handler, `desynchronized` flag, dpr, WebView version from the UA ('wv', 'Chrome/NNN'), prefers-reduced-motion; (b) for deep dives enable debugging, open chrome://inspect (Performance panel) and chrome://inspect?tracing with categories input, cc, viz, gpu, blink plus systrace gfx,view,input to see MotionEvent -> renderer -> functor draw -> SurfaceFlinger; (c) use WebView DevTools (`adb shell am start -a com.android.webview.SHOW_DEV_UI`) to flip Chromium features for A/B experiments (e.g. UseAndroidBufferedInputDispatch) without a rooted device.
- Test matrix for acceptance: Samsung tablet in Adaptive 120 Hz vs Power saving (60 Hz + CPU cap), with and without 'Remove animations'; record samples/s, input latency p95 and LoAF count for a 10 s fast scribble, a 2000-stroke page pan/zoom, and a lasso-move of 500 strokes; target constant per-frame cost (<8 ms at 120 Hz) independent of page content.
- Contingency: if after the above the pen still trails Samsung Notes, plan a Tauri Android plugin that overlays a SurfaceView using Jetpack Ink / GLFrontBufferedRenderer for the in-progress stroke (front-buffer rendering + native prediction) and hands the finished stroke to the web layer; the web Ink API (Delegated Ink Trails) will not arrive on Android WebView.
Open questions:
- Repo-wide confirmation that no Chromium code path expands MotionEvent historical samples into coalesced web events (three touch-path files were read; grep.app/Sourcegraph were rate-limited/blocked).
- Does calling requestUnbufferedDispatch on the WebView container interfere with WebView's own pinch/scroll gesture handling or its stylus-writing controller on Samsung firmware? Android warns of 'jittery scrolls'; restricting to TOOL_TYPE_STYLUS should avoid it but needs a device test.
- Is `window.isSecureContext` true under the Tauri Android origin (tauri://localhost or http://tauri.localhost)? If not, pointerrawupdate disappears once the tablet's WebView reaches 142.
- Does the desynchronized canvas's separate frame sink yield any measurable latency gain inside WebView's in-process viz/functor path, or only the rAF-alignment bypass? Measure with and without the hint.
- Does Samsung's Adaptive refresh policy keep a WebView-based app at 120 Hz during pen input without a preferredRefreshRate/setFrameRate hint? (Only forum reports found; measure rAF cadence.)
- Exact Android System WebView version on the user's tablet and whether WebView Beta/Canary changes behaviour (WebView DevTools requires a non-stable channel for the launcher icon).
- Current values of the WebView tile-memory multiplier (the primary document dates from Android K/L).

## storage
Notes Gallery today keeps every stroke as its own IndexedDB record but writes it through a one-transaction-per-record layer, loads a level by reading the whole workspace through the `ws` index, and autosaves by re-serialising the entire workspace (attachments re-encoded as base64 data URLs) into one JSON string on the main thread and rewriting the whole file (Tauri `writeTextFile` or FSA `createWritable`). Primary sources establish that (a) IndexedDB's structured clone runs synchronously on the calling thread at `put()` time and web.dev's rule is to store individual records and only touch what changed; (b) transaction count, not bytes, dominates IDB write cost (RxDB measured 1k docs: 80 ms in one transaction vs ~2 s with one transaction per write; Dexie's bulkPut is just "one transaction, listen to the last onsuccess"); (c) Chrome's default durability is already relaxed since Chrome 121 (3–30x faster commits) so nothing to gain there; (d) Chromium wraps any value > 64 KiB into a Blob and caps a single IDB message at 128 MB, so records should stay small and a whole-page blob is the wrong unit; (e) IndexedDB works in Web Workers, but Tauri's `invoke` does not (issue #12277 closed "not planned"), so the file write must be triggered from the main thread even if the bytes are produced in a worker; (f) Tauri 2's fs plugin already moves bytes as raw IPC bodies (TextEncoder → raw body; `read_text_file` returns `ipc::Response` bytes decoded by TextDecoder) and exposes `open()/FileHandle.write()` for chunked writes; a maintainer reports ~10 MB binary IPC at ~5 ms on macOS but ~200 ms on Windows; (g) on Android the dialog returns `content://` URIs, the fs plugin opens them through ContentResolver with mode "wt" (write+truncate) and there is no atomic rename, so a crash mid-write can truncate the user's file; (h) FSA `createWritable` writes to a swap file and commits on `close()`, and permission does not survive reload without a gesture; (i) tldraw, Excalidraw, Obsidian and Logseq all keep a small-record live store (IDB records / localStorage JSON / SQLite-in-OPFS) with 300–350 ms debounced delta writes, and serialise a single-file JSON snapshot ({schema, records[]} / {elements, appState, files}) only for export. Recommended design: keep `.notesgallery.json` v2 exactly as the on-disk/export format; move all IDB traffic into a dedicated storage worker that batches deltas into one readwrite transaction per frame; make autosave O(delta) by caching each record's serialised JSON string and assembling the file from cached chunks (Blob parts on web, chunked `FileHandle.write` on Tauri); rewrite the full file only on idle/visibility-change with a 10–30 s cap, guarded by a last-good copy in app storage on Android; add a `[ws,parentId]` index plus maintained child-count records so level load is O(level) not O(workspace); bulk-import in one transaction per store; request `navigator.storage.persist()`.

Findings:
- [high] ESTABLISHED (code): the current DB layer opens one readwrite transaction per record, and every bulk path fans out N transactions. - db.js `put()` does `db.transaction(store,'readwrite').objectStore(store).put(value)` per call; `DB.saveBlock = (b) => put('blocks', b)`. app.js `putAll()` (line ~1512) maps `recs.blocks.map(b => DB.saveBlock(b))`, so an undo/redo of 500 strokes is 500 commits; `createWorkspaceFromData()` (line ~6848) does `for (const b of data.blocks) await DB.saveBlock(nb)` — sequential, one commit each; `loadLevel()` (line ~499) issues 2 more transactions per visible block for child counts. `deleteBlockDeep`/`deleteWorkspaceDeep` do the same. The comment 'all requests are created in one task, so IndexedDB orders them as written' is true (same-scope transactions run in creation order) but each still commits separately. (G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Interface\js\db.js (lines 96-121, 130-145) and js\app.js (lines 1511-1531, 6848-6862, 487-505)) => Transaction count is the dominant IDB cost (see RxDB numbers below). For Notes Gallery 2.0 this is the single biggest storage win: one transaction per batch of deltas, and one per store on import.
- [high] ESTABLISHED (code): autosave re-serialises the entire workspace on the main thread and rewrites the whole file every time. - `workspacePayload()` (app.js ~6145) reads ALL blocks/edges/files via `DB.allByWs`, converts every attachment Blob to a base64 data URL (`FileReader.readAsDataURL`, cached up to `FILE_CACHE_BUDGET = 32 MiB`, beyond which 'attachments are re-encoded per save'), spreads every block, then `JSON.stringify(payload)`. `saveWorkspaceNow()` writes via `NGShell.writeFile(rec.path, JSON.stringify(payload))` (Tauri) or `writeToHandle()` → `handle.createWritable()` (no `keepExistingData`) + `write(new Blob([JSON.stringify(payload, null, 0)]))` + `close()`. Scheduling: `markChanged()` debounces 900 ms with a 10 s deadline and `autosaveTick()` defers while any gesture flag is live. (G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Interface\js\app.js lines 6132-6170, 6837-6841, 6921-7040) => Save cost is O(whole workspace) per save regardless of what changed, and it runs on the same thread as pointer handling. The gesture guard hides it during a stroke but not between words; with big workspaces the 10 s deadline forces it mid-session.
- [medium] ESTABLISHED (code) + INFERRED (size): a stroke record is 2.5–5 KB of JSON for a 200-point stroke; a dense handwriting page is several MB. - `finalizeInk()` stores `pts` as `[x,y]` rounded to 0.05 plus pressure (2 dp) and nib factor (3 dp) when present, e.g. `[123.45,67.9,0.53,0.412]` ≈ 23 chars + comma per point → ~4.8 KB per 200-point stroke with pressure/taper, ~2.6 KB without. INFERRED: 1,500–3,000 strokes per handwritten page → 4–15 MB of JSON per level; a 10-page workspace ≈ 40–150 MB before attachments. Each record is well under Chromium's 64 KiB wrap threshold (good), but the whole-workspace string is what autosave stringifies and ships across IPC each time. (G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Interface\js\app.js lines 5717-5740 (finalizeInk) and 4248-4250 (sample push)) => Sets the scale for every cost below: per-stroke work is tiny (a few KB clone/stringify), whole-workspace work is tens of MB. The design must make autosave proportional to the former.
- [high] ESTABLISHED (code): root-level load reads the entire workspace's blocks/edges through the `ws` index and filters in JS. - db.js `childBlocks(parentId, ws)`: `if (parentId === ROOT) return allByWs('blocks', ws).then(l => l.filter(b => b.parentId === ROOT))`; same for `levelEdges`. Nested levels use the `parentId` index (fine). Then `loadLevel` runs `DB.childBlocks(b.id)` and `DB.blockFiles(b.id)` for every block on the level to compute `childCounts`/`childPeek`. (G:\Other computers\My Computer\LAT_WorkSpace\Notes\Interface\js\db.js lines 130-137; js\app.js lines 493-505) => Opening the home canvas costs O(all strokes in the workspace) of structured-clone deserialisation, plus 2 transactions per visible block. A compound `[ws, parentId]` index (or a per-level index key) and a maintained `levelStats` record make it O(level).
- [high] ESTABLISHED: IndexedDB serialises (structured-clones) the value synchronously on the calling thread when put() is called; web.dev's guidance is small individual records, update only what changed. - web.dev: "The structured cloning process happens on the main thread. The larger the object, the longer the blocking time will be." and "Reads and writes to IndexedDB shouldn't be larger than required for the data being accessed"; avoid storing an entire state tree as one record — "break it up into individual records and only update the records that actually change." The W3C spec defines record values as output of StructuredSerializeForStorage (section 'Clone a value') and states "Requests must be executed in the order in which they were made against the transaction." (https://web.dev/articles/indexeddb-best-practices ; https://w3c.github.io/IndexedDB/) => Confirms the per-stroke-record model is right and a 'one blob per page' model is wrong. Because the clone happens where put() is called, moving the DB layer into a worker moves that CPU cost off the input thread.
- [medium] ESTABLISHED (measured by RxDB team): one transaction per write is ~25x slower than one transaction for the batch; in-memory state + background persistence is ~100x faster than direct IDB reads/writes. - RxDB: "Inserting 1k documents into IndexedDB takes about 80 milliseconds, 0.08ms per document" in one transaction, but "Inserting 1k documents with one transaction per write, takes about 2 seconds." Also: relaxed durability gives minor gains mainly for many small transactions; explicit `commit()` gives a small gain; sharding across stores gave 28–43% faster batched cursors; a leader election adds ~150 ms startup. Hardware not specified — treat the ratio, not the absolute, as the takeaway. (https://rxdb.info/slow-indexeddb.html) => Directly quantifies the cost of the current `DB.saveBlock`-per-record pattern for undo/redo, lasso-move (which calls `DB.saveBlock` per moved stroke), paste and import. Batch-per-frame writes fix all four.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Dexie's bulkPut and the idb library add no magic — bulkPut is 'issue all puts in one transaction and listen only to the last onsuccess'; idb is a ~1.2 kB promise wrapper with no batching. - Dexie docs: "bulkPut() is faster than doing put() in a loop. Mainly because it only has to get called back for the last onsuccess of all indexedDB requests." Source (dbcore-indexeddb.ts `mutate`): loops `store[type](args1[i], args2[i])`, attaches `req.onerror = errorHandler` to each and `req.onsuccess = done` only to the last, then collects `req.error` per request. Caveat: "If you call bulkPut() outside a transaction scope and an error occur on one of the operations, the successful operations will still be persisted." idb README: "a tiny (~1.19kB brotli'd) library that mostly mirrors the IndexedDB API"; "Do not await other things between the start and end of your transaction, otherwise the transaction will close before you're done." (https://dexie.org/docs/Table/Table.bulkPut() ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/dexie/Dexie.js/master/src/dbcore/dbcore-indexeddb.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/jakearchibald/idb/main/README.md) => A hand-rolled layer (as db.js already is) is sufficient for 2.0 provided it: opens one transaction per batch, resolves on `tx.oncomplete` (or last request), never awaits non-IDB promises inside a transaction (db.js currently awaits `open()` before creating the tx — fine — but callers must not interleave), and reports per-request errors.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Chrome's default IndexedDB durability has been 'relaxed' since Chrome 121 (3x–30x faster commits); 'strict' forces an fsync per transaction. - Chrome blog: strict "explicitly instructs the OS to flush changes to disk before issuing the complete event"; relaxed "issues the complete event after changes make it to the OS buffer, which is typically flushed every couple seconds"; observed speedups "between a factor of 3 and 30"; "The Chrome team recommends taking no action for the vast majority of developers." MDN/spec: `transaction(stores, mode, { durability })` with values strict/relaxed/default; MDN notes IDBDatabase.transaction "is available in Web Workers." (https://developer.chrome.com/blog/indexeddb-durability-mode-now-defaults-to-relaxed ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/IDBDatabase/transaction) => Nothing to gain by setting durability; do NOT set 'strict' on the hot path. Data safety comes from the .notesgallery.json file + journal, not from IDB fsyncs.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Chromium wraps any IDB value larger than 64 KiB (after Snappy compression) into a separate Blob, and caps one IDB IPC message at 128 MB. - indexeddb.mojom: `const int32 kIDBWrapThreshold = 65536;` — "Default threshold for IDBValueWrapper::WrapIfBiggerThan(). This should be tuned to achieve a compromise between short-term IndexedDB throughput and long-term I/O load and memory usage."; `kIDBMaxMessageSize = 134217728` ("128MB"); `kIDBMaxMessageOverhead = 1048576`. idb_value_wrapping.cc: "Values that are still 'large' after compression are converted into a Blob"; compression is skipped "for less compressible or jumbo data". (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/third_party/blink/public/mojom/indexeddb/indexeddb.mojom ; https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/third_party/blink/renderer/modules/indexeddb/idb_value_wrapping.cc) => Keep records < 64 KiB (individual strokes are); do not store a whole page's strokes as one record or a whole-workspace JSON snapshot as one record (a 100+ MB record would hit the 128 MB message cap). A `getAll()` over a huge index is also chunked internally — prefer per-level reads.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Chromium is replacing IndexedDB's LevelDB+flat-files backend with SQLite, shipping in Chrome 150 on desktop, Android and WebView; Chrome 150 went stable 30 June 2026. Existing databases stay on LevelDB until a later migration step. - blink-dev PSA: "Chromium's IndexedDB implementation is rewritten on top of SQLite, to replace the previous implementation that uses a hybrid of LevelDB and flat files… expected to improve reliability and, to a lesser extent, performance." Rollout: step 1 in-memory contexts, step 2 "applied to new data stores… existing data stored in LevelDB is unimpacted", step 3 migrate existing data. Milestones: DevTrial 148, shipping 150 (desktop, Android, WebView). Chrome 150 stable: 30 June 2026. (https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/jS0khnC5IWA ; https://chromereleases.googleblog.com/2026/) => INFERRED: an up-to-date Samsung WebView is ≥150, so a 2.0 database created under a NEW name lands on the SQLite backend, while the existing 'blocknotes' DB stays on LevelDB until step 3. If 2.0 renames its DB (e.g. for the schema rewrite) and imports from the old one, it gets the new backend for free; verify the WebView version on the tablet.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: `getAllRecords()` and `direction` for `getAll()/getAllKeys()` ship in Chrome 141 (desktop, Android, WebView) and replace cursor round-trips for batched reads. - Intent to Ship: "This functionality enables certain read patterns to be significantly faster when compared to the existing alternative of iteration with cursors. One key workload from a Microsoft property showed a 350ms improvement." `getAllRecords()` returns primary key + value (+ index key on an index) so the next batch can start after the last key without an IDBCursor. Estimated milestones: desktop 141, Android 141, WebView 141. (https://groups.google.com/a/chromium.org/g/blink-dev/c/h9Xk1cFORh0/m/2YEUbwsTAwAJ) => For level loads and exports, use `index.getAll(range)` / `getAllRecords` in batches (e.g. 2,000 records) with a `[ws,parentId,id]`-style key range rather than cursors or one `getAll` of the whole workspace; feature-detect and fall back to `getAll`.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: IndexedDB is best-effort storage — an origin's data can be evicted wholesale under storage pressure unless `navigator.storage.persist()` is granted; Excalidraw's maintainer observes IDB evicted 'more often than localStorage'. - MDN: Chromium lets an origin use "up to 60% of the total disk size"; best-effort data is evicted by LRU under storage pressure and "when an origin's data is evicted, all its data is deleted at once"; persistent mode (via `navigator.storage.persist()`) is "only evicted if user chooses to delete via browser settings". Excalidraw PR #9810 review (dwelle): "IndexedDB is being evicted by browsers more often than localStorage e.g. in low disk space cases (likely the reason why people tend to lose images but not elements)." That PR (move elements to idb) is still an open draft (created 2025-07-31, state open, draft true, merged false). (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Storage_API/Storage_quotas_and_eviction_criteria ; https://github.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/pull/9810 ; https://api.github.com/repos/excalidraw/excalidraw/pulls/9810) => The linked `.notesgallery.json` file is the durable copy; IDB is a cache/working set. 2.0 should call `navigator.storage.persist()`, and its save design must guarantee the file (plus journal) is always sufficient to rebuild the IDB — which the existing import path already does.
- [medium] ESTABLISHED (secondary report of Surma's measurements): postMessage cost is roughly proportional to the JSON size of the payload; ≤100 KiB stays within a 100 ms budget even on a Nokia 2, and ≤10 KiB is 'risk-free' for JS-driven animation. - InfoQ summarising Surma (Chrome team): "The stringified JSON representation of an object is roughly proportional to its transfer time" (relevant only above ~100 KiB); "Even on the slowest devices, you can postMessage() objects up to 100KiB and stay within your 100ms response budget"; "If you have JS-driven animations, payloads up to 10KiB are risk-free." Devices: 2018 MacBook Pro, Pixel 3XL, Nokia 2. The primary page (surma.dev) returned 403 to the fetcher, so this is quoted via InfoQ. (https://www.infoq.com/news/2019/08/postMessage-performance-study (primary: https://surma.dev/things/is-postmessage-slow/)) => A per-stroke delta (2–5 KB) posted to a storage worker is far under the 10 KiB animation-safe budget; posting a whole workspace (MBs) is not. Transfer ArrayBuffers (zero-copy) for the assembled file bytes coming back from the worker.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Tauri APIs (`window.__TAURI__` / `invoke`) are not available inside Web Workers, and the feature request is closed 'not planned'. - Issue #12277 (opened 2025-01-06): "Web workers are severely limited since they cannot utilize Tauri APIs. They only work for isolated tasks without IO or state persistence." Status: closed as not planned, label type: feature request. Community workaround: delegate to the main thread via postMessage. (https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/issues/12277) => In the Windows/Android apps the storage worker can own IndexedDB and build the file bytes, but the final `invoke('plugin:fs|write_file'…)` must be issued from the main thread. Transfer the Uint8Array (transferable) so the main-thread share is a ~µs hand-off plus the IPC itself.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Tauri 2's fs plugin already uses raw IPC bodies: `writeTextFile` TextEncodes in JS and sends bytes with path/options in headers; `read_text_file` returns `tauri::ipc::Response` bytes that JS decodes with TextDecoder; `open()`/`FileHandle.write()` give chunked writes. - guest-js index.ts: `const encoder = new TextEncoder(); await invoke('plugin:fs|write_text_file', encoder.encode(data), { headers: {...} })`; `readTextFile`: `const arr = await invoke<ArrayBuffer | number[]>('plugin:fs|read_text_file', …); … return new TextDecoder(options?.encoding ?? 'utf-8').decode(bytes)`; `writeFile` with a ReadableStream loops `await file.write(value)`; `FileHandle.write`: `invoke('plugin:fs|write', { rid: this.rid, data })`, plus `ftruncate`, `seek`. Rust commands.rs: `read_file_inner` → `Ok(tauri::ipc::Response::new(contents))`; `write_file_inner` accepts `InvokeBody::Raw(data)` (or a JSON number array fallback) and builds OpenOptions `{ write: true, create: true, truncate: !opts.append, append }` (default `truncate: true`). This landed in PR #1251 'feat(fs): use IPC streaming for read and write file' (merged 30 May 2024). Tauri docs: "Return values that implements serde::Serialize are serialized to JSON… This can slow down your application if you try to return a large data such as a file"; use `tauri::ipc::Response` / pass `ArrayBuffer|Uint8Array` as the invoke payload. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/v2/plugins/fs/guest-js/index.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/v2/plugins/fs/src/commands.rs ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/pull/1251 ; https://v2.tauri.app/develop/calling-rust/) => The current `NGShell.writeFile(path, string)` path is already binary over IPC — the remaining costs are (1) `JSON.stringify` of the whole workspace on the main thread, (2) `TextEncoder.encode` of a multi-MB string (a full copy), (3) the IPC transfer itself. 2.0 can hand the worker-built Uint8Array straight to `writeFile`/`FileHandle.write` and skip (1) and (2) on the main thread. Confirm the plugin's JS and Rust versions match (the PR notes mismatched versions break).
- [medium] ESTABLISHED (maintainer statement, informal benchmark): Tauri 2 binary IPC moves ~10 MB in ~5 ms on macOS but ~200 ms on Windows; it rides the WebView's fetch machinery and 'can't improve much further' with system webviews. An earlier discussion reported plugin-fs ~100x slower than the browser File System API for reads. - Discussion #11915 (FabianLars): "A user on discord did some non-scientific benchmark for the binary ipc and got ~5ms on macOS but ~200ms on Windows" (10 MB); "Tauri has a serialization-free ipc in v2 too but it still uses the fetch api (without actually hitting the network) so there's a bit of a delay"; "With the currently used system webviews i believe that we can't improve it much further." Discussion #9700 (FabianLars): "Because the IPC, the communication channel between rust (where the plugin-fs is actually implemented) and js is pretty slow" — v2 improved on v1 and PR #1251 was pending at the time. (https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/discussions/11915 ; https://github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/discussions/9700) => INFERRED budget: a 20 MB whole-workspace rewrite on Windows costs on the order of 0.4 s of IPC alone, on Android unknown (measure). That is acceptable only when it happens rarely and off the input path — hence: incremental journal appends (KBs) frequently, full rewrite rarely and only at idle.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: on Android the Tauri dialog returns `content://` URIs; the fs plugin opens them through ContentResolver.openAssetFileDescriptor with a mode built from OpenOptions ('w'+'t' = "wt" for a default write); Android documents that "w" may or may not truncate but "wt" does. There is no atomic rename over a content URI. - Tauri dialog docs: "On iOS, a file://<path> URIs are returned. On Android, content URIs are returned"; "The filesystem plugin works with any path format out of the box." fs lib.rs: `fn android_mode(&self) -> String { … if self.read { mode.push('r') } if self.write { mode.push('w') } if self.truncate { mode.push('t') } if self.append { mode.push('a') } mode }`; FsPlugin.kt: `activity.contentResolver.openAssetFileDescriptor(Uri.parse(args.uri), args.mode)?.parcelFileDescriptor?.detachFd()`. Android docs (mirror): mode "Can be 'r', 'w', 'wt', 'wa', 'rw' or 'rwt'. Please note the exact implementation of these may differ for each Provider implementation - for example, 'w' may or may not truncate." Notes Gallery stores the URI string via `DB.savePathRec(wsId, path)` and rewrites through it on every autosave. (https://v2.tauri.app/plugin/dialog/ ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/v2/plugins/fs/src/lib.rs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tauri-apps/plugins-workspace/v2/plugins/fs/android/src/main/java/FsPlugin.kt ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/android.content.contentresolver.openfiledescriptor?view=net-android-34.0) => INFERRED risk: the current whole-file rewrite opens the user's document with "wt" and streams bytes; if the app is killed mid-write (Android does this to backgrounded WebViews) the file is truncated with no backup. 2.0 should keep the last-good full copy + journal in `appDataDir` (a real path, where temp-file + `rename` is possible) and treat the content:// document as a mirror written at idle. Also watch for SAF providers that ignore 't'; write-then-verify-length is cheap insurance.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: File System Access API `createWritable()` writes to a temporary/swap file and only replaces the target on `close()`; `keepExistingData` copies the old file first; permission is not reliably persisted across sessions and `requestPermission` needs user activation; `showSaveFilePicker` needs a secure context + transient activation and is 'limited availability'. - WHATWG FS spec: "This is typically implemented by writing data to a temporary file, and only replacing the file entry… with the temporary file when the writable filestream is closed"; "If keepExistingData is false or not specified, the temporary file starts out empty, otherwise the existing file is first copied to this temporary file." MDN adds modes 'exclusive' and 'siloed'. Chrome docs: "Changes are not written to disk until the stream is closed"; "permissions are not always persisted between sessions"; "The web app can continue to save changes to the file without prompting until all tabs for its origin are closed." Notes Gallery already documents this caveat (Ctrl+S once after reload). (https://fs.spec.whatwg.org/ ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/FileSystemFileHandle/createWritable ; https://developer.chrome.com/docs/capabilities/web-apis/file-system-access ; https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/showSaveFilePicker) => On the web build every autosave is inherently a full-file write plus an atomic replace — incremental in-place patching of the user's file is not possible (and `keepExistingData: true` would add a full copy). So web autosave = journal to OPFS/IDB frequently + full rewrite to the handle at idle. FileSystemFileHandle objects are structured-cloneable, so the worker can hold the handle and do the write itself (verify in the target Chrome/Edge).
- [high] ESTABLISHED: OPFS `createSyncAccessHandle()` is available in Chrome/Chrome Android/WebView 114+, Firefox 127+, Safari 18.2+, dedicated workers only, with in-place synchronous writes and `flush()`; sqlite-wasm requires a worker for OPFS persistence. - MDN compat: Chrome 114, Chrome Android 114, WebView Android 114, Firefox 127, Safari 18.2; "This feature is only available in Dedicated Web Workers"; writes are in place (`accessHandle.write(content, { at: size }); … accessHandle.flush()`). WHATWG: sync handles "allow for higher performance on contexts where asynchronous operations come with high overhead, e.g., WebAssembly." sqlite-wasm README: "Only the worker versions allow you to use the origin private file system (OPFS) storage back-end" and the 'opfs' VFS needs COOP/COEP headers (`Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`, `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp`). PowerSync's 2026 survey: IDB-backed SQLite "performance degrades with larger databases (100MB+)" while OPFS-based keeps performing "even for databases over 1GB"; Chrome incognito imposes a 100 MB limit; Safari incognito has no OPFS. (https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/FileSystemFileHandle/createSyncAccessHandle ; https://fs.spec.whatwg.org/ ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sqlite/sqlite-wasm/main/README.md ; https://powersync.com/blog/sqlite-persistence-on-the-web) => OPFS in the storage worker is the natural home for an append-only autosave journal on the web build (and inside the Tauri WebView too, as a fallback): synchronous, in-place appends of a few KB per stroke with no transaction overhead. Full SQLite-wasm is available but overkill for this data model unless the app later needs queries.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: tldraw persists one record per shape in an IDB 'records' store, applies each change batch as one readwrite transaction (put/delete per record), throttles persistence to 350 ms, writes a full snapshot only on schema mismatch/error, and its .tldr file is `{ tldrawFileFormatVersion, schema, records[] }` with per-record migrations. - TLLocalSyncClient.ts: `PERSIST_THROTTLE_MS = 350`, `PERSIST_RETRY_THROTTLE_MS = 10_000`; `if (this.shouldDoFullDBWrite) { … await this.db.storeSnapshot(...) } else { const diffs = squashRecordDiffs(...); await this.db.storeChanges(...) }`; BroadcastChannel `postMessage({ type: 'diff', … })` for cross-tab. LocalIndexedDb.ts: DB `'TLDRAW_DOCUMENT_v2' + persistenceKey`, stores `records`, `schema`, `session_state`, `assets` via the idb library; `storeChanges` loops `recordsStore.put(record, id)` / `recordsStore.delete(id)` inside one transaction; `load()` does `recordsStore.getAll()`. file.ts: extension `.tldr`, MIME `application/vnd.tldraw+json`, `parseTldrawJsonFile` validates shape, checks version, migrates records via the schema; `serializeTldrawJson` prunes unused assets and inlines remote assets as data URLs. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/utils/sync/TLLocalSyncClient.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/editor/src/lib/utils/sync/LocalIndexedDb.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tldraw/tldraw/main/packages/tldraw/src/lib/utils/tldr/file.ts ; https://tldraw.dev/docs/persistence) => This is the closest analogue to Notes Gallery and validates the target architecture: record-per-shape IDB, squashed diffs → one transaction every ~350 ms, snapshot-to-file only for export. Notes Gallery's `.notesgallery.json` `{version, workspace, blocks[], edges[], files[]}` is already this shape; keep it.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Excalidraw saves elements+appState as JSON to localStorage with a 300 ms debounce, stores binary files individually in IndexedDB via idb-keyval, pauses saving when hidden/locked, flushes on demand, and its .excalidraw file is `{type, version, source, elements, appState, files: {id: {mimeType, dataURL,…}}}`. - app_constants.ts: `SAVE_TO_LOCAL_STORAGE_TIMEOUT = 300`, `FILE_UPLOAD_TIMEOUT = 300`, keys `excalidraw`, `excalidraw-state`, `version-dataState`, `version-files`. LocalData.ts: `_save = debounce(...)`, `pauseSave/resumeSave/flushSave` (`this._save.flush()`), skipped when `document.hidden || this.locker.isLocked()`; `LocalFileManager` uses `getMany/setMany/set(id, fileData, filesStore)` on store 'files-db'/'files-store' and `clearObsoleteFiles` evicts images unused for 24 h. JSON schema docs: files embedded as `"files": { [fileId]: { "mimeType": "image/png", "dataURL": "data:image/png;base64,..." } }`. (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/excalidraw-app/data/LocalData.ts ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/excalidraw/excalidraw/master/excalidraw-app/app_constants.ts ; https://docs.excalidraw.com/docs/codebase/json-schema) => Same split as Notes Gallery (attachments as Blobs in IDB, embedded as data URLs only in the export file). Their flush-on-hide/pause-during-gesture lifecycle is what 2.0's autosave scheduler should copy; their whole-scene stringify to localStorage is the pattern to avoid at handwriting scale.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: Obsidian stores notes as plain Markdown files in a folder and uses IndexedDB only for a rebuildable metadata cache; Logseq's DB version runs SQLite-wasm inside a dedicated worker on OPFS via `installOpfsSAHPoolVfs` (initialCapacity 20) and talks to the UI over Comlink. - Obsidian: "Obsidian stores your notes as Markdown-formatted plain text files in a vault"; ".obsidian configuration folder"; uses IndexedDB, "a low-level, client-side database", for a "metadata cache" that "can be rebuilt". Logseq browser.cljs: `(.installOpfsSAHPoolVfs ^js sqlite #js {:name pool-name :initialCapacity 20})`, `(new (.-OpfsSAHPoolDb ^js pool) path)`, per-graph directories prefixed `.logseq-pool-`; db_worker.cljs exposes the worker via `(Comlink/expose proxy-object)`. (https://obsidian.md/help/data-storage ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/logseq/logseq/master/src/main/frontend/worker/platform/browser.cljs ; https://raw.githubusercontent.com/logseq/logseq/master/src/main/frontend/worker/db_worker.cljs) => Two more data points for 'user-visible files are the source of truth; the fast local store is a cache/worker-owned DB'. Logseq shows a worker-owned OPFS store is production-viable in browsers today.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: JSON Lines is 'UTF-8, one valid JSON value per line, \n-terminated, .jsonl' and is designed for appending/streaming; JSON Canvas (Obsidian) is a single JSON file with `nodes`/`edges` arrays. - jsonlines.org: "Each Line is a Valid JSON Value", "Line Terminator is '\n'" (\r\n tolerated), extension `.jsonl`, MIME `application/jsonl`; suited to "processing one record at a time" and "generating and concatenating JSON Lines files". jsoncanvas.org 1.0: "The top level of JSON Canvas contains two arrays: nodes … edges"; node types text/file/link/group; required `id, type, x, y, width, height`. (https://jsonlines.org/ ; https://jsoncanvas.org/spec/1.0/) => JSONL is the right shape for the append-only autosave journal (one `{op, rec}` per line); the user-visible `.notesgallery.json` stays a single JSON document (same family as .tldr/.excalidraw/.canvas), so import/export compatibility is untouched.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: V8's JSON.stringify got >2x faster in V8 13.8 / Chrome 138 (Aug 2025) via a side-effect-free fast path that requires no replacer/space, no toJSON, no indexed properties; JSON.parse is ~1.7x faster than an equivalent JS literal; V8's maximum string length is 2^30 − 25 characters on 64-bit. - v8.dev/blog/json-stringify (2025-08-04): "more than 2x speedup" on JetStream2 json-stringify-inspector, available in V8 13.8 (Chrome 138); fast path conditions: no `replacer` or `space`, plain objects without `.toJSON()`, no indexed properties, simple string types; Dragonbox for number formatting; segmented output buffer. Cost of JS 2019: JSON.parse is "1.7× as fast" than an object literal, "apply this technique for objects of 10 kB or larger". V8 6.2: "The maximum string length on 64-bit platforms increased from 2**28 - 16 to 2**30 - 25 characters." (https://v8.dev/blog/json-stringify ; https://v8.dev/blog/cost-of-javascript-2019 ; https://v8.dev/blog/v8-release-62) => Stringify is cheaper than it was but still O(document) and on whichever thread calls it. LOW-CONFIDENCE detail: `JSON.stringify(payload, null, 0)` (app.js 6838) passes a `space` argument; whether `0` disqualifies the fast path is not stated — call `JSON.stringify(payload)` to be safe. The ~1 GiB string cap plus base64 attachments means a single-string export cannot scale indefinitely; assemble the file from chunks.
- [high] ESTABLISHED: in Chromium a Blob is a handle — the bytes live in the browser process (paged to disk under pressure) and passing a Blob between contexts or into IDB does not copy the data. - storage/browser/blob/README.md: "after blob creation the actual blob 'data' gets transported to and lives in the browser process. The renderer just holds a reference"; when building dependent blobs "we just share the item between them"; in-memory limit 2 GB on x64 (scaled down on mobile), disk 10–50% of disk; blobs are destroyed when the last handle goes away. (https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/storage/browser/blob/README.md) => Keep attachments as Blobs in the `files` store (already the case) and keep them OUT of the per-save path: the current save re-reads every Blob into a base64 string (1.33x size) and inlines it. For the export/mirror file, build `new Blob([headerChunk, ...cachedRecordStrings, attachmentChunks])` — on the web `writable.write(blob)` streams it without a giant string; in Tauri, stream the same parts through `FileHandle.write()`.

Recommendations:
- Keep the on-disk/export format exactly as `.notesgallery.json` v2 (`{app, kind, version, exportedAt, workspace, blocks[], edges[], files[]}`); it is already the same single-file shape as .tldr/.excalidraw/JSON Canvas. Reserve `version: 3` only if fields are added, and keep the importer accepting v2.
- Introduce a dedicated storage worker that owns IndexedDB (IDB is available in workers; structured-clone and stringify then run off the input thread). The main thread keeps the in-memory model and posts small deltas `{added, updated, removed}` per frame or on stroke commit (a stroke is ~2.5–5 KB, far under Surma's 10 KiB animation-safe budget). The UI never awaits storage; undo/redo apply deltas in memory and post the inverse.
- Write each delta batch as ONE readwrite transaction covering blocks/edges/files (put + delete in a loop, resolve on `tx.oncomplete`, never `await` anything non-IDB inside). Expect ~25x fewer commits than today (RxDB: 1k docs 80 ms in one tx vs ~2 s one-tx-per-doc). Leave durability at default (already relaxed since Chrome 121). Keep records < 64 KiB (kIDBWrapThreshold) — never store a page or workspace as one record.
- Fix level loading: add a compound index `[ws, parentId]` (or store `level = parentId==ROOT ? 'root:'+ws : parentId` and index it) so the home canvas reads only its own records; maintain a `levelStats` record (child/file counts, first 4 titles) updated by the worker on each delta instead of 2 transactions per visible block; read with `getAll`/`getAllRecords` in ~2k-record batches (Chrome 141+, feature-detect).
- Make autosave O(delta): the worker keeps `Map<id, string>` of each record's serialised JSON (re-stringify only records in the delta) and a cached base64 string per attachment; the full file is assembled by concatenating cached chunks. Cost per full save becomes a memcpy of the file plus the write, not a stringify of everything. Build it as chunks (array of strings/Uint8Arrays) — `new Blob(parts)` on the web, `FileHandle.write()` per chunk on Tauri — so no multi-MB string ever exists on the main thread and the V8 ~1 GiB string cap is irrelevant.
- Two-tier persistence per workspace: (1) an append-only JSONL journal — one line per delta `{t, op, store, rec|id}` — written immediately (within ~350 ms, tldraw-style) to app-private storage: OPFS via `createSyncAccessHandle` in the worker on the web (WebView 114+) or `appDataDir/<ws>.journal.jsonl` via `FileHandle.write` in Tauri; (2) the user's `.notesgallery.json` rewritten from cached chunks only at idle (no gesture for ≥2 s), on `visibilitychange`/`pagehide`/blur, on workspace switch/close, and at a 10–30 s cap — never inside a gesture (keep today's busy-guard). On open, if the journal is newer than the file, replay it into IDB; truncate the journal after a successful full write.
- Because Tauri `invoke` is unavailable in workers, the worker transfers the finished Uint8Array(s) to the main thread (zero-copy transferable) and the main thread calls `writeFile`/`FileHandle.write` with the raw body — the main-thread share is microseconds; the IPC itself (~200 ms per 10 MB on Windows per a maintainer's informal benchmark; measure on Android) happens off the JS thread. Verify the plugin-fs JS and Rust crate versions match.
- Android crash-safety: the content:// document opened with mode "wt" is truncated at open and cannot be atomically replaced. Always write the full snapshot to `appDataDir/<ws>.notesgallery.json.tmp` first, `rename` it over `appDataDir/<ws>.notesgallery.json` (add `fs:allow-rename`/`fs:allow-copy-file` to capabilities), then mirror the same bytes to the user's content:// URI; on open prefer whichever of (user file, app copy + journal) is newest/valid. Verify written length after a content:// write since some SAF providers ignore 't'.
- Web build: keep the FSA handle flow, but do the full write from the worker if the handle clones into it (FileSystemFileHandle is structured-cloneable; verify in Chrome/Edge), otherwise via main thread with a Blob. Each FSA write is inherently a full swap-file write + atomic replace, so the journal (OPFS) carries the high-frequency saves. Call `navigator.storage.persist()` once; IDB is best-effort and evicted wholesale under storage pressure.
- Import/export: parse in the worker (`JSON.parse` is fast; run it off-thread anyway for 50+ MB files), convert data URLs to Blobs with `fetch(dataUrl)` in the worker, and bulk-put with one transaction per store (or chunks of ~2–5k records) instead of one awaited transaction per block. Export streams the cached chunks — the same code path as the idle full save.
- Schema/versioning: bump `DB_VERSION` and do the migration inside `onupgradeneeded` using cursors in the same versionchange transaction as today (this is the one place `durability: 'strict'` is reasonable); when a new `[ws,parentId]` index is created, build it in that transaction. Consider a new DB name for 2.0 (with a one-time import from 'blocknotes') so a current WebView/Chrome ≥150 creates it on the SQLite IDB backend.
- Instrument before/after: `performance.mark/measure` around delta writes, full saves, level loads and import; Long Tasks/`event-timing` observer during inking; target zero storage-attributable main-thread tasks > 16 ms while `inking` is true, and full-save durations that scale with delta size not workspace size.
Open questions:
- What are the real sizes on the user's tablet — stroke count per page, number of levels, total attachment MB and the resulting `.notesgallery.json` size? Everything above is scaled from a 2.5–5 KB/stroke estimate; a quick `navigator.storage.estimate()` plus the file size would calibrate the save budget.
- Which Android System WebView version is on the Samsung tablet (chrome://version inside the app)? ≥150 means new IDB databases use the SQLite backend and `getAllRecords()` is available; older WebViews stay on LevelDB.
- Does `FileSystemFileHandle` clone into a Worker and does `createWritable()` work there in the user's Chrome/Edge build? If yes the web full-save can run entirely in the worker; if not, the main thread must call `createWritable` with the assembled Blob.
- Does Tauri's Android `webview.fs().open()` for a content:// URI support `seek`/`ftruncate` (needed only if the journal were to live on the user-visible document; the recommendation avoids this by journaling to appDataDir).
- Does the SAF provider behind the user's chosen save location (Samsung My Files / Drive / SD card) honour the 't' in "wt"? Android documents provider-dependent truncation; a length check after write is proposed as insurance.
- Actual Android IPC throughput for a 10–50 MB raw-body `writeFile` (only macOS ≈5 ms and Windows ≈200 ms per 10 MB are reported, informally) — measure on the tablet before fixing the full-rewrite cadence.
- Does passing `space = 0` to `JSON.stringify` (current web save path) fall off V8's 13.8 fast path? Unverified; trivially avoided by calling `JSON.stringify(payload)`.
- Multi-tab/multi-window: with the worker owning IDB, two tabs (web) or a second window (desktop) need coordination (tldraw uses BroadcastChannel; RxDB measured ~150 ms for leader election). Is this a supported scenario for Notes Gallery 2.0?

## inventory
# Notes Gallery 1.5.3 (web build v113) — complete feature & behaviour inventory for the 2.0 rebuild

Sources read in full: `Interface/js/app.js` (8109 lines, one IIFE), `js/db.js`, `js/platform.js`, `js/pdf.js`, `sw.js`, `index.html`, `css/styles.css`, `manifest.webmanifest`, `Software/app/src-tauri/{tauri.conf.json,Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs,src/ink.rs,capabilities/default.json}`, `Software/app/build-{windows,android}.ps1`, the scratchpad regression suite (`suite.sh`, `*test.ps1`, `perfbench.ps1`, `suite-final.txt`, `perfbench-final.txt`). Line numbers below are `app.js` unless prefixed. **[E]** = established by reading code/tests; **[I]** = inferred.

---

## 1. Runtime, shell and packaging

| Item | Where | Behaviour to preserve |
|---|---|---|
| Script load order | index.html:805-808 | `platform.js` → `pdf.js` → `db.js` → `app.js`, all `?v=113`; no modules (runs from `file://`). |
| Cache-bust rule | index.html:12,805-808; sw.js:4 | `?v=N` on css/js must equal `CACHE='notes-gallery-vN'` in sw.js. |
| Service worker | sw.js; `registerSW()` 8076-8081 | Registered only in secure contexts; network-first for same-origin GET, cache fallback, `./index.html` shell for navigations; `skipWaiting`+`clients.claim`; old caches deleted on activate. **App builds strip `registerSW();`** (build-windows.ps1 / build-android.ps1 step 1). |
| PWA manifest | manifest.webmanifest | `display: fullscreen`, `display_override [fullscreen, standalone]`, theme/background `#0a0b0d`, single SVG maskable icon. |
| Fatal banner | 10-21 | `window.error`/`unhandledrejection` → fixed red `#fatal-banner` "please screenshot this". Tests assert `fatal=false` via this element. |
| Full screen | `toggleFullscreen()` 2139-2147 | `requestFullscreen({navigationUI:'hide'})`, F11, ⋯ menu + cmdk; `#fs-state` label. |
| Theme | `setTheme/initTheme` 7498-7508 | `data-theme` on `<html>`; localStorage `bn-theme`; default from `prefers-color-scheme` (dark unless light). Two palettes in styles.css:16-59. |
| NGShell (Tauri bridge) | platform.js | Defined only when `window.__TAURI__.core` exists: `isApp:true`, `saveDialog(name)` (filters json / pdf by name), `openDialog()` (single .json), `writeFile(path, string|Uint8Array|ArrayBuffer)` (binary → `fs.writeFile`, text → `writeTextFile`), `readFile(path)` (text), `reveal(path)` (opener revealItemInDir), `recognizeInk(strokes)` → `invoke('recognize_ink')` or `null`, `basename()`. |
| Tauri commands/plugins | lib.rs, Cargo.toml, capabilities/default.json | Plugins dialog/fs/opener; one command `recognize_ink(Vec<Vec<(f64,f64)>>) -> Option<String>`; fs scope `**`; window 1280×800 min 360×480, `withGlobalTauri`, `zoomHotkeysEnabled`, CSP null; NSIS currentUser installer. Version 1.5.3 in both tauri.conf.json and Cargo.toml (2.0 starts at 2.0.0). |
| Windows Ink recogniser | ink.rs | Windows only: strokes → `InkStrokeBuilder` (pressure 0.5) → `InkAnalyzer`; returns lines joined by `\n`, else words joined by space; `None` on non-Windows/error. The app hides "Convert to text" when `inkRecognizerKind()` (3707-3711) is null; Chromium `navigator.createHandwritingRecognizer` used where present. |

APK arm64, signed with `android-keys/ng-release.keystore`. |
| Home-screen file note | `fsStatus()` 6820-6826 | SHELL → "saved as files on this computer"; web needs `showSaveFilePicker` + secure context + not `file:`. |

## 2. Data model

### 2.1 IndexedDB `blocknotes` v3 (db.js)
- **workspaces** keyPath `id` — `{id, name, color, paper?('dots'|'grid'|'lines'|'blank'), createdAt, updatedAt}`.
- **blocks** keyPath `id`; indexes `parentId`, `ws` — common fields `{id, ws, parentId ('__root__'=DB.ROOT or block id), kind?, title, description, notes, tags, layout('canvas'|'list'), color, icon, x, y, z?, group?, locked?, createdAt, updatedAt}` + per-kind fields (§6).
- **edges** keyPath `id`; indexes `parentId`, `ws` — `{id, ws, parentId, from, to, createdAt, label?, style?('curve'|'straight'|'elbow'), both?}`.
- **files** keyPath `id`; indexes `blockId`, `ws` — `{id, ws, blockId, name, type, size, kind('image'|'pdf'|'other'), blob: Blob, createdAt}`.
- **meta** keyPath `key` — `{key, value}`; **unused by app.js** (no getMeta/setMeta callers).
- **handles** keyPath `ws` — `{ws, handle: FileSystemFileHandle}` (web) or `{ws, path: string}` (app shell).
- Migrations: v1→v2 creates `workspaces`, adds `ws` indexes and moves orphan records into `ws-default` "My Workspace" (#2b7fff); v2→v3 adds `handles`.
- Access pattern: root level = `allByWs` filtered `parentId===ROOT`; deeper = `parentId` index. `childBlocks` order = index order = **UUID order, not createdAt** (renderBlocks appends in that order → DOM stacking for z=0 blocks is UUID order) [E].
- `deleteBlockDeep` recurses children, deletes files, then scans **all** edges for touching ones. `buildPath` walks parentId up to Home.

### 2.2 localStorage keys (all wrapped in try/catch)
`bn-theme`, `ng-loc` (JSON `{ws, level}`, saveLoc 7061), `ng-autosave` ('1'/'0', default on), `ng-pen-color`, `ng-pen-style`, `ng-pen-size` (1-100), `ng-pen-width` (**dead: read at 218, never used**), `ng-finger-draw` ('on'|'off', default off), `ng-minimap` ('1'/'0' default on), `ng-snap`, `ng-shape-snap`, `ng-eraser-mode` (normal|stroke|lasso), `ng-eraser-size` (1-100, default 24), `ng-pen-bar-pos` (JSON `{anchor, vert}`), `ng-axis-lock` ('x'|'y' or removed).

### 2.3 Export file format `*.notesgallery.json` (version 2) — `workspacePayload()` 6145-6169
```
{ app: 'NotesGallery', kind: 'workspace', version: 2, exportedAt: ISO string,
  workspace: { name, color },                       // paper NOT exported
  blocks: [ { ...every block field except `ws` } ], // kind, pts, src (data URL), rows, group, z, locked... all kept
  edges:  [ { id, parentId, from, to, createdAt } ],// label/style/both are DROPPED
  files:  [ { blockId, name, type, size, kind, createdAt, data: dataURL } ] }
```
Import (`validWorkspaceData` 6871: `app in ('NotesGallery','BlockNotes') && Array.isArray(blocks)`, no version check; `createWorkspaceFromData` 6843-6870): new workspace id, name default "Imported workspace", colour from file or palette; every block gets a fresh id via `idMap`; parentId remapped (unknown → ROOT); edges remapped (drop if an end is missing); files `data` → Blob via `fetch(dataURL)`. Filename `safeFileName(name)+'.notesgallery.json'` (6170). Data URL cache for attachments up to 32 MB (`FILE_CACHE_BUDGET`).

### 2.4 File linking & autosave (6816-7055)
- `SHELL = !!window.NGShell`; `FS_OK` = SHELL or FSA API in a secure non-file context.
- New workspace → native save dialog (shell) / `showSaveFilePicker` (web) → handle/path stored in `handles`; initial write. Import via picker binds the file. Properties/About offer "Choose a file location…" (`relinkWorkspace` shell, `linkWorkspaceFile` web).
- `markChanged()` (7030): if autosave → 900 ms debounce with a 10 s hard deadline; `autosaveTick` waits while `inking|erasing|dragging|gizmo|selScale|lasso|panning|pinch`; else sets `dirty`. Ctrl+S = manual save. `visibilitychange`→hidden flushes a pending autosave; `beforeunload` warns only when `!autosave && dirty`.
- Save writes the **whole** `workspacePayload` JSON (single writer, queued `saveNext`, newest wins). Without a linked file the save is a no-op that clears `dirty` (manual save toasts a hint). Web handles need `queryPermission/requestPermission` (a gesture) — autosave silently flags red until Ctrl+S.
- Status pill `#save-status`: grey = autosave off, blue = on, red = unsaved/blocked. `reportSaveFailure` confirm dialog offers relinking; nags once for autosave.
- IndexedDB is always the live store; every edit persists to IDB immediately (`persistBlock`, debounced editors).

## 3. Home screen (workspaces) — 7057-7408
Hero brand + typed tagline (`HERO_TAG`, 24 ms/char, 180 ms after '.', 320 ms after newline). Cards sorted by `updatedAt` desc with block count; hover actions Properties / Export / Delete (always visible on `hover:none`); `#ws-add` card; header buttons Import (`importViaPicker`) and New workspace. `newWorkspaceFlow` prompt: name, colour swatches (default `PALETTE[count % 23]`), template Blank/Mind map/Kanban/Project (`seedTemplate` 7228-7258: kanban 3 lists at x 40/300/560; mindmap centre + 4 spokes with edges; project Goals/Tasks(list)/Resources/Notes with seeded markdown). Delete = confirm, `deleteWorkspaceDeep`. `restoreOrHome` reopens `ng-loc` (falls back to root if level gone). Brand button → `goHome` (drops gestures, awaits ink writes, clears history, clears caches). Brand **hover** (mouse only) → `#brand-menu` switcher listing workspaces + New + All. `#fs-note` status line. `home-mode` class hides workspace chrome (styles.css:1083-1105).

## 4. Levels, navigation, list layout
- `loadLevel` (487-528): drops live gestures if the level changes, resets `lastInk`, loads blocks/edges/path, child counts + first-4 child peek per card, toggles list mode, resets tag filter, renders breadcrumbs/outline, `fit` option, `saveLoc`.
- History: `goToLevel(push)`, `navigateTo` (drops tools, closes drawer, pushes), `navBack/navForward`, `updateNavButtons` (Home disabled at root). Breadcrumbs (bottom bar, `pointer-events:none` except crumbs): root crumb = workspace name, `›` separators, current bold.
- List layout (`b.layout==='list'`): `#list-view` rows sorted by `createdAt`, per-kind rows (`listRowHtml` 871-921), header with count, `#list-add` opens Add menu; click selects, dblclick opens (text → text editor); canvas-only tools hidden (`#app.list-mode`), pen/link/eraser refused in list mode.
- Cards: monogram tile, title, description or notes-peek (markdown, first 240 chars), tag chips (click → tag filter, dims non-matching `.dim`), meta chips (child count, files, todo `☑ d/t`, pencil), list cards show 4-item peek + "+N more". Hover actions Edit/Open shown only when selected.

## 5. Viewport
- `state.view = {scale:1, tx:60, ty:40}`; `applyView` (442-461) sets `#world` transform, `--inv = 1/scale` (deferred during pinch, `flushInv`), paper `background-size` (26·scale, lines 30·scale) and position, sel bar/frame, zoom label, minimap rAF; redraws the live stroke if inking.
- Zoom: `zoomAt` clamps **0.02–64**; wheel ×1.12; ctrl-wheel `clamp(exp(-dy·0.012), .6, 1.7)`; buttons ×1.18; Ctrl +/−/0 ×1.15 / reset; Safari gesture events; pinch = two pointers (`pinch.dist`). `fitToView` pad 70, clamp 0.02–8, empty level → `{1, w/2-105, h/2-120}`. `resetZoom` keeps the centre.
- Pan: left-drag on empty canvas / unselected ink; axis lock `x`/`y` (⋯ menu, ctx menu, cmdk; persisted); `.panning` cursor; tap (<4 px) clears selection or picks tapped ink word.
- Paper: `applyPaper` sets `#stage[data-paper]`; dots (default) / grid / lines / blank (styles.css:337-348); chosen in Workspace properties with live preview.
- Mini-map (5834-6022): 190×130 (150×104 ≤860 px, 130×90 ≤560 px, hidden ≤480 px tall or presenting), shown when on && canvas && blocks>0, dpr-scaled canvas, faithful per-kind previews (`drawNodeMini`, ink polyline cached per `pts` array in a WeakMap, images cached), viewport rectangle, click/drag pans. Toggle M / ⋯ / ctx / cmdk. Pen bar dodges it.

## 6. Block kinds — fields, defaults, renderer, editor, handles
All kinds share `.block` absolute at `left/top = x/y`, `zIndex = z || (ink ? 500 : none)`, `.locked` badge, `.selected` glow in own accent (styles.css:612-615), `.dim` for tag filter.

| kind | Creation defaults | Renderer | Editor (drawer) & handles |
|---|---|---|---|
| **card / list** (no `kind`) | `createBlock` 1098: title 'New block'/'New list', colour `PALETTE[(n·7)%23]`, centred (210×~60), opens editor with title focused | `paintBlock` 641 | `#drawer`: title, Opens-as Canvas/List seg, 23 accent swatches, description (+ "Add description as text inside"), notes markdown (+ "Add notes as text inside" → child text node 60,60 size 16/22, converts list→canvas), tags, attachments (upload/drop, open/download/remove, thumbs), Delete, Open inside. Debounce 350 ms, one undo entry per session. Width 220 CSS. |
| **text** | `kind:'text', text:'Text', font:'sans', size:22, bold/italic false, align 'left', orient 'h', rot 0, glow false, glowColor ''`, no colour (theme text) | `paintTextNode` 693-736: box hugs content (`fit-content`, `white-space:pre`), `b.w` = wrap width (`pre-wrap`, `max-width`), justify → fixed width `b.w||320` `pre-line`; vertical `writing-mode: vertical-rl`; `FONT_STACK` sans/serif/mono | `#text-drawer`: textarea, font seg, size 4–400 (typed values unclamped), B/I, align 4, orientation, rotation −180..180, colour (Default + palette), glow toggle + colour; Reset (whole session) and per-param reset; Done. Handles: rotate (top), `e` edge = wrap width, corner = font size (`size = start + d·0.7`, clamp 4–4000, scales `b.w` proportionally). Imported text (`createTextFromFile`, 20 000-char cap, mono 14, `nowrap:true`). |

Handles rotate + corner resize (w,h clamp 12–200000). |

Handles rotate + corner (aspect locked, min 20). Sources: Add ▸ Import ▸ Images, drop, clipboard paste (`bindImagePaste`, at cursor if over stage, +24 px per extra image). |
| **ink** | `finalizeInk` 5715 (see §7) | `paintInkNode` 753-772: `<div><svg class=ink-svg><path>`; size `(w||1)+2·pad` with `pad = width+2`; path via `inkStrokeD`; `applyInkStyle` sets fill/stroke/width/cap/join/dasharray/opacity; svg reused in place when repainting | `#ink-drawer` (`showInkPanel` 3122): title "Ink drawing" / "Handwriting — N strokes", Convert to text (hidden without recogniser), style picker (PEN_ORDER + hidden marker only if current), colour (palette + white/black), width 1–24; edits apply to every selected stroke (`applyInk`, rAF-batched repaint, 300 ms save), one undo entry for the panel session (`flushInkEdits`); Reset restores all; Delete deletes selection. No edit pencil on ink; dblclick opens the panel. |

Handles: `e`/`s` wrap edges, corner scales font (`+d·0.12`) and proportionally w/h/colW/rowH; column/row grips (`.col-resize/.row-resize`, min 24/16). Right-click / long-press a cell opens the panel focused on it.
